His mind as usual was focussed on the perceived threats, which had heightened after the recent bugging incident. Carl’s thoughts never strayed much from his job, she had caught him distracted the occasional time when she was in the company of another beautiful woman, but his thoughts never went that way with her. She was glad that Carl could separate business and pleasure so well, but to be honest, also a little disappointed that he did not indulge the occasional thought about her.

A flight of course would have taken under an hour, but flying was still one of her biggest phobias even though she was often forced by work to take dozens a year. Another reason was that the heightened anxiety was usually enough to wake her inner voice, though she had now gotten used to its occasional Delphic, and very ignorable chatter.

Carl drove the hire car from the train station up to her friend Gabrielle’s house in the mountains, then returned to his hotel three kilometres distant after checking the area. He resigned himself to the fact that Charlie was two people, the ultra-professional negotiator, and the young woman who wanted to pretend she was normal and that none of that other life existed. It made his and Simon's life difficult but Charlie accepted compromises as did Carl.

* * *

Charlie kissed both Gabrielle’s cheeks then brought her in for a proper hug.

“It’s been ages Charlie!” Said Gabrielle. “And you are never on social media or allow us to post anything about you, so nobody has a clue where you’ve been or what you’ve been up to.”

“And what would we talk about if you already knew everything I’ve done!” Charlie smiled back cheekily. “A moment shared between good friends is intimate, you will remember tonight the next time we meet, surely that is better than a few likes.”

The night was wonderful, they talked about school, their last reunion and of course all Gabrielle's love interests. As a successful writer of romance novels Gabrielle needed to keep in practice.

“Oh a bug in your office, it sounds very exciting, and a handsome English scientist, this all sounds like a good plot for my next novel.” She said predictably.

“Oh Gabrielle, please not another. And don't try and kid me I wasn’t your first heroine! Remember Charlene?”

“I promise Charlie,” she said truthfully, “I did not know that was your real name, and my heroine was nothing like you, super-model by day, secret agent by night, and let’s not forget her forty inch heaving bosom!”

“Only in your world Gabrielle, could that ever happen.” Charlie laughed.

“Well, my world plus a hundred thousand readers you forget,” she replied laughing. “So come on spill some more, what was the jazz festival like with Bethany? I would really have loved to come, but you always seemed to do things like this so last minute. Are you sure you’re not a real secret agent or something?”

“Or something,” replied Charlie drolly.

“And this man, tell me more.”

“Oh just some young, green English scientist, he was cute, but much too nice for the likes of you or me, any one of your characters would have chewed him up and spit him out for breakfast. And while on the subject, let me guess the new book’s plot: A strong independent woman, goes on a dangerous adventure to an exotic location and seduces any man she wants along the way to solve the mystery.

“Close, you missed the part of how her powerful thrusting hips will send both him and the heroine to heaven at the close of every chapter. Are you sure you didn't get an advance copy?”

“I hope all your readers don’t think it’s that easy? Why doesn't your heroine find a good man who she can, you know, just be good friends with, and they can have a laugh and a joke?”

“Pish!” She said, topping up their wine glasses, “my plots are much more believable. Show me a man like that and I will marry him tomorrow!”

Both women laughed.

“Are you sure you don't want to stay a couple of nights Charlie, most of the girls hardly ever come here these days and I cannot talk to men the way I talk to you guys. I assume you will be going to Lianne’s engagement party in October?”

“I wouldn’t miss it, and if you want a plot for your next novel, ask her how she really met Dominic and how he really got that scar.”

Both women giggled into the night. Charlie tried not to read her friends' minds and had gotten dampening down her gift to an art-form, but plenty always leaked through especially when you were next to each other.

She did have one hard and strict rule though, another one of those that her mother told her, and if she went anywhere she expected to be alone she would open her mind to ensure nobody was around or listening in. It had saved her mothers life on more than one occasion and Charlie did have a greater range by far than Gerty. All she caught was Carl for a few brief moments early on, then nothing.

The two women had breakfast in Feldkirch, five kilometres over the border in Germany, and after breakfast Charlie and Carl began the slow drive to Munich for her meeting with the chief of police. No record of them entering or leaving Liechtenstein was ever made.

* * *

She had no agenda with the chief of police and opened with a compliment of how efficient his department was. Moving to the planned interviews, she smiled and nodded to the few small conditions he requested, including giving their interviewer any insights she may get.

“The American agent Tanner was released, but as he has dual German citizenship, and the matter has not yet been resolved, he has been ordered not to leave the city. We have timed all three interviews so you can be present. Please remember though, you will be behind the one way mirror and will need to give your questions to the interviewer. Inspector Von-Gruden will perform the interviews as you seemed to have formed a cordial relationship from last time.”

Not being able to ask questions was far from perfect but she could work around it. Charlie and her mother usually found it very easy to get any answer, by simply proposing a question as open or specific as necessary. People always thought about the answers they gave; In the case of a lie, they had to remember whatever story they had fabricated, and usually remembered a lot more of the details of what actually happened when they did, allowing her questions to get more specific. There was no real defence against it, as long as she got close to the topic, she could move any topic to the front of their mind.

Charlie added two questions to the interviewers list, and waited patiently as the suspects lied their way through their interrogations.

The interviewer finally got to her questions, questions about their interview with the young lady, and if they knew who she was. The interrogator was briefed not to interfere if they did not answer. He was well aware that the woman behind the mirror was the one who had uncovered the crimes, and who was simply here at the moment to analyse the body and facial movements. Von-Gruden was a professional and stuck to the script, leaving uncomfortable silences from the three men and giving them time to recollect other memories.

The chief accountant Schultz knew nothing, he really was just greedy, sad and gullible. Mendez followed the same smug pattern as when she first interviewed him, though his thoughts towards whoever the woman was, were less than friendly. Charlie did however catch a brief thought on some organisation called The ElderNacht, a Matheus Franke their top dog in Europe, and some serious worry that they might kill him, or even the auditor. That was interesting!

The American agent Tanner, was different still, as he sat there thinking of the lie as his inner mind went through his debriefing process with another man a few days earlier. This other man was someone from CIA delta operations, whatever that was, and again his thoughts drifted on whether they knew who this negotiator woman was.

She waited until the end of the interview, and thanked the interviewer when he came in. She told him that the facial expressions and body language told her they had lied about three things, and gave him a pointer to the next line of questioning he should ask. The interviewer took what Charlie had said very seriously. He thought he could read people well but this woman was truly next level.

She thanked the Chief of Police one more time, then Carl drove them to the airport to drop off the hire car, and for their flight to the USA.

The Interpol agent assigned by the CIA Director, Ethan Curran landed at Munich airport an hour after Charlie and Carl flew out.

* * *

The visit to the USA by Charlie was purely coincidental. Being up to date in almost everything was essential to a negotiator's work. She and her mother often paid big fees to various world experts for one-on-one training in their specialist fields. The visit would be to do a small two day job for the Swiss embassy's California office, then four days of training at UC Berkeley with Professor Montgomery, and the rest was visiting friends.

As the plane hit some turbulence Charlie gripped the armrest for dear life. She realised she still did not know if it was the CIA, this organisation called The ElderNacht, or even another party who had bugged their offices but she was now leaning towards The ElderNacht.

[...] [...] [...]

Charlie sighed, eyes tightly shut. [Go back to sleep dragon]

Her inner voice was just another distraction, another one of the hundred inputs, like sounds, vision, pain, other people's minds, all clamouring for her attention, and any one of which could be overridden by her body feeling a little cold, hungry wanting the toilet, even an itch that needed scratching.

Charlie and Gerty had become much more expert on consciousness over the years, having insights into it that others did not. To Charlie it was much more than a little ticker-tape narrative at the front of the mind, the one running from the day you were born to the day you died and broken every day by deep sleep.

She saw the comparisons people made to computers were both valid, and shallow simultaneously, whereas a computer processed inputs logically and to a specific conclusion, the mind rarely finished anything before being pulled off in another direction as another input condition overrode it. In a computer this would lead quickly to a stack-overflow condition, where memory would run out, no more return addresses could be stored and the computer would crash.

She assumed that it was the function of sleep, to fix this imbalance, but she also knew many autistic people had a much more severe reaction, the crash ending up as a withdrawal from life until they reset.

Charlie really wished her grandfather was still alive to talk about this, as she was never going to forward her insights to others. Rule one was to try to keep below the radar, and act only when there is a credible alternative explanation.

She knew some animals had the narrative, and Charlie would love one day to use her gift on a dolphin, but when she got into a bathing costume it was always to lie on a beach never to swim. Swimming was another thing she hated, she couldn't be good at everything.

She was however looking forward to her little one-on-one boot camp with Professor Montgomery, he was a genius in the embryonic world of AI. Young, quite good looking, a little flirtatious but also understood she was no normal corporate pushover. He also knew that the thirty thousand dollars she would pay him for these few days of his time benefited his research as well as her.

* * *

The overweight man was sitting at his multi-screened computer setup, eating nachos and wondering where the negotiator woman had gone. The bugs and surveillance they had put on the negotiators was basically proving useless, and he personally thought it a waste of time. He wondered what the end game was, and why The ElderNacht wanted to know about these auditors.

Leader Matheus was happy to use bottom-feeders like Mendez, then dispose of them once their usefulness had finished, but The ElderNacht never usually went after the legal authorities as it tended to end badly. He guessed the orders came from higher. Matheus was second-tier to his father, the group leader, and the man who ran the organisation from the island in the Caribbean. He had overheard rumours The ElderNacht was the remnant of some Nazi’s who escaped during the war, but he knew that was probably bullshit, Nazi’s would never help Islamic terrorists.

No, The ElderNacht might have harboured a few Nazis at one time, but it now had some deeper purpose, one that only its inner circle knew about. The criminal part of the organisation was just the tip of the iceberg, a means to an end. There were the drugs of course, but its fingers were in every unethical pie from mining to counterfeit pharmaceuticals, but he knew there were also many legitimate parts to the organisation and it certainly was not short of cash. So again why help terrorists?

He knew of course to keep his thoughts to himself. Whatever their endgame was, he knew it paid to just do the job and not to ask questions.

Matheus Franke chose this moment to walk in the operations room and he slumped up to attention as the man addressed his boss, the floor supervisor, two desks over.

“Has the clean-up team been sent to Munich?” The leader said in his usual gruff voice, and without preamble.

“Yes Sir, they are just waiting for the transfer of Mendez and the other prisoner from the police station to the remand facility, it will be easier and cleaner if they hit them en-route. The last man, Tanner the CIA agent will be done by another team simultaneously at his house. He is on home detention and the house he is in is unguarded. ”

“Good, I want Mendez especially out of the picture in the next few days before he has had a chance to talk. Anything else?”

“The younger negotiator, the one from Munich, left by train to Liechtenstein four days ago, our agent was unable to follow. The bugs he managed to plant have given us no real information but we now know there are two negotiators, a mother and daughter, the mother was some prominent judge in her day and may have done some of the heavy lifting on this case.”

Matheus grunted, seemingly thinking. Nobody in the room dared to interrupt.

Matheus was indeed thinking. Father was impatient with the slow results on the artefact, and worried about the woman who had interviewed Mendez finding out any more. Matheus had made it clear a few hours ago to his father that he thought it would be better that she was also out of the picture, but father was curious to know how much she knew.

“These negotiators will be taken care of soon enough. Let me know immediately when Mendez and the other two men are dead.”

The floor supervisor outwardly acknowledged the order with a nod and a “Yes Sir!”

Satisfied, the leader walked back out of the open area.

The overweight man sank back down into his chair, looking quickly around the room at the other faces. ‘Taken Care of’ was usually a euphemism for killed, and it looked like The ElderNacht were about to cross another line; a line where all the targets were all relatively well connected to government agencies. He couldn't see it ending well.

* * *

Charlie slid the bedsheets aside and walked naked to the shower. Professor Joe Montgomery was not quite as exceptional in the sack as on a computer or whiteboard, but she gave him a pass. He was still snoozing off the last of the red wine, the morning sun was filtering through the Venetian blinds in his top floor apartment overlooking the botanical gardens.

Charlie had learnt a lot, she had also enjoyed Joe’s company so much so that she finally succumbed to his flirtatious charm, the man after all met most of her criteria, charming, handsome and intelligent. The fact he wasn’t Italian being the only reason she would be leaving back for Geneva in a few days.

“Please say you don't want to go back to the lab?” A tired voice came from under the sheets.

Charlie was drying her hair and sat at the foot of the bed in her underwear.

“Oh Joe, I’m sorry if my extremely loud hair dryer woke you up, but there is the little matter of how you were going to show me how to set up the voice interaction and external control stuff this morning. You do remember that I still have people to visit and I am on a flight in a few days?”

A brown hand came out from under the sheet and pulled back enough to expose a young, good looking man with short black hair and a very designer five o'clock shadow.

“Seriously Charlie, you could just ask the AI to do that, and how do you expect me to be able to think of anything but your gorgeous..”

Charlie moved his creeping hand casually away from her rear.

“You missed your chance Joe, I only brought enough shampoo in my overnight bag for one shower.”

“Yet you managed to fit a hairdryer in your bag..” He said, shaking his head.

“Tut tut Joe, a lady always comes prepared, now get your arse into the kitchen and make your favourite student something for breakfast.

* * *

The computer lab was mostly empty the next day. A couple of Joe’s graduate students were working in other parts of the lab quietly, and Joe and another of his students were standing by a large triple monitor workstation. The door opened and two suited men were shown in by the department’s secretary Janice, who looked slightly worried. The two men walked through to where he was teaching.

“Professor Montgomery, a moment of your time please.” They both flashed badges indicating they were CIA.

Joe had dated enough lawyers and had enough swing in the university and community not to be intimidated by the two men.

“One second please gentlemen..” He said.

He ignored them for a few seconds pointing at something on the screen to the student then saying, “come back with the results in thirty minutes please Peter.”

The student left. And Joe gave his full attention to the two agents.

“Now what can I do for you fine gentlemen today?” Joe was having a hard time telling the two men apart, they looked and dressed the same and it was only that one was a little shorter, uglier and paler that he could distinguish them.

It was the tall one who spoke. “You recently spoke to a Miss Schneider from Switzerland. Can you tell us the nature of your business with her?”

“Ahh, Charlie said you may call and ask, and she said I should tell you. Well quite simply gentlemen, Miss Schneider had arranged a four day tutorial on the latest AI systems.”

“Isn’t that a bit unusual?” Said the shorter man with a much more unpleasant demeanour, and a distinctly southern accent.

“Not at all, I run several private tutorials and consultancies outside of my normal university tenure. I ran one for your NSA guys not two months ago and covered the same topics I did with Miss Schneider. I can show you the slides and notes if required.”

The two men looked at each other, unsure of the next question, Joe could tell they probably expected him not to be so open.

“Where did Miss Schneider go after the training course?” Said the short one again, his unpleasant demeanour unchanged.

“I have no idea,” said Joe casually. “She did say she was staying with friends before her flight out in a few days. Please don’t ask me who they are because I have no idea.”

“Did she discuss anything you thought was strange or unusual?” Said the short one again.

Strange, and unusual, basically covered everything Charlie said and did. The professor got a small flash drive out of his pocket and handed it to the more pleasant taller one, addressing him and not the shorter one. “She asked me to give this to the agents who would come and visit me. Does that count as strange or unusual?”

The two men looked at each other both seriously and confused. Seeing their expressions, Joe continued.

“Miss Schneider is an enigma gentleman, and the most intelligent person I know, period. I don't say that lightly, as I am a tenured professor at one of the world’s top universities, but that woman probably learnt more in those four days than most of my students learn in four years, and computers are not even her specialist subject. I suggest when you see her that you ask her yourself, though you may be a little late if she has left the country.”

The tall guy grunted, looked at the shorter guy with a shake of his head and said, “thank you professor, we will be in touch if we have any further questions.”

The two men walked off. Joe gave a mild shrug quietly adding, “good luck gentlemen, if you’re as smart as me then she will only be two steps ahead of you.”

* * *

Chambers sat in her usual chair in the executive meeting room above the operations floor. The view looked down onto her area through the one way glass windows.

The operations manager had just finished giving herself, her aide and the two liaisons the brief on the killing of Mendez and their agent Tanner in Munich. She stopped him when he moved on and casually mentioned the movements of the negotiator.

“The woman is in this country?”

“Yes Ma'am,” he said.

“Do we know what she is doing here?”

“Yes ma’am,” he said looking at his laptop. “Somebody contacted the address at the embassy she put down. The embassy replied that Miss Schneider was attending an AI training course at UC Berkeley with a professor Montgomery.”

“Two local field agents conducted a visit to the professor and he gave them full cooperation on what she had been doing. Professor Montgomery then handed the lead agent a flash drive from the woman, the drive was marked for the attention of the CIA.”

“Say again!” Said Chambers, giving the aide a strange look.

The aide began to repeat the sentence when Chambers stopped him. “It was rhetorical Castillo.” She shook her head, the shear fucking hubris of the woman!

“What the hell was on the drive?”

“There was a summarised report of the Munich operation, concentrating on an organisation she thinks was behind it, one called The ElderNacht. We already have a substantial file on the organisation, the DEA has an ongoing investigation for drug trafficking, so most of the information she gave us we already have.”

“However she added some new information, giving us the probable leader of the Munich branch of the organisation and the probable location of its headquarters in the Caribbean. Some of this checks with uncorroborated information we already have.”

The man paused a second as if unsure then added. “She also gave us more damning evidence that our Munich agent we planted inside of the company was on the take..”

“At least your department won't be getting any further comeback on that one,” interrupted the army liaison, a heavy set woman with McCloskey written on the breast pocket of her uniform.

She continued the jibe. “From where I am sitting, it looks like this little girl is doing a much better job than the whole of the delta operations department!”

Chamber’s chose to ignore the army brat, tempering down her anger. She was still seriously pissed off that the CIA director had assigned this agent Ethan Curran to investigate what happened in Munich and took her department off the case. But at least the storm over the Munich operation seemed to have died down; and with the murder of agent Tanner, she was also now hoping that the investigation would quietly disappear.

* * *

Charlie had worked with Joe’s AI models during the training to make a system that combined the university's colossal and up to date archives with the gigabytes of information she had brought in on her encrypted pen drive from her own Interpol and FIS sources. From that she now had a pretty good idea of who The ElderNacht was and their probable location, just not what their goal was.

Once she had the main facts in front of her though, she could speculate much better than any AI model could. The result was the condensed report she produced for the Swiss FIS, Interpol, and the CIA, who she knew were probably still struggling their way through the Munich data.

The ElderNacht puzzle had a lot of missing pieces she was unhappy with. Home, as far as she could tell, was in the independent Caribbean island state of Costa Verde. The Franke family owned a large portion of the sparsely populated east of the Island, a mountainous area with rain forests and a few small plantations. The family was very wealthy, very powerful, and very secretive, cleverly running their illicit operations through a series of shell companies and cells. The turnover from the Franke family operations she determined easily dwarfed the GDP of the little island state, and the small army of security they had, meant they were never going to have any problems with whoever was in power.

The history of the family was not very clear however. Matheus Franke, the name she plucked directly from the thoughts of Mendez, was the son of Doctor Henry Franke, whose father was Rudi Franke, a prominent Nazi in occupied Rome. It was believed Rudi Franke escaped to South America at the end of the war with his infant son, three other Nazi historians, and hundreds of items of stolen and looted artefacts.

So The ElderNacht seemed to be an empire built on Nazi loot, but not on Nazi ideals, especially if they were aiding Islamic terrorists. Something felt wrong to Charlie.

* * *

Ethan Curran was speculating on exactly the same thing, but for much different reasons. His carefully negotiated visit to Munich was smacked-down hard when Mendez and their agent were both shot dead the day before he was due to interview them.

With two days until his return flight he decided, rather than waste the time, he would try and rebuild some of the damage done between the CIA and the police force there. As he spoke fluent German, was technically an Interpol agent, and the fact was that it was now a murder investigation against an American citizen, he received a slightly more sympathetic ear from the police.

After apologising to the police chief for the stupidity of the CIA, and asking if he could have any extra information they had that would help him complete his analysis, he was surprised to receive full access to the interviews and all the case notes that the negotiator had left for them.

The notes from the negotiator were impressive, more like a scientific paper rather than the normal investigative report he was used to reading.

The comments from the interviewer regarding the negotiator also intrigued him as Von-Gruden came across to him as a very competent investigator. The investigator was very impressed with the woman, saying her insights seemed to go beyond the usual mentalist ones, and in all cases proved to be both helpful and accurate.

After he had done all he could in Munich, he decided his next logical step was to arrange a meeting with this negotiator. He expected anyone who impressed an efficient investigator like Von-Gruden would likely have some further insight, and to tell the truth he was actually looking forward to talking with her. It was on impulse that he decided to phone the number given by the negotiator to the police in Munich.

“Negotiator liaison office. Alice Bortolusso speaking,” came back the crisp French voice.

“Hello Mrs Bortolusso, my name is Ethan Curran. I am an agent of the US Government working on the murder in Munich of an American agent and the person he was investigating. I was given information that one of your employees, Miss Charlene Schneider, attended the final interviews of both the victims. I was hoping I could speak with her, or at least arrange a meeting to see if she could give me any further information.”

“Mr Curran. Miss Schneider is not an employee of any company I am aware of, but I can get in touch with her. Can you email your request to me, and I will expedite it. Please note she is currently out of the country for a few days, and not obligated to reply to you, however I expect she or the other negotiator will wish to talk to you.”

Alice gave him her email contact address and, after a few pleasantries, rang-off.

Interesting thought Ethan.

He received the new copy of Charlie’s summary report from his Interpol office when he arrived back at his hotel. He realised the same report had also gone to other agencies including the CIA and the Munich police. He was very impressed by the summary she gave and decided to follow it up in his call to the deputy director later that evening. His flight to DC was the next day, if there were follow up questions he could answer in person.

Ethan was very different from the normal secret service types. His doctorate was in criminal psychology and during his four year stint in the FBI was responsible for solving two of the most perplexing cases in US history, including catching the serial killer known as the death angel.

Because of his language skills, acquired from his Italian father and German mother, he was moved sideways into the New York Interpol office backing up the FBI, police, and the DEA.

Ethan earned a reputation for thoroughness, and political neutrality, which meant he often got pulled from his normal duties to discreetly assist many of the other agencies especially during delicate international matters.

A few weeks before getting the call from the new CIA director he had been going through a bit of a mid-life crisis. Work was getting more and more mundane, and the bureaucracy was eating more and more into the cases he liked to pursue. He had already made the decision to resign at the end of the year, sell off everything he owned and travel the world for a few years. The decision, which had not told anybody about yet, had already lifted his spirits a lot.

He arrived back at his Washington apartment just after eight PM, and decided to go for a short run along the Potomac river, before turning in early. Ethan did his best thinking while jogging, but he was barely fifteen minutes into his run when he put two and two together and realised that this woman was probably in very great danger if The ElderNacht were tidying up.

He broke off his jog to call his usual CIA contact Priscilla at the operations office.

“Hi Pri, Ethan here. Do you have the location of the Swiss negotiator woman Charlene Schneider?”

“Yes, she is due to fly out of San Francisco airport tomorrow afternoon.”

Interesting! “Do we know what she was doing here?” He asked curiously.

He heard the rapid typing in the background as Priscilla searched for the files.

“Yes, she was training with a professor Montgomery at..” Priscilla stopped as she read ahead. “Wait, oh that's weird, there is a detain order for her on the system! She is to be stopped and detained at the airport for questioning by.. By, well, by us!”

Priscilla sounded confused. “That’s not right Ethan, I checked the system yesterday and there was no such order. There is also no record of which agent has been assigned.. It seems very, unspecific..”

“Shit! Pri, I need that order removed now. I am guessing that whoever issued it has a motive other than interviewing her.”

“I can’t Ethan, whoever did this knows the system, it's locked me out, I will need to phone the San Francisco office directly..”

“Do that Pri, tell them that she is not to be touched, and also I need to be on a flight to San Francisco tonight, can you get me there?”

“Of course. Let me call you back in a few minutes.”

The phone went dead, and Ethan about-turned, and ran straight back to his apartment. He got the call as he entered his front door. He was booked on the 10 p.m. flight which with the zone change would get him there at one in the morning.

* * *

The five hour flight was uncomfortable but thankfully nearly empty, allowing him to take all three rows and get a few hours shut-eye. Priscilla had booked him in the airport hotel and he managed a little more sleep before working through the few contacts who would take his early calls.

Ethan was inside the director of immigration’s office, at the airport, at eight AM, on a conference call with the operations director of the local CIA office, and the captain of the airport security police.

The level of acrimony between the three parties would usually make such a meeting unbearable, but he was currently negotiating this particular minefield quite well.

“No agent has been assigned from this office,” the CIA operations director said bluntly. “I can see the detain order was added to the system only yesterday and then removed from the system last night, something doesn't feel right. Have you phoned Chambers? The order is in her name.”

“I checked with my liaison at the CIA and it could not have been her, however we will need to shelve this part of the discussion. The main thing is to confirm that you have no agent assigned, and the order has been removed from both systems.”

Ethan looked to the director of immigrations who looked at his terminal. “Yes I have just cancelled the order here. It is an old IT system so it will show as cancelled, but my officers are aware of the deficiencies in the system and will not impede her.”

“Good.” He turned to the captain. “We may still have an issue. The fact remains that somebody issued that order and somebody may still turn up. It may still be a genuine error, but there are a lot of bad vibes coming from all this. We need to assume whoever turns up will not be CIA, will be armed and will most certainly be dangerous, so we need to ensure your guys are prepared for that eventuality.”

“My guys are well trained Agent Curran.” Said the hard faced stoic man in the police uniform.

“Good, then we should have the advantage. If whoever this agent is, has not been informed that the order has been cancelled then they should not be suspicious and they will turn up expecting full cooperation. If they have been informed they will not turn up.”

Ethan got up from the seat in front of the director of immigration’s desk.

“I thank you all. This still remains a CIA operation, and if somebody is taken into custody, the CIA will take custody of the suspect. However regarding the detention, airport security will be the ones to take them into custody. I think we can do this without anybody getting hurt.”

“I will get some agents down as soon as possible,” said the CIA man.

* * *

Crane was used to power and getting what he wanted, and didn't think twice when he was admitted to the secure area without so much as a glance at his fake credentials. He was a well regarded member of The ElderNacht with fifteen confirmed kills. He liked his work. Moran had been his CIA contact for four years now without even so much the slightest hint of any problems.

“The special interview room has been prepared for you sir,” said the police officer in a deferred tone that Crane liked. Then the man led him down the windowless grey corridor.

Crane smiled as the man opened the metal door for him to enter, then as he did he stopped in shock as two police officers stood to either side of him with full tactical response suits and loaded automatic weapons pointed straight at him.

The two policemen's demeanours said they were very aware of the person they were dealing with, and how dangerous he was. Crane’s brief impulsive thoughts to reach for his gun disappeared instantly.

The door clanged behind him and a new voice said slowly and carefully.

“Place both your hands behind your back now!”

“You guys are in a world of trouble, you do realise I am CIA and the second..”

“Shut up and do exactly as you are told NOW!” Came the same voice, this time with no trace of pleasantness.

Crane heard another gun readied behind him. And the same voice continued. “You have five seconds to comply, if you do not I will shoot your left shoulder. Five.. four.. Three..”

“OK.. OK..” Said Crane anxiously. Both his hands coming up in a surrender gesture, then coming together slowly behind his back.

A rough set of hands gripped his arms and a set of cuffs were snapped onto him. A hand reached over him and into his jacket pocket lifting the 9mm Beretta out carefully.

“This will be the end of all your careers..” started Crane in his most imposing voice before he was kicked in the back of his knees and went to the floor.

The same hands placed a set of ankle cuffs on him, then dragging him up, frisking him, removing the knife and the second gun from his ankle. The hand forced him into the chair next to the metal table. Both the table and the chair were fixed to the floor and unmoveable and his handcuffs were then fixed to the hook at the centre of the table.

“Thank you guys.” Said the mystery voice, as he appeared from behind him. “I think we have all his toys.”

The two policemen relaxed, their guns falling back to their sides. Crane looked around the bland room, to the two cops standing a few metres back, then to the policeman who had so thoroughly disarmed him, and finally to the new man walking into focus, who was not even armed.

The new man was most definitely the one in charge, he was younger than he expected, with medium black hair, two day old stubble and a pair of thin rimmed glasses over what looked like green eyes. The man also did not get the memo about the CIA standard issue black suit, and his tweed suit made him look more like a history professor than any agent Crane had ever dealt with. The new agent held both his fake ID and his wallet and was looking at them.

“I am sure you have a very good explanation for impersonating a CIA officer,” he looked at the man’s wallet. “Mr Crane.”

“The CIA will be here soon enough but since you obviously have a contact or two in the CIA we will be using agents from the local office. Also, in case you are thinking that the person who sent you will get you out of here, please note that I have also informed the Director of the CIA. So Mr Crane, unless that is your contact you won't be getting out of this mess anytime soon.”

* * *

“You didn't really have to drive me to the airport Pamela,” said Charlie to the woman next to her in the back seat of the limousine.

“Oh come on Charlie, you’ve not been state-side for ages! If you think I was going to let you go back to Europe without seeing me, you were very much mistaken. Anyway, I have to fly to London for his award ceremony tomorrow, and what is Roger going to do without someone to pick out the right shirt and tie to wear.”

Charlie laughed. “Since when do US Senators have to dress their husbands Pamela?”

“Since they have to stand next to them at award ceremonies and make them look good my dear.”

Charlie had stayed at a large serviced apartment next to the University for the last five nights then drove down to Monterey to stay with a friend, then as a guest at Pamela’s family home yesterday spending a relaxing evening chewing the fat over dinner.

Pamela was a good friend of her mothers and often stayed over when she was in Europe. Gerty and Pamela went to school together for two years in Oxford while Pamela's father was the UK ambassador there in the mid seventies. Little Charlie had seen Pamela several times over the years, and was a very good friend of her youngest son Harry, who was unfortunately still serving overseas in the military.

The car stopped in the airport’s VIP drop-off zone and Charlie heard the rear trunk of the car click open before the driver and Carl got out.

“How do you travel with no luggage? I mean it's easy for me as I have my house here and an apartment in DC but you..”

“My luggage will follow me. I still have the diplomatic passport which means my luggage can travel on its own using the diplomatic services, sort of a thank you for my mother and me doing the occasional service for the Swiss government.”

“It’s a shame you’re not an American citizen, I could really do with someone like you on my technology committee, those big tech-giant assholes are lying through their teeth and I know it..” She paused, forgetting whose company she was in, and getting out of her senator mode. “Anyway, it was really nice to share some of your insight into that AI voodoo with me.”

Pamela’s door was opened by the chauffeur before Charlie replied, and both the women got out; Charlie with her little backpack, and Pamela with a cabin bag on wheels. The chauffeur trailed along behind pushing two massive cases. Carl held a small brown leather rucksack over his left shoulder following all of them another eight metres behind. Carl could not carry his weapon in the USA, despite the fact he had the same diplomatic stamp. It did not however make him any less deadly.

“Why don’t you check in and I will meet you at the Centurion Lounge in thirty minutes, I have a quick private phone call to make,” said Pamela.

Charlie stepped up to the immigration officer and presented her passport and boarding pass, then immediately knew something was possibly wrong.

The man was looking at some message on his screen which said ‘CIA detain for interview’, and ‘cancelled’ next to it, he was also reconciling it with the fact he had just seen the diplomatic visa stamp on the passport.

“Is there a problem officer?” She said seriously.

“Er, no miss, everything is fine,” he said respectfully and handed her passport back, then directed her into the diplomatic line, bypassing the usual TSA madness.

So the CIA had issued a stop and interrogate order and then cancelled it. Interesting. She waited for Carl who had the same visa.

* * *

Ethan watched the woman from behind the one-way glass, as she waited for the large blond man to pass through immigration. He was trying to reconcile the image he had of her in his mind before this, and the image of the beautiful woman standing before him now. He couldn't.

Thank god he had checked in with Priscilla, had got here in time, and had the detain order cancelled. The Munich debacle would have paled into insignificance if anything would have happened to her here.

Crane, whoever he turned out to be, was obviously a hired killer, and whoever had hired him, had the audacity to want her murder framed on the CIA and the USA. He knew the shitstorm he had initiated was going to cause someone a few sleepless nights, but that was far preferable to what could have happened.

He looked again at the woman, yes she had a bodyguard he doubted anyone would tangle with, but the man would have been useless here, where the whole system would have been against him. It would only have taken a short time for Crane to get her away and.. He shuddered again, looking into her dark eyes, he looked forward to meeting this woman properly and under nicer circumstances.

She was still waiting for her bodyguard when she suddenly turned directly to him in the window and winked.

* * *

Charlie wondered who the stranger was behind the one way glass mirror. She had caught the thoughts several times from the man wondering about if she could see him. She had even thought about being a little smarter, getting her lipstick out and using it as a makeup mirror while reading more on the man who had presumably just averted her attempted abduction. But as she caught the kind thoughts, and the ones that they would meet soon anyway, she just smiled and winked. Let him read into the gesture what he wanted.

It was one of those nice rare occasions where two people met and immediately liked each other. One saw the mind but could not see the person, the other saw the person but could not see the mind.

She was joined by Carl a few seconds later and headed off to the guest lounge.

# Anne-Marie’s Awakening

ANNE-MARIE WAS AN ENIGMA. She had never fitted any of the perceived definitions of a French lady, she neither dressed well, nor had any of the sophistication society expected of one. She was short, a bit skinny, never exercised and was pale skinned due to her never going out much. Her medium length black hair was often unwashed for days and held in a bun, the antithesis of a shampoo commercial. Yet there was also an underlying beauty to her, unrecognised by herself, but apparent to anybody who looked closer.

She was also extremely intelligent and analytical, and the top of her small field of chaos mathematics. She shunned all invites to conferences, declined to give talks, and straight up ignored collaboration requests on papers. In fact her only published work was from her PhD dissertation which was received extremely well in the very small community of mathematicians she belonged to; and was enough to get her the invite to join the small team at CERN twelve months earlier. It was a job that suited her demeanour well, and she did not look back. She was happy to disappear.

Anne-Marie’s chronic shyness had been diagnosed early, and was something she had tried to come to terms with many times but failed. She was now paying the price of hiding away too many times from would-be friends and suitors, with a life of loneliness. She hid it all behind a lab coat and a thin veneer of happiness, a twenty four year old virgin who lived for her work.

All that changed for some reason when Michael appeared.

Whatever had happened in those first few seconds to Anne-Marie had been a slap in the face. Carlos noticed, Luc noticed, and the engineers, the technicians and even the cleaner noticed, but Michael, without context, did not. Something about Michael had made her warm to him immediately, for the first time knowing what to say without freezing, or shying off back into her work. Feelings that she had known were there, bubbled to the surface, and she found herself distracted from her work, looking at her reflection in the mirror and wondering, for the first time, what it would be like to be with a man like him.

In the shopping mall after work, she walked aimlessly and self-consciously past the fashion shops and hair salons, watching the women who shopped or worked there, but not knowing how to proceed. The only women Anne-Marie even knew to talk to were her step-mother, step-sister and Julianne the cleaner. There was also Jessabelle, one of the electronics engineers who shared their workspace. But Jez terrified her even more than the men, she was a big woman, a goth, whose tattoos and dark makeup scared Anne-Marie so much that she would avoid her presence whenever she could. When she had to converse with Jez it was usually in the form of single syllable words as she shied away.

Julianne the cleaner was different. She was quite approachable, friendly and was always non-threatening with complementary banter when they crossed paths. Anne-Marie knew she also had another side, as she had once met her outside of the facility in a cafe with a man. She had hardly recognised Julianne when she walked over to Anne-Marie that day to say hello, as she was a different woman. Not the uniformed office cleaner, but a well dressed and sophisticated lady, as beautiful as any she had seen walking in and around the city. Anne-Marie had, as usual, shied away from the encounter, making the briefest polite comment she could before locking up as Julianne’s husband and children approached. Luckily Julianne had recognised the symptoms of her shyness immediately from her experience with her sister, and backed off with a pleasant smile and goodbye.

“Julianne?” Anne-Marie said the next morning. “What do you think of Michael?”

Julianne looked at Anne-Marie, hiding her surprise much better than Luc and Carlos had, and knowing something was different. Looking into Anne-Marie’s sweet blue eyes she finally understood the question for what it really meant.

“I think he is very handsome, ma Cherie.” she said kindly. “He is also very friendly and has a very sweet strange accent which makes you want to talk more with him. I can also see he has noticed you, and likes you a lot.”

Anne-Marie was shocked. How could Julianne tell such a thing from her brief encounter between the three of them. She tried to ignore it, but couldn’t.

“But how would someone like me..” She never finished the sentence.

“Anne-Marie,” she said softly. “You are your own worst enemy! Your looks are hidden away behind a lab coat and buried underground by a hundred metres of concrete. You are young, intelligent, have a beautiful face and a nice body, yet you de-emphasized everything about your femininity until yesterday.”

She looked into Anne-Marie's eyes. “Even a middle-aged woman like me, who has two children, can excite passion in a man, yet you never try.”

“Oh Julianne, you should not demean yourself, the woman I saw in the cafe that day was beautiful and charming.,”

“And the woman I saw in that cafe that day could also be beautiful and charming if she made a tiny little effort.” Julianne replied assertively, emphasising the statement with her finger and thumb very close together.

“Yesterday, I saw a tiny effort to make that change and the effect was profound. Everybody has noticed the new woman, the one who was previously invisible.”

Julianne saw that Anne-Marie was seriously considering what she said, but that some apprehension was still there. She got her cell-phone out of her overall pocket and scrolled through some screens. “Let me write this number down, it is the number of my younger sister Sophie..”

Anne-Marie felt her anxiety rising up, and Julianne saw the change in her countenance.

“I can see you are worried,” she said softly, touching Anne-Marie’s arm gently. “But Sophie is not like that. She too is pretty, and she too has a similar shyness, but like you now, she is making the effort to change, and slowly she is conquering it. She still does not have many friends, but I know you both and I think you would like each other and could help each other.”

She moved her hand down Anne-Marie’s arm to hold her hand. “I have talked to Sophie many times, I even mentioned you as a possible friend, but she was much too shy then and I did not think the time was right. Now it is.”

Juliette squeezed her hand gently. “Sophie will never judge you as she is in the same place as you and trying to move to a better one. Please let her try and help you Anne-Marie, I see you alone here with nobody to talk to but these computers and I am sad inside that the rose before me will wither before it has bloomed. If this man is in your future you deserve all the help you can to catch him. All women deserve to be loved, and love does not mean you cannot still be a great woman in other areas.”

Anne-Marie was stunned by the prophetic words. Julianne was a lot more than she appeared to be. But she kept silent, not replying or moving for several seconds as Julianne wrote down the number on a scrap of paper and handed it to her with just one word, backed by an intense emotion. “Please.”

Julianne may not have known what was going through Anne-Marie's head but she was clever enough not to push it further, smiling and turning away and going back to her work.

Anne-Marie slowly and carefully added the number into her phone and walked back to her workstation in a daze. Michael was there already, animatedly talking to Carlos next to a large screen, Luc nowhere in sight. She looked at him, the unfamiliar emotions arising again in her, and she resolved to call Sophie.

* * *

Michael gradually started to notice the same thing everybody else had, that Anne-Marie was actually a quite beautiful woman. Each day he would see some little change in her, and though Michael was naturally flirtatious, he kept it under control with Anne-Marie due to them being work colleagues, and he in an unfamiliar country with unfamiliar customs. Instead he was complementary of her new looks and careful not to push the narrative. While Anne-Maria understood that his compliments were not romantic, it was hard not to secretly imagine his words had real meaning.

“Luc, what is it with Anne-Marie?” Michael said a week later. “Was she just having a bad-hair day when we first met?”

Luc looked at him, putting his finger in the air in a ‘wait’ gesture then finished typing something into his computer.

He spun his chair around to face Michael. “Bad-hair day! Ha, most certainly not Michael. Anne-Marie is criminally shy, it took her a month before she would even say two words to me that were not related to the project. Something has changed, for the better. Maybe she has found a boyfriend or a girlfriend, but whatever it is, and whoever caused it I am so very happy for her, she always seemed so lonely.”

Michael looked at him bemused. “I wanted to ask her to help me improve my French, she seems to like to talk to me, but I do not want to cross any lines.”

“You know Michael, if I was not engaged to be married, I would be on my knees to someone like Anne-Marie this minute to ask her to help ‘improve my French’.”

Luc smiled and winked using air quotes for the last three words, and Michael grimaced. Luc had obviously found a double-meaning for the words ‘improve my French!’.

“No seriously Luc, it's not like that with Anne-Marie! I met someone special, but they insisted that the next time we met that the conversation would be fully in French. It is tearing my hair out Luc, time is ticking away, and my French is still terrible. I thought Anne-Marie might want to help, she has been so patient with me here.”

“So did you ask her?” He said off-handedly.

“Not.. Er, not really..” Said Michael slowly, “I may have hinted I want to improve my French outside of the CERN facility, but..”

“But you never asked?” Prompted Luc for the second time.

“No, I guess I didn't. But if she has a boyfriend it would be very inappropriate for me to ask..”

“Why would it be inappropriate if your reasons are just to talk?”

Luc shook his head and went back to his screen, not waiting or interested in the reply. Damn, Luc was right, he needed to ask her. Since that first day when he first talked to her, his spirits had improved considerably. Each morning he came in early and made small talk with Julianne and then Anne-Marie, talking to Anne-Marie was the highlight of his day. But the work setting was wrong, stifling.

He sighed inwardly, why was he even obsessing over this Charlie woman anyway! She probably had millionaire playboys falling at her feet every evening. Yet every time he had that same argument with himself he always lost. Charlie made the invite, she paid the bill, she owed him at least the chance for him to buy her a coffee. It did not have to be romantic, and the more he thought about it, the more he realised it could probably never be romantic. He really did have nothing a woman such as Charlie would ever want, but it could be platonic, they did seem to hit it off after all.

He shook his head resolved. Yes he would talk to Anne-Marie, ask her properly, and make sure she knows it is just as friends so as not to scare her off. But the situation had to be right to ask the question.

Michael sighed, suddenly realising he had now doubled his women problem!

* * *

“Anne-Marie.” Michael said, pausing his playlist. They were finally alone in the office, Carlos was at a meeting for the rest of the day and Luc had left the office early a few minutes ago, leaving just the quiet hum of the distant computers and racked equipment.

“I have never really asked you what you are working on.”

She paused her typing and slowly swivelled her chair to face Michael across the room. The help Julianne’s sister Sophie was giving her was above and beyond beauty and fashion tips, she was becoming a friend, and to Anne-Marie it was priceless. Yet she had been wondering for the past few days if the changes she was making to herself were actually pushing Michael away, not bringing him closer.

Julianne had laughed at the notion. “If he has become more awkward around you, it is because he is seeing a different woman. His eyes however do not lie, his attraction to you is if anything greater than it was.”

“But why has he not hinted anymore about going out?”

“My darling, I have no idea what he is thinking, he is a man, men can be stupid not noticing what is right in front of their face, but it will come, and if not, there are many more men noticing you now.”

That last bit had scared her a little, Anne-Marie had been known to cross streets when a man so much as looked her way, and despite the help she was getting from Sophie, she doubted that would have changed any.

She looked at Michael knowing the few seconds that passed in silence would be taken for her thinking of an English reply.

“You have only been here two weeks Michael and there is a lot for you to learn and far more important things for you to do. The help you are giving them with your new program has certainly improved Carlos’s disposition.”

“But,” Michael continued, “I would really like to know what you are doing, I am genuinely interested and maybe I can even help.”

His smile was contagious, and she returned it.

“It is a bit of a wild tangent from our current work, it is not even related to our department but more to do with the work of the guys in the WOW lab above. It is based on some interesting incident matches their team has found over the years and a tenuous connection with some other research, it is probably a little too silly and too far fetched for me to talk about.”

Michael was intrigued.

“I don't mind Anne-Marie, even if you said you were looking for flying saucers causing their problem. I would love to be able to help you prove or disprove your theories either way, and..” He hesitated enough for Anne-Marie to notice, “we have not talked as much as I would have liked these last few days.”

It was true; Since Julianne had mentioned it the other day, she had noticed the quick glances in her direction with those beautiful eyes, he really had been waiting to get her alone to talk to her. However Anne-Marie hesitated in replying, more for professional than personal reasons. She had recently read a speculative paper from a famous physicist with absolutely no foundation, but which resonated somehow for her with the problem the other team were having. She inwardly sighed, well, at least it was not as bad as flying saucers!

“Have you heard of the theory of quantum consciousness?”

“A little,” said Michael, pensively. “I heard a podcast a few years back from a physicist who briefly went into it, something about an explanation for how human consciousness works and how it is related to the collapsing wave function or something or other, but I am not a physicist or a biologist.”

“Yes it is very speculative, and not widely accepted, many scientists think we do not need a quantum anything to explain how we think. However, I was checking the timeline of the two largest events the guys above logged, and I came across a weird blog post for the second one. It was from our local university which is only five kilometres away.”

She paused, still a little unsure she should be mentioning it, but Michael’s expression was open and interested.

“The post said everyone in the library felt the same sense of anxiousness and fear at the same time, everybody looking at each other as if they were the cause. The woman who posted it said she and several others also got the picture of some dangerous animal about to attack, the fear, anxiousness and image disappearing almost immediately. The timing was coincidental almost to the minute. I would love to find somebody who was there.”

“So their WOW event, which may or may not be quantum but does randomly affect the distribution and paths of certain particles at our detectors may also have been detected by some students at Geneva University using their minds?”

“Yes I know, when you say it like that it sounds stupid, also why just the university here? It would imply their event was local to Geneva itself, and I know from random chatter from the technicians around the coffee machine that Fermi-lab in America has similar detection events.”

“No Anne-Marie it is not so stupid, it is the smart, out-of-the-box thinking, which makes breakthroughs in science.”

He briefly had to explain what out-of-the-box meant to her and she gave him the translated euphemism in French.

“You know, Geneva university could have been doing some sort of experiment whose effect fell off at an exponential rate, and you don’t have any data from Fermi-lab to correlate. I think there is some merit in checking further though. You should talk to one of the professors in the WOW project, or ask professor Bonito to talk with them..”

“Oh no,” said Anne-Marie looking much more shocked than Michael expected, “please don’t talk to anybody else, I would rather work on the data privately before I make myself look silly.”

“Then why don’t I help you? It would not be too difficult to trawl the datasets I have accumulated and I have access to all of the data the WOW lab does. The public data I can access contains billions of posts and may go back to that time and others.”

“You would help?” She said enthusiastically.

“Of course I will help!” He repeated with the same enthusiasm and suddenly realising it was true, he really did want to help her, to be closer to her.

“Even if we do not find any correlations that back up your theory,” he continued, “it might help me improve my programs for other departments.”

Michael paused for a few seconds studying the woman, and knowing the time was right; He had practised the sentence he was going to say in French, and hoped he didn’t bodge it. It was a slower, more emotional voice that spoke to her.

“Have you realised we are sort of opposites, you and me. You work in chaos and I work in logic, yet we seem to get on so well together. Do you think it is true that opposites attract?”

Anne-Marie was a little stunned at the about-turn in their conversation and his countenance. Their talk up to now had been in English and work related, except for the little snippets each morning, ones which seemed to have got more awkward for Michael.

She stayed silent, trying her best not to change her neutral expression in case Michael interpreted it the wrong way. He didn't seem to be expecting an answer, but instead looked at her deeply, and continued in the same voice.

“I have been meaning to ask you, but have been too scared. I was thinking that maybe we can meet outside of work? I would love to improve my French, Anne-Marie, and talking with you is so easy.”

Too scared? Michael? Of talking to her? It didn’t make sense, why would someone as amazing and confident as Michael be too scared to talk to her?

Michael realised he was fumbling this a little and had added far too many words into the sentence, but continued nevertheless. “I have no real plans for the weekend, maybe we can explore Geneva together, my treat?”

He watched her smile widen and knew before she said it that the answer was going to be yes.

“I would really like that Michael.”

Even Anne-Marie, who had little empathic ability, saw his expression change from apprehension to joy. They spent the next hour at her workstation looking at her theory, there was no awkwardness as they talked, they sat closer than was probably appropriate, feeling the small bond of friendship between them solidify into something a little deeper.

Their talk strayed off the science and onto the weekend excursion to the city, and they agreed to meet the next morning at the bus stop by the CERN visitor centre.

He really didn't want to leave her, but tonight he was viewing what was hopefully going to be his new apartment in Saint-Genis. He would be sharing it with another two of the guys he arrived with, but he would have a room of his own and he was really looking forward to getting somewhere more permanent.

Things had started to look up for both of them.

* * *

The next day Michael and Anne-Marie got the shuttle bus from the visitor centre to Geneva central. It was the first time he had seen Anne-Marie without a lab coat on and he thought she looked stunning in her pastel dress, light green hat and low heel shoes.

Anne-Marie’s new clothes had been bought two days before as she and Sophie navigated through a fashion mall on the outskirts of the city. Sophie was becoming a real friend, like with Michael, the awkwardness had vanished with her almost at their first meeting.

With seemingly so little in common Anne-Marie was happy to learn more about Sophie's specialist subjects of fashion, romance novels, and men watching, subjects Anne-Marie had never taken an interest in but found strangely addictive. Sophie made the last one fun, she had only begun dating herself only a few months earlier, and she was eager to pass on her new found knowledge of men to Anne-Marie.

“Anne-Marie, you look amazing,” Michael said with what she thought was great enthusiasm, kissing both her cheeks and making her feel like a million dollars. For some reason, neither the crowded bus, nor the busy streets when they arrived in the city bothered her, and being with Michael, talking to him, laughing with him, removed all the anxiety she normally felt in these situations.

They had breakfast at a cafe on the north bank at the entrance to the river. Michael told her of his history, his school, university and why he had accepted the position at CERN, and Anne-Marie chipped in occasionally correcting his grammar or giving him a better word. It was nothing like the baptism by fire from his tutor in Montreux, it was pleasant and he was genuinely enjoying himself for the first time.

She was quite shocked when she found out that he had only started learning French nine weeks earlier on an app, and had only been speaking it for five weeks. It had taken her years to get anywhere near this level of proficiency in English and she asked him if he would reciprocate doing the same for her at work. Unfortunately in both languages, there were a shocking amount of colloquialisms and slang.

They walked the lakeside promenade stopping at various landmarks, and during this Anne-Marie told him of her not so exciting life growing up in Toulouse. She talked at normal conversational speed in her best French, stopping to translate words or colloquialisms he did not know, which he tried to save for a break in her talk so as not to spoil the flow.

Anne-Marie was the happiest she had ever been. She knew it wasn't a date, but just being around Michael and talking to a man as a friend was something she often dreamed about. He surprised her with a two hour lunch cruise around the lake, hoping to cool down from the mid-summer heat and taking in the sights of Mount Blanc and the beautiful mountain vistas.

“So Michael, what is this obsession with learning French?” Anne-Marie queried after they had finished lunch and were sipping a Sauvignon Blanc.

Michael quickly explained the story of his meeting with Charlie, the strange coincidences, explaining carefully how he saw this new girl as just a friend not a lover. Anne-Marie felt that same sensation of deja vu as they were talking about the woman, but she felt relaxed, there was just enough breeze to keep her cool and the drink was having some small effect on her.

“You know, she did say she saw you the day before, and if you were on your computer then she may just have a good memory. And if she was as good at English as you say, she probably could tell your original accent quite easily. Also, a woman with a twelve thousand dollar bag would very likely have gone to a top English school. She may also have been native English and it was just her French that was very good, you had nothing to judge her against. Remember you could only just string a couple of words together at the time.”

“You’re right Anne-Marie, Charlie was also probably the first person who talked to me like a real person, and not a cash machine. I guess I am just reading a little too much into it, but there was something about her that said she was different, like she was a princess who came out of her castle to see how the common people lived.”

Anne-Marie laughed. “Still there were the birds you say left when she stared at them, and returned once she left, I have no logic that can answer that, though you did seem to be blinded by her, and coincidences happen all the time.”

“I am feeling a little stupid, talking these things through with you, and for talking about another woman when the one before me is so lovely.” He took a small sip of his wine. “I do at least owe Charlie a coffee for being so nice to me, and for giving me an incentive to learn your beautiful language.”

He paused and looked into her eyes hoping he had not gone too far, then went further, “also, if I hadn't met her I might not be here with you now.”

Sophie had asked Anne-Marie how much she knew about sex, and, from the giggling, guessed almost immediately from her answer that she had never been with a man. The next few hours were difficult. Sophie’s first two encounters were nothing like her romance novels, they were flattering, but shallow, and both expected sex, both left her feeling upset and used. The man she was now with was much nicer, more interested in her as a person and happy to leave the pace of the relationship to Sophie, she wanted someone like that for her friend. Anne-Marie, she could see, was much more beautiful than her, but also very naive and probably gullible, her only advice was not to rush, not to trust without some form of trust in return, and never to make decisions when drinking alcohol.

Anne-Marie certainly felt a little light headed, even though she had only had a glass and a half of wine, and Michael was certainly being flattering. No, she dismissed it, she was still in command, and Michael did not have any of the subtleties of the French language to be deceptive, and at no point had he been inappropriate or even suggestive.

She found she was not even jealous of this other woman, though by all accounts she was prettier, more sophisticated, richer and spoke better English than her. Strangely and inexplicably, she actually found herself wanting to meet this woman herself.

“You know Michael, I think I would rather like to meet this woman too.” The words came out slow and casual and Anne-Marie was really shocked she was saying them out loud. Her meeting a stranger, outrageous, yet she had made the effort twice now and she was so much happier than she had ever been!

“Oh I think she would love someone like you,” Michael said without the same level of analysis. “Though I seriously doubt she has your easy demeanour or half your intelligence.”

She smiled shyly, while Michael laughed and turned the subject briefly back to work and to English, before they realised they were back at the shore.

Anne-Marie had an amazing day. Everything conspired to make it perfect from the weather, the company to the food and the wine, she didn't want it to end and was sad when they arrived back at the visitor centre late afternoon. There was a lot more of the day and evening left and Anne-Marie could happily have done it all again.

“Thank you again Anne-Marie, I had a wonderful day and learnt so much, you were truly wonderful company. If they ever invent a time machine I will mark this morning off in my diary and that is where I will go back to.”

He really did say the weirdest and sweetest things, she shivered strangely, wondering if Michael’s eyes really did just twinkle!

“I hope we can do it again soon, Michael.”

She glanced at Sophie in the distance looking at her, smiling, then she kissed him on both cheeks and he brought her in for a small, not too tight hug, one she did not want to end. He broke it off too quickly and bounded off towards his dormitory seemingly very happy.

Sophie walked over and kissed her too, “you are right, ma Cherie, he is a very handsome man, and that dress looks beautiful on you. Come, let us go for a coffee together and you can tell me all about your not-a-date, and I will tell you if your lessons are complete.”

Anne-Marie smiled, her words came out almost in song, “Oh Sophie, everything you have done for me made my day perfect, he really was a gentleman. If I died now I would die the happiest girl alive.”

“Oh, Anne-Marie,” she smiled, taking her arm in hers and leading her over to the cafe, “you had better not die now, you have only just taken your first step of happiness, there may still be slippery slopes and disappointments on the way, but when you reach the end of the path, the happiness you feel today will feel like a rainy day in the winter.”

The two women walked off to the visitor centre coffee shop arm in arm. Sophie had also been apprehensive when her sister phoned her that afternoon and told her she had given her number to a strange woman she did not know. Yet the more she heard of the woman the more she saw herself and someone she would have liked to have been a friend with. She was pleased to get the call the same day from a very nervous copy of herself.

Sophie owed her sister Juliette big time for bringing her to Geneva and for being there when nobody else was. She now owed her again for putting her together with Anne-Marie, in whom she saw a soulmate, someone she would like to get to know better and become friends with.

Anne-Marie was similarly happy. From having no friends, she now had two amazing ones in Sophie and Michael, and she felt like the luckiest girl alive.

* * *

“So this data also shows a very small event last Thursday, in fact with the better resolution I would say there was an event at two PM, that was just over the noise threshold and a larger one a few seconds later.”

Michael pointed to the large monitor, “Now if you run Anne-Marie’s new noise cancelling algorithm on the detector data you can see, here and here, small secondary events smaller than the primary events.”

All of the team had gathered around the big monitor and were looking at the three coloured spiky graphs.

“This is really good,” said Carlos, “I am seeing the chief scientist of the WOW project tomorrow, I’m sure it will be of great help to their team. How are you eliminating the noise Anne-Marie?”

“Just some new algorithms I’m working on,” she lied. The truth was she was still trying to understand where they came from, it was like she was typing away on auto-pilot thinking about her and Michael's trip to Geneva, and next thing you know she had added some beautiful mathematics into her code.

“Well they are Genius, and deserve to be written up as a paper!” Carlos said enthused. “I’m really sure that if Michael and Luc can use them to localise a few more events it is going to really help everyone.”

Carlos stopped as if trying to remember something. “Oh yes, there was one other little thing that came out of the management meeting last Tuesday. The WOW team is finally going to be collaborating with Fermi-lab. Some guys are due to fly over in the next ten days I think. I’m not sure if it will affect your access to the data, but don't let it discourage you from looking at helping other departments.”

Michael smiled, “I think we all deserve a beer.”

* * *

Anne-Marie left her single room apartment in Chevry, and walked the short distance to the fire station to wait for Michael, who turned up in one of the staff rental cars he had booked out for the day. It was the usual tiny little white Fiat, and he had spent the first twenty minutes driving it around to get used to driving on the right-hand side of the road. Once he felt confident he made the short, incident free, six kilometre drive to where Anne-Marie was waiting.

He saw her waiting in a slightly darker floral dress, with light training shoes and a slightly different hat. She carried a very small backpack, similar to what Charlie had but on closer inspection just a stylish copy. He himself was a lot more casual, wearing faded jeans, a retro white ‘The Smiths’ tee-shirt, and some worn Reebok trainers. She jumped in.

“Hi Michael, you do realise the cable car is only a kilometre away from here, we could have walked?” She said, hugging him awkwardly across the transmission.

“Yes but it is uphill on a twisty country road and it is another very hot day, plus there is a lot of walking at the top.”

“I’m not the fittest person, I really hope I don’t slow you down.”

“Don't be silly, if we get tired we stop, and we are not walking too far, it is just a picnic not a hike.” Michael pointed his hand over his shoulder to the small cooler bag on the back seat. “Bread, cheese, wine, some grapes and strawberries and a couple of sodas and some water.” He had stocked up this morning at the local supermarket, hoping he got everything right, he had no real idea of the French norm for a picnic.

“You do know there is a restaurant at the top where we can get some food,” she teased.

“We may find somewhere with a nicer ambiance to sit, I came prepared.”

She nodded, “and also please tell me how do you feel for your first time driving on the correct side of the road?”

He gave her a funny look and shook his head. “Like you say Anne-Marie, it is a kilometre away and I am sure you won’t be a..” He stopped, then did a literal translation of ‘back seat driver’ from English to French which she laughed at, then corrected him.

She had checked the cable car’s timetable beforehand, as it had some pretty sporadic operating hours, and knew the last car came down at 5PM. Neither the road, nor the cable car was busy, the car park being only half-full. He eased the small car carefully between two larger cars, it looked positively tiny.

They had a cab to themselves going up. Michael asked Anne-Marie if she could ski, as he looked at his guide book and saw it also had a small ski field at the top, one his decidedly amateur skiing ability might be able to handle. Anne-Marie had never skied.

“You have so much to live for Anne-Marie, this winter we will both have lessons and I bet you will love it.”

They had a hot chocolate in the mountain cafe, sitting on the mostly empty wooden tables looking out over the view to lake Geneva in the distance with the large mountain ranges either side. They didn't really walk too far from there, finding a spot sheltered from the sun, with a slightly more impressive view.

“I guess this will be fine for our picnic.” Michael got out a blanket and put two cushions down and placed two glasses, the cokes, the wines, cheese and bread.

“Impressive,” she smiled, sitting on the other cushion, the one with the best view.

“I have saved the impressive part for now.” He dug into his pack and pulled out his laptop. Anne-Marie gave him a funny look but said nothing.”

He opened the laptop, pressed a few keys and passed it to her. She looked at the screen, her eyes widening as she read.

“These are accounts of the incident in Geneva university for the hours following the large event, I thought we could read them together.”

“Oh there are quite a few Michael, I thought I had the only one.”

“No, it was quite the talk of the day apparently, we matched thirty one in total, in no particular order.”

The two sat there, Anne-Marie would read an account and they would then discuss it a little, trying to get the feeling for what happened that day twelve years earlier.

“Mrs Schneider, cancelled the lecture near the end and rushed out of the class. The bursar let us know the lecture would be rescheduled as she had to take her daughter Charlie home..”

There was that deja vu again, just for a fraction of a second, a thought entering Anne-Marie's head, then disappearing and gone before she could recall anything.

“Charlie!” She repeated louder and directly at Michael. “You don't really think that could be your Charlie do you?”

Michael was thinking exactly the same thing, and had stopped halfway through spreading his cheese on his bread the second her name was mentioned. Anne-Marie re-read the account fully.

“Mrs Schneider, cancelled the lecture near the end and rushed out of the class. The bursar let us know the lecture would be rescheduled as she had to take her daughter Charlie home as she was feeling sick. Someone said they heard the girl, who was only twelve, screaming for her mother at the time and she may have been the worst affected.”
