Anne-Marie scanned ahead, nothing else about the girl, or the mother, “unfortunately we don't have internet up here to do a search..”

Michael was pondering, he had completely zoned out because Anne-Marie had noticed he was talking to himself in English.

“Michael!” Anne-Marie said over his mumbling, “it may not be the same woman you met, Charlie is quite a common name and many girls shorten their name to it.. Also you are mumbling in English!”

She said the last part in English to get his attention.

“Sorry Anne-Marie, you are right again, hey let's carry on reading the rest of the accounts.”

Anne-Marie did, and they found two other accounts, from it they got that Mrs Schneider was a very famous high court judge who gave guest lectures to the university, and that the effects for everyone else were relatively minor.

“Let us speculate a little,” said Anne-Marie, “let's say this Charlie is the same one you met in Montreux, how old did you say she was?”

“I don't know but mid twenties would be about right, and that fits this twelve year old child. And the daughter of a high court judge would be well educated and rich I guess.”

“OK, so she was affected most by the event..” She hesitated then added quickly, “and she may be the best candidate to interview. Also maybe she has had other incidents coinciding with events, oh wow, wouldn't that be amazing.”

Michael laughed, “Now look who is getting carried away with herself Anne-Marie!”

“It's a working theory, and theories can be tested,” she said, smiling at him and taking a small sip of wine. She moved the laptop to the side and moved a tiny bit closer to him. “Thank you Michael, you really know how to show a mathematician a good time.” She brought her glass up to his until it tinkled as they touched.

* * *

With Anne-Marie's help, Michael had gotten a lot more confident. He was a good learner and quick to take every point in his learning to heart. He had already decided that his first email to Charlie would be written in French, he really did not know what to say, so he kept the email short, simple and friendly. After deleting about twenty previous versions he finally wrote.

*Hey Charlie,*

*My job is going well, meeting lots of new friends and French improving in leaps and bounds, though it’s obviously a long tail to become fluent. I would love to meet again sometime for a coffee or maybe dinner. I am getting a taste for French food. My shout.*

*Michael.*

He looked at it over a dozen times and pressed send. He then went immediately to his sent folder and re-read it. Damn! Dinner! Why did he mention dinner, it sounded far too pushy, oh god, she was going to think he was desperate! And the comment about the long tail, what if she did not understand it? Great, he had probably blown his only chance, she was just going to delete it. Maybe he left sending it too long, maybe she was expecting him to email her straight after his course in Montreux.

He started penning a new email then stopped. Jesus Michael, get a grip on yourself! You met a nice woman and talked to her for thirty minutes and you’re all ready to marry her! She was just a friendly encounter and she probably already has a partner! What if it went to spam, he had used his old gmail account and not his work one as he did not want it to sound too official. She was never going to reply!

Her reply popped in two minutes later and in French.

*Hi Michael,*

*Good to hear from you and glad to hear everything is going well. I'm out of the country for the next few days, but would love to catch up over dinner or coffee when I return. I will get in touch.*

*Ciao Charlie.*

He read it, re-read it, then got the computer to translate it to English, just in case there was some nuance he wasn't aware of. He wondered again why he was going to so much effort for this, and it really came down to the unanswered questions in his notebook and the new unanswered question if she was the girl at the university event all those years ago. Charlie held mysteries, and to Michael that made her even more interesting.

* * *

“If this girl is the same Charlie I met in Montreux then you would think there would be a little more information on her,” said Michael to Anne-Marie and scratching his head. “There does not seem to be much about her anywhere on the Internet..”

“Oh wait,” said Anne-Marie. “This is her I think.”

She turned her screen to him, and to a picture from a national newspaper a year before, from a charity event the judge Mrs Schneider attended. Michael slid his chair closer to examine the picture and the brown haired woman next to the judge.

“Oh my, that is definitely her. So she was the girl in the university, what is the coincidence?”

Anne-Marie was thinking the exact same thing, but mathematically there was no way to make such a calculation, except.. Some weird thoughts and maths popped into her head, before just as quickly fading.

“She is very beautiful,” she said a little less enthusiastically, “and it says here that her uncle, Count Joerg Bergmann, is the second richest person in Switzerland! I would have been so scared to meet someone like that!”

“She was nothing like that, Anne-Marie,” he gave her his best and nicest smile, realising how bad he was feeling about discussing this other woman with her. He waited for her to reciprocate the smile, then continued.

“Yes you could tell immediately she was someone with money, but she came across totally different, very approachable, and very friendly. And it looks like she is also quite reclusive. I would expect somebody with her looks and money to be on the front cover of every socialite magazine and on hundreds of social media posts, yet all we have is this one formal occasion. I guess we shouldn't jump to any assumptions.”

“It says her name is Charlene and she would be the same age as me, twenty four.” Anne-Marie searched a bit more on her name and came up with a couple more formal pictures at grand occasions, but nothing relating to social media.

“It doesn't make sense someone like her not having a social media profile of some kind, especially in this day and age,” said Michael, quickly realising that Anne-Marie did not have one either.

“I should ask her about that day at the university, but the post did say she was quite upset and she was only twelve at the time, maybe I will just hint about it and see if she volunteers anything.”

“That might be best,” said Anne-Marie as she went back to her main screen, turning it back so she could work on it.

Michael fiddled with his pencil obviously deep in thought, looking at the ceiling ducting and listening to the strange but kind of intoxicating synthesiser instrumental from Anne-Marie's playlist, and wanting to move the subject off Charlie in case it was upsetting her.

“What's this one?” He said to her, “I’m sure I’ve heard it before somewhere.”

“It is a French composer from the seventies, Jean-Michelle Jarre, very famous.”

“Never heard of him, but I could get used to it.” He moved his gaze from the ceiling to Anne-Marie who was looking at him curiously.

“Never heard of him! You are joking!”

“What can I say, I guess I still have a lot to learn, and I’m glad you are here to teach me.”

The comment got the intended smile from her, he wondered if she knew how much she was coming to mean to him. Luc walked in, dropping a metal enclosure on his electronics workbench with a clang and breaking the moment for both of them.

The enclosure was the size of a small shoe box, with screw holes at one end and a funny round connector at the other.

“The tech says this has a bad connection, but he has a dental appointment and won’t be in until tomorrow so I thought I’d see if I could repair it for him.”

“What is it?” Said Michael curiously.

“One of the WOW detectors,” said Luc, examining every aspect of the box.

“I never really asked, but how do the detectors work?” Said Michael.

“Amazingly simple really,” said Luc, “sealed light proof metal box, that also acts as a faraday shield with some sort of pressurised gas, or was it a vacuum.. And several very sensitive arrays of optically sensitive transistors, which react to photons, I think, or was it electrons!”

He turned it around in his hands as if it helped the explanation, “These transistors feed through a very high gain amplifier to the collator system. They have a thousand and twenty four of these boxes forming a detector block and they look for annihilation events which may indicate it has been hit by a neutrino or electron or something else that has appeared out of the quantum vacuum.” He looked at the device for a second as if deep in thought.

“..Or something like that, well that is how Jez sort of explained it.”

Michael could see Anne-Marie trying not to laugh at Luc’s terrible, and probably totally wrong explanation.

“Clear as mud.” Michael said after Luc had finished. “So those guys have three of these detector blocks spaced evenly around the twenty-seven kilometre large hadron collider ring?”

“Yes, there is a lot of science, engineering and software behind it though,” replied Luc, as he began undoing the screws on the case..

The phone call came out of the blue. It had been so long since Michael had actually taken a real telephone call on his cell phone, that he at first didn’t know what it was. He fumbled the phone out of his pocket, and spent an extra few seconds finding the actual call answer button on the vast and busy screen. He just assumed it was his mother.

“Hi Mum!”

“Well, that’s a first!” A young woman's voice on the other end of the line laughed. “It’s Charlie here, remember? You know Montreux? Your email yesterday?”

She had totally caught him off guard.

“Oh Charlie, god yes. Er sorry!” Bloody hell, get it together Michael! “Yes, it’s really great to hear from you, though I am not used to anybody ever actually calling me up, except for my mother..”

He thought then added, “Oh and I am at work so we are really supposed to take calls in English, here let me get somewhere private.” He walked over to the kitchen wondering how she even got his number?

“Good,” she said without waiting, “I was just calling to check if you were free this Sunday for dinner? There is this delightful fondue restaurant I know which makes the most wonderful, extremely unhealthy food and where the view is to die for, even on a bad day.”

Wow, the woman had immediately taken control again, but what could he say? At least he could change to French now he was away from the others.

“Well I only know a few people here, and my diary is pretty empty for Sunday evening, so meeting you again would be delightful..”

“Oh Michael, you speak French now,” she replied in English. “But if work expects you to speak English we do not want to get you in any trouble, and we have plenty of time to get to know each other in French. I will make a booking for 6 p.m. and email you the details. Oh and the restaurant is a little bit snooty, so dress to impress.”

“Oh! Er, how formal are we talking? I don't have a tie..”

“No just a collar, shoes, no jeans, you can always say you’re with me if there are any problems. I look forward to seeing you, then, ciao.”

“Bye!” He added just before she hung up. A fondue, gosh, that all sounded a bit posh, and he had to dress up, damn he was always on the back foot with this woman.

Both Luc and Anne-Marie were watching him from about ten metres away. “Ah, the mystery woman has finally got in touch!” Said Luc loudly, as Michael walked back towards them.

Michael glanced at Anne-Marie, who didn't look jealous at all. He and her were having dinner tonight, nothing special, just pizza after work at six o'clock in the local restaurant, but he suddenly now felt very guilty for talking to Charlie.

“It’s not like that, Luc.. in fact I was thinking of asking Anne-Marie to come along and meet Charlie, after all she does want to meet her and we will be talking in French.” The words just came out of his mouth without properly thinking and he was already thinking how to back-pedal with either Charlie or Anne-Marie, when Anne-Marie spoke.

“Oh don't be silly Michael,” she said. “Charlie is your friend, and your little project, it would not be nice for you to bring another friend along uninvited. But I would like to meet her sometime if she is as interesting as you say she is.”

“Me too,” said Luc, “no woman is that interesting..” He looked at Anne-Marie, “Present company excepted of course.”

Michael laughed, “Luc, you really should engage your brain before you put your mouth in gear!”

Both of them looked at him funny for a few seconds, looked at each other, then both laughed simultaneously.

* * *

Michael looked at the posh looking restaurant that the taxi had dropped him off at. He had made a special visit to town on the Saturday with Anne-Marie to buy some new trousers and shirt and they had made a day of it, having lunch and window shopping on millionaires row, where a suit or a dress was usually more than a month’s pay.

He knew he had found a soul-mate in Anne-Marie, and he found himself wanting more but being too afraid to go the next step for fear of scaring her off.

He looked around at the people in the busy tourist street and considered himself presentable for anyone, or any occasion. Michael had considered arriving a little early, then a little late, but his British upbringing made him walk up to the maitre-d at exactly six o’clock on the dot.

The maitre-d gave him a casual look, and not finding anything on first inspection asked him if he had a booking, he said yes but felt a little silly saying it was under Charlie.

“Under the name Charlie,” he said as dignified as he could.

The man showed a hint of recognition but held it well contained behind his aloof and scripted attitude. He guided Michael to a small, empty table set out for two people and set apart from others.

“Sir. Mademoiselle Charlie phoned us five minutes ago to let us know she is running about ten minutes late, and for you to order a suitable starter for the two of you.”

He handed him the menu, all in French, and in some weird thin cursive font almost impossible to read.

“Thank you, can I please order a small Heineken beer and I will peruse the menu before I order.”

Michael couldn’t detect anything wrong with the French he had used but the man looked a little confused. A few seconds later he nodded politely and walked off, putting his hand up and clicking his fingers to a waiter as he walked past. He whispered something in the waiter's ear, pointed at Michael’s table and was back at his dark mahogany podium a few seconds later.

Michael finally felt like he had the upper hand if she was running late and needed to apologise, it would at least mean he was not on the back foot immediately, and it also gave him a bit more time to think of what to talk about. He had never felt this apprehensive in his life, not during exams nor even when he was pulled over for a speeding ticket that time.

That was why he liked Anne-Marie so much, she was so different, beautiful, charming, easy going and amazingly intelligent, she also did not make him apprehensive in any way. But Charlie was also all of that, and in buckets, she was also mysterious, rich, sophisticated, and, and, well he did not know what. Something about her still made him apprehensive, like a man walking a tightrope without a safety net, and with lions below. The women were chalk and cheese but also similar, he was so happy both were in his life.

“Michael, I’m so sorry, I decided to drive myself here but traffic was a bit heavier than I anticipated.”

Charlie looked stunning, and a lot of the heads, not just those of the males, had turned as she waltzed in. Her pink dress, black high-heeled shoes, hat and coat all looked like they had been made personally for her. The fact she had managed to move that quickly in high heels was not even the surprising part, it was the fact she did it with such class and style that was impressive. The maitre-d hurried up behind her and then caught her thin black coat, just as she let it go into his hand. Michael blinked, the whole entrance could not have been choreographed better if it was a Royal ballet performance. As the maitre-d walked off with her coat, another man rushed up with a coat hanger and took it off him. Charlie walked up to Michael and kissed both cheeks.

She said something quickly to the maitre-d Michael did not understand, it wasn't French, maybe Italian, he wasn't sure but some of the words sounded a little familiar, like wine and stop drink.

She looked at Michael with her usual smile, and continued in clearer French. “Oh I’m sorry, my bad I specially reserved a white wine produced at my cousins winery. The Maitre-d was a little confused as you ordered a separate drink. I hope you don't mind me being presumptuous but I told them to cancel your beer as it would spoil the bouquet of the wine. Oh and do not feel bad, I know the maitre-d he is from the north of Italy, it is a local dialect, but many words are similar to the French.”

“You were only a few minutes late Charlie, you must drive fast.”

She laughed, “Oh no! Seriously Michael, I’m the worst driver ever, I drive at the speed limit everywhere and follow all the rules; it totally confuses the French and Italian sense of machismo but what are they going to say to a little woman.”

Simon had asked her to take a different route than the normal one, and he would now probably be somewhere close. But like with Carl she had given up trying to second-guess him when it came to security. The new route had however made her a bit late.

The waiter arrived with an ice bucket and held out a bottle with both hands with the label facing Charlie. In this game Michael was simply a spectator. She nodded and the waiter uncorked the bottle and poured a small taste which she sniffed, tasted, and approved with a nod.

“Chasselas is the main variety grown in the lake region. This Chasselas is from one of the family vineyards my cousin manages in the Lavaux region, it is a very boutique winery and a bit expensive. But while it is very drinkable, and nobody would ever say a bad word about it, between you and me this vintage was not one of Eliot’s best. Anyway, cheers!”

They touched glasses and Michael had a small sip, it tasted quite pleasant but he really hadn’t a clue as he was more a beer drinker, still he could get used to it, especially in this company.

“It’s actually really nice,” he said, “though I think it is the person I am drinking it with that makes it so special. You know, you were probably the first person I got to know here, and that actually caused a real problem for me. For at least three weeks, nobody could live up to the high bar you set, and everyone I told about you, believed you a figment of my imagination.”

“For only three weeks hah!” She shrugged, “I must be losing my touch if it only lasted that long. I can only guess you have found someone special since then, I hope it doesn't preclude us being friends though, and whoever she is, she has very good taste, and is a very good teacher, as I must say I am very impressed with how far you have come.”

God how did the woman do it he thought as he looked at her taking another dainty sip from the glass.

“Well you were a hard act to follow. You know it was weird how much I obsessed over this moment for the last five weeks. I kept a little notebook of our first meeting, trying to understand what exactly had happened. It was like I had just had coffee with a street magician, everything you did and said was like magic. I felt like a man paddling in a lake and having a speedboat run over me.”

Charlie’s smile lessened a little, enough that Michael noticed. He was struggling a little with the language.

“Say it in English Michael,” she said in English, “There is no point in muddling your head trying to find words you do not know yet. In time they will come, and remember, this is supposed to be your evening too, you can change back when you are comfortable.”

“Sorry,” I guess I am struggling a bit. Anne-Marie has been great at pushing my vocabulary and grammar, but I guess I am not quite ready for the big time. I think I may have made it sound worse than it is, what I meant to say was you were everything I had grown up thinking a perfect woman could be, you were Princess Diana, Scarlet Johanson, Lara Croft and Wonder Woman, all rolled into this amazingly beautiful package, and you helped me much more than you ever know. Learning French became such an obsession because you showed me that the high bar I had set for my perfect woman was way too low, and I could never be happy again with somebody who does not push me to be better.”

He laughed, “This isn't even coming out right in English!”

Her smile returned. “Well I am disappointed you missed Audrey Hepburn from your list, but I think I know what you are trying to say. I am really flattered you have put me on such a high pedestal, and I do feel a little bad I put you in that space.”

Her eyes twinkled as she unconsciously moved her finger around the glass. “I did actually quite enjoy our little rendezvous by the lakeside, and I have really been looking forward to seeing you again and hearing your experiences. You know I remember last time we met you hardly got a word in edgeways, I totally bullied you into talking French when it was probably your first day, then I was so busy doing my little Sherlock Holmes thing on you I was almost late getting home.”

“Dinner and this evening is my little way of saying I am sorry for all that, finding out a little bit more about you, your new job, your new friends and what you think of our little town. Things I would have done in English if I had more time at our first meeting and hadn't started teasing you..”

“Oh Charlie,” he interrupted, “don't you see you made me want to stay, made me want to learn, and seriously, only you could make Geneva sound like a sleepy mountain village.”

He noticed all the things he should have noticed on their first meeting as he talked, his eyes moved over her face, her eyes, her lips and neck, the little gold watch that seemed too small for anything, the lack of jewellery and how little makeup she wore. Skin, hair, teeth, nails, nothing seemed flawed and he re-ran his eyes around her body as subtly as he could trying to find something, anything, but could not.

Charlie followed his thoughts as well as his eyes and his voice, men could indeed multitask if they wanted to! She caught the comparisons to Anne-Marie, and the dilemma he was going through as he felt it was impossible as both women were amazing, stimulated different emotions and filled different roles in his life. She pushed the narrative as he paused.

“So, come on Michael,” she said, returning to French, “you have told me nothing yet of your story here.”

His thoughts flickered between English and French for a while as he got back onto track, settling on English.

“Well as far as it went, the first weeks in Montreux, which if you remember I timed for the Jazz festival, were so intense I found myself studying or sleeping instead of watching concerts. By the second week I may not have been thinking in French, but I was certainly having nightmares in it.”

She smiled at his joke.

“The job I eventually got at CERN was, well, predictable. We are helping a little on one interesting project, but the management wanted it kept a little quiet so I really can't say much, which you should be grateful for as I don't want to spoil the evening with boring techno babble.”

“Oh so there is an area 51 in CERN after all, the rumours about the anti-matter being for a flying saucer are true then!” She said in fake surprise, then continued normally.

“Seriously Michael, I think you will find I can swim with the big fish in the big pool when it comes to techno-babble, you do not get very far in business these days without knowing something about it. Anyway I am happy to defer to your better judgement and we won't talk about science stuff this evening, but don't tell me you can't talk about your new friends, and maybe a special one in particular?”

They were interrupted when a small salad starter arrived on a silver cart, the waiter graciously setting everything up and disappearing as efficiently as he arrived.

“Oh, I remember I was supposed to order a starter, did you do this?”

“Yes it was a teeny-weeny Italian word slipped into the sentence, you probably missed.” Charlie repeated the sentence, then did it slowly with a small French translation after each part.”

“Wow, it's amazing you can cram so much information into two seconds of talk.”

“Oh a real Italian could probably say it quicker just with hand gestures,” she laughed. “So come on Michael you were going to tell me about your new friends.”

Charlie listened intently, content in letting Michael talk, the side thoughts she had been listening to also intrigued her, the work was frankly a bit boring, but the deep emotions he was feeling for this strange girl was so cute she could hug him. The girl fascinated her more than it should have, each time he or she moved from her, the thoughts just flipped back into his head. She was obviously some sort of genius, and from what Michael was thinking she only came out of her shell when he arrived. For some strange reason she found herself wanting to meet her too.

They were well into the fondue an hour later. Michael had dominated the conversation moving back to French somewhere midway. He now seemed to have exhausted the topics of work, friends and Switzerland, and had moved on to his history, family, university and reason for coming here. He really was a nice chap and she was glad he was warming to her as a friend and not looking at her as a suitor.

Charlie opened up a little to him about her family and her life, skipping over work, the billion dollar businesses, the castles, the vineyards and a few other distractions and more concentrating on the friends she had, the books she read and the music, films and shows she liked, tempering back her recall ability to sound more normal.

“I hope you are not going to drive home,” he said as he looked at the empty wine bottle, “there must have also been at least another bottle of wine in that fondue as I am feeling quite light headed.”

“Oh gosh no, my mothers driver Simon is picking me up. I did tell him eight, but he won't mind a little wait, he’s a bit scary, but a real dear. His wife will pick my car up tomorrow, unfortunately I have to go to Brussels for business.”

“How long are you away this time?

“Oh only a few days, I will be back for my uncle Joerg’s sixtieth birthday on Saturday, he is throwing the usual big party at his house starting mid-afternoon and going through the evening. Say why don't you and Anne-Marie come? I would really love to see her and of course you. It will probably be quite an international crowd, also after tonight's performance I am happy to say you passed and I will release you of your obligation to only speak French to me. But if you want my advice I think you should keep up the good work, I expect by this time next year you will be fluent.”

“Are you sure your uncle wont mind, it does sound like an imposition?”

“God no Michael, quite the opposite, there will be about three hundred guests there, Joerg will hardly know anyone. I usually use it as an excuse to meet my family, and my mother and grandfather's old friends, many of whom I grew up knowing. I sometimes bring a couple of guests, but it’s usually a bit too boring for my girlfriends as they are used to younger people and excitement; old money walking around an old castle is generally not their scene. It is also the only time of the year the castle is open, and it will be great to show you both the history of the place.”

She looked him up and down, “again you have to make a little effort with your appearances, but what you are wearing would be fine. Let me know by email and I will get Alice to arrange your transport.”

Michael smiled, “I won't be offended that you think we would be more suitable company than your girlfriends for the boring party, but I must admit being shown around some old castle by you does actually sound quite a nice way to pass an afternoon.”

“Michael, I did not say you were boring!” She said in mock anger. “It’s just you and Anne-Marie might appreciate it more than most. You know, see how the other half live and realise how much nicer you really are than them.”

“Just joking,” he smiled, “I cannot answer for Anne-Marie, but I am definitely free. I will talk to her tomorrow and let you know by email. I know she is keen to meet you, but I may have over-sold you so you may need to play down the jet-setting princess image.”

“The..”

“No, hear me out,” he interrupted, touching her arm gently across the table. “Anne-Marie is a little special to me, from all accounts up until I came she was super-shy and very much an introvert. I however have not really seen that side of her, either it is only me she behaves normal around, or something changed with her since I arrived. From everything I know about her, I expect she will open up to you, but this experience will be well outside any known Venn-diagram for her.”

Charlie heard the words and saw the underlying processing, whatever Michael had for Anne-Marie was much deeper than he said, even his mind had trouble getting the words to describe her to Charlie. And what was this question Anne-Marie wanted to ask that briefly surfaced in Michael’s thoughts?

Oh dear, the incident at the university! Charlie wanted to dig into it further like she normally did, but she was not going to do that with Michael. Nevertheless Charlie was totally intrigued. This talk about going from introvert to a normal person in a few days sounded a little implausible, maybe there was something more about Michael or Anne-Marie.

It was Charlie’s turn to touch his arm. “Please don’t worry Michael, she sounds like a very special person to you and I am very much looking forward to meeting her. But thank you for giving me a little of her history, if she does come I will keep a close eye on her and everything. I promise.”

She sensed Michael's relief that he had told her, and he really did want Anne-Marie to like her. The man was so nice it was a little unbelievable. She could certainly see how someone like Anne-Marie would come out of her shell with him.

There was no rush for anything that evening. Fondue was a food best eaten slowly, the longer you waited, the richer and tastier the sauce became, though Michael got the feeling he was doing most of the eating and drinking. The two people took their time to get to know each other a little more, sharing an aperitif and finally a coffee. The restaurant was mostly empty by the time they had finished and as they were getting up Michael said he would email tomorrow letting Charlie know if Anne-Marie was coming.

The Maitre-d appeared with Charlie's coat the second her chair moved, and he held it there while she put it on. Up to now there had been no talk of the bill but he guessed in a restaurant like this, people like Charlie probably had other arrangements. He really wanted to pay for it, doing so felt right, but he was now worrying if he had enough money in his checking account. He guessed even posh restaurants accepted credit cards.

“Thank you for a wonderful evening Michael,” she said genuinely, “I really hope I will see both of you on Saturday afternoon. Oh and don’t worry about the bill, my uncle has an account at the restaurant.”

She sniggered and in an exaggerated whisper said in English, “don't worry, he’s loaded and won’t even notice!”

He walked her into the street where a large black Mercedes was waiting in the valet area, a well muscled older black man in a dark suit was standing by the open rear passenger door. Charlie stopped to kiss both Michael's cheeks and waved as she went over to the car.

He watched as she walked up to the man, stood on her tiptoes and gave him a friendly kiss on the cheek. She said something Michael couldn't hear, then the big man tutted, walked forward to the front passenger door and opened it for her, shaking his head as she got in.

* * *

The large set man stopped at the picture of Gerty again, the one in her high court judge robes, staring at the woman for a long time. It was a picture he could not get out of his mind, one he could not forget. It was the same woman who had put his father away for multiple homicides twenty eight years earlier.

Damon had learnt all the tricks of the trade from his father and had even accompanied him on several of the assassinations as a thirteen year old boy. His father and mother had split up when Damon was twelve, his father fleeing Dublin with his boy after his wife, Damon’s mother was found dead and he was accused of the murder.

The father, over the course of that year had convinced the young boy that it was another guy who did it, he told Damon he worked as a secret agent for the American CIA and had been tasked to kill the man who was an assassin. He had left the man alive and the killing of Damon’s mother, and the framing of him, was the man’s revenge. Damon was very impressionable and had loved and emulated the tough guy persona his father had. It reinforced all his biases, and his father maximised this to its fullest.

Damon became an errand boy for his father, thinking he was helping a secret agent, when all he was really doing was helping his father who was a contract killer who would work for the highest price. The two toured Europe on false passports working for the highest bidders. Damon was taught how to use weapons, fight, and hide from the authorities and the many other people who wanted revenge on his father. Damon killed his first man at fourteen.

When his father and Damon were caught, after the botched assassination of a Saudi sheikh in Zurich, his father was tried and sentenced to multiple homicides, including his mothers. Damon, who was still only fourteen at the time, was considered a minor and sent back to Ireland under the care of the state. As Damon had never been called as a witness and stood well back from the judge's seat, Gerty never once caught the fact he was an accomplice, nor the hatred he had of her in his mind.

Damon was of course now known as The Priest, an agent and hitman for The ElderNacht. He was thumbing again through the documents he had as he waited impatiently in his cheap hotel.

By all accounts The ElderNacht still had next to nothing from the bugs he had planted and were getting very impatient. There was also something not quite right about this whole government department bullshit, just three people in an almost empty prestigious down-town office, it felt like a front for something, and it made him a little more wary than usual.

It was the mention of the family vault however that held his interest. That first day when he had set off the office fire alarms, to place the bugs he had also photographed a bunch of documents. The documents, which he translated on the internet, talked about five hundred thousand euros in transferable bearer bonds that were to be placed there in escrow for a client. The document also said this small vault was located in some library room in her house. It felt like time was running out to redeem the mission, but also a plan was forming in his mind, one where he could finally retire from The ElderNacht with a few million euros bonus, and maybe even get a little payback on the judge for what happened to his father.

* * *

Ethan Curran was furious. The man called Crane he had detained in San Francisco was now dead too.

Crane had locked up, refusing to say anything except to demand his lawyer and his rights, not realising as a possible terrorist, he had none. He was moved to a secret Langley detention facility where he had been questioned twice getting precisely nothing. Then this morning he was dead, they didn't even know how he had died, and wouldn’t, until the autopsy was completed.

So they again had nothing. No real idea who the suspect was, and no information on who inside of the CIA was Crane’s controller, or who faked the orders on the system. It wasn't just Curran who was unhappy, the director of the CIA was fuming too.

“We need to find out just what the fuck is going on Ethan.” He said in a resigned and slightly exhausted voice.

“I’ve been in this job two months, and I come straight into some sycophantic Jason Bourne novel, with murdered agents, attempted murders of diplomats, cover ups, and a bunch of narcissists, all of who seem to be looking after themselves more than the country. To be frank Ethan I have no clue which of my people I can trust and which I can’t.”

Ethan had known the new director, when they were on more equal footing and the director was working in a senior position at the Justice Department.

“I don't think the rot is endemic, sir,” he said. “I have received good cooperation from some of your departments and the Liaison officer you appointed seems very efficient. My big worry is that this is not over by a long way. Firstly you have someone high up who has enough access to the CIA systems to fake an order from Chambers. She could not have done it herself as she was physically over three hundred miles from the terminal that issued the order in Langley at the time it was issued.”

“That same person probably killed Crane and is likely working for The ElderNacht, an organisation we still have no real clue what their agenda is, and who are untouchable while they are sheltered by another state.”

“Thirdly we still have this Swiss negotiator, who not only solved the Munich problem but who has given us a lot more information than we had before on an organisation that is happy to fund terrorists and carry out murders inside the USA.”

“You are not going to like what I suggest Daniel, but this negotiator is now a target and will probably be more invested in clearing up this problem than anybody. I suggest we pool our resources to get to the bottom of this before we find we have missed another nine-eleven.”

At the words nine-eleven the director looked sharply at Ethan, but he continued.

“She doesn't have to be let in on any US secrets Daniel, in fact I suggest she is kept as far away from the CIA and America as possible as somebody here has already ordered her dead. So it will just be me and her, and whatever resources you can give me.”

“I also suggest we do this officially and quietly. My role in Interpol is much more suitable for an investigation like this, and does not expose your organisation. I will give you regular reports and if and when we get enough information to act I will pass the ball back to you. Of course it will mean you will need to call in another favour from my Director in the liaison office.”

The director seemed relieved, agreeing to Ethan's strategy and the few extra favours he would need to begin.

* * *

Charlie was on the train back from Brussels and deep in thought after reading the Interpol alert she was copied on. Mendez’s murder along with the additional research she had dug up on The ElderNacht was enough to make her worried.

The additional information she had given the CIA contained details on some really bad people, a more secret upper echelon with an unknown goal, and a bunch of legitimate looking operations which were anything but legitimate. Most of them centred around mining in third-world countries, counterfeiting pharmaceuticals and other products. But why give money to terrorists, what did The ElderNacht get out of this from their end of the deal?

The last straw was the thoughts she extracted from the man in the airport. She, her mother and maybe the people around her might be in danger, and she needed to get to the bottom of it.

That was when she picked up Alice’s secure email about the request for a meeting with an American Interpol agent. She opened the signal app on her phone and patched it through to the Interpol secure system to look him up.

“Ethan Curran,” he answered promptly, seeing the call had come in on the Interpol secure messaging system. Where the account name usually appeared though, just said the number 957659.

“Mr Curran, this is Charlie, I believe you wanted to talk to me regarding the death of an American agent in Munich.”

Ethan was caught on the back foot a little, especially the fact she was coming in from an unlisted account on the Interpol secure system. Her voice was clear in a slightly British accented English, it was not what he was expecting.

“Er, yes miss.. Er Charlie. Our government is about to make this all official but I believe we have a common problem we need to solve and I was hoping I could meet you and pool our resources.”

“You are correct. Mr Curran, or do you mind if I call you Ethan since we will be working together?”

“Ethan is fine, if I can call you Charlie. Anyway I am tying up what little threads we have here and I have booked a flight to Geneva for next Wednesday..”

“Next Wednesday is fine Ethan. You can use this number to directly message or phone me, it is an Interpol case number I have just created, and is valid while I need it. I won't presume to know your agenda but contact me before then and as soon as you have an itinerary."

“I look forward to seeing you then Ethan.”

“And I you Charlie. And please take care until then, these are not nice people we are playing with.”

And with that ominous ending he hung up the phone. Charlie sighed, time to let Carl and Simon know what was happening.

* * *

Charlie sat with Gerty, Simon and Carl in the lounge of the house. She had filled them in on the case, the murder of the people in Munich and the warning from the Interpol agent that she may also be a target. Gerty was of course worried, immediately wanting to go thermonuclear. Charlie calmed her down.

“We need to do this logically Mama, it is not a given, we are on home ground and they likely know very little about us. The fact they are bugging us, logically says they don't know enough and need to get a bit more information first.”

“We don’t really have the resources to fully protect you guys.” Carl said, looking at Simon, “It feels like whoever is watching us has all the advantages at the moment. Simon has notified the FIS and they say they have tasked some additional resources to check those bugs out. They also said they will assign somebody for the next two weeks to cover the Count’s house. That, along with all the extra security during the party should mean we are safe for a few weeks at least.”

Charlie nodded, concerned a little that Carl didn't seem to think much of the FIS guys. She knew Swiss secret services were no James Bond’s, or Jason Bourne's, but neither were any of the other secret services. In Charlie’s opinion, having a secret service nobody had even heard of, i.e. one that was actually secret, sounded a lot better to her. Carl set his standards pretty high.

“Did the Interpol agent give you any further information? I assume it was not the CIA bugging us then?”

“To tell the truth Simon, you never know with those guys.” Said Charlie, shrugging her shoulders. “The American agent said no, but he admits he wouldn't trust his own side to be fully open with him, and a lot of the CIA departments have their own agendas. I talked to Uncle Frank and he says that agent Ethan Curran is a good choice, and one of the good guys. From what I have heard of him and seen of his results I concur.”

“We should not change our life, Mama, but we should also randomise it a bit, go with what Carl and Simon suggest, and ensure one of them is with us at all times when we leave the chateau.”

“What about the trip to New Delhi?" said her mother.

“I have cancelled it, and I have asked Alice to put my itinerary on hold for the time being. I doubt anything will happen this week or next, as it is Joerg’s party and five of your friends are staying here so security will be massively increased.”

“OK, you should meet with this Interpol guy and both of us should work on the problem,” said Gerty, “hopefully it is just a storm in a teacup.”

Charlie agreed, but very much doubted her mothers optimism. Mother went off to her music room to play the piano, something she often did to get away from people's thoughts and clear her mind. Simon and Carl moved to the common room they used for security stuff, both looking deeply serious. And Charlie was left on her own in the living room on the sofa working on the threads in her mind.

* * *

The brief thoughts she had picked up from Michael regarding Anne-Marie would also not go away. Why was she interested in talking to Charlie about the university incident?

Charlie had looked up Doctor Anne-Marie Verne a little earlier. Michael's description of her was spot on, quiet, brilliant, shy and an extremely low profile. She was a mathematician, her only publication was a paper in data analysis and its relation to chaos theory. She gave up reading it after twenty minutes, admitting it was a little beyond her, and did not seem to contain anything relevant. And what about this area they were working at CERN? Ghost squad and special events were the two terms Michael had briefly thought about when he had mentioned it to her. Anton would of course answer her call if she made it, but there was nothing at all secret about CERN, for god sake it was full of scientists from about eighty different countries, it was the antithesis of a secret society.

Hmm, but maybe she could..

She pulled the phone from her pack, turned it on and dialled the number from memory. “Yes, Director Hertzwig please, just tell him it’s Charlie.”

* * *

“I’m not sure Michael,” said a worried Anne-Marie on Monday morning.

It seemed like her every-nightmare was about to come true, and even though she felt like she was improving, and had been in crowds of people, she had never interacted with anyone. But a party was different, would she be supposed to talk to other people? She could not do that, she could not talk to total strangers, lots of them. Anne-Marie felt terrible, she had kept this part of her persona a secret from Michael, really hoping it would always just be the two of them. The last few weeks had been amazing for Anne-Marie. She felt like she had two good friends now, Michael and Sophie, she even had a social life of sorts; She would go for walks, coffees and yoga with Sophie, then see Michael outside of work two or three times a week. But even though individually she was very comfortable, she was not even sure if she could sit at the same table with both of them together.

Michael looked at the anxiousness that was on Anne-Marie’s face, he hadn’t really expected she would say no, but the history from the other guys was making a little bit of sense now. He couldn't do this to her.

“We don’t need to go of course,” he said off-handedly, “I would be just as happy, the two of us going somewhere a little less exciting, it looks like a beautiful weekend for weather. I bet we could have a walk around the lake, or another cruise.”

Michael was so understanding but she couldn’t do it to him.

“No Michael!” She interrupted, “I really want to meet this Charlie, she does sound nice, it's just..it's just.. I'm really not good with talking to strangers or crowds.”

“Then let’s not go to the silly party and we can meet up somewhere quiet just the three of us over a coffee. She really does want to meet you Anne-Marie, and I really want her to like you as much as I like you.”

‘Like you as much as I like you’, did Michael really just say that?

“It will be just fine Anne-Marie,” he continued, “I will send Charlie an email saying we can’t make it..”

“Oh Michael, I'm such a mess!” She said, sounding a little upset. “I really want to do this with you but I am so afraid I will make a fool of myself and you won’t like me..”

“Don't say that Anne-Marie.” He touched her arm softly the way he had done with Charlie, and she felt a tingle, and her heart rate increase. “Not everyone likes crowds or socialising with strangers. It is all about the people you are with. You know I was quite shy once, but I learnt that socialising is mostly about just smiling and listening to others.”

“I have been to parties where the nicest person I met simply listened, smiled and said nothing. Yes there are always those who need to be the centre of attention, and sometimes deservedly as they are naturally witty and charming, but you don't have to be that person. It is also not about being popular or thinking of the next cool thing to say to make you popular, if you do like somebody then just saying hi, telling them who you are and listening might be all that is needed.”

He moved his hand off her arm and laughed. “You know Anne-Marie, we English have raised talking about the weather to an art-form, just so we have something to talk about, though we also have about a hundred words for rain! Just feel lucky you were born in a country where the sun comes out once in a while.”

Luc came over and they changed back to English.

“Hi Luc,” said Michael. “The weather was awesome over the weekend, don't you think?” Michael turned and winked at Anne-Marie who giggled.

“Hi guys!” He replied. “Well I for one am making the most of it, I really hate the cold and the snow here over winter.” He walked to his workstation and pressed the power button, he looked at his browser, “you’re out of luck though, rain is forecast next week so better make the most of this weekend.”

Anne-Marie smiled at Michael and tried unsuccessfully to wink causing him to smile even more.

Luc wasn't so bad, Anne-Marie thought, if everyone was like Luc, Julianne, Carlos, Sophie, Michael.. Now that she thought about it, most of the people she actually got to know were not that scary to talk to, well except maybe Jez. Maybe she could do this, Michael made it look so easy.

* * *

The Director was quite busy and had struggled to free an hour from his schedule so instead elected to talk to Charlie over a long lunch in the staff restaurant, anyone seeing him with the girl would probably have assumed she was his daughter.

“Charlie, it is really nice to see you again, I did try and clear my schedule for a proper meeting but with so little time and with you being unusually unspecific on what you wanted, I thought we might chat while I grab some lunch.”

“Lunch was a great idea Anton, I often forget to eat myself so this will kill two birds.”

They chatted in German, Anton did not even think about any other language as she was as fluent as him and this was a private conversation, at a quiet table he had specially reserved a little away from the rest; Plus the rules he usually handed down to his HR department rarely applied to him.

His big plate of sausages and cabbage looked positively massive compared to Charlie’s four pieces of sushi and reminded him he really should eat a bit more healthily.

“It is more of an informal query Anton, you are of course welcome to say no and we can still have lunch and chat, but I have a friend who works here who doesn't really know my background. I asked him what he was working on and he said it was to do with an event but he couldn’t really talk about it. Now it kind of surprised me as generally you guys are as open as a book when it comes to your research. And as you know I do have a passing interest in theoretical physics and I was wondering if you could tell me a little more. That’s really all it is. Everyone has their little secrets and I am happy to respect yours here if you wish, but I am planning on attending Professor Wolf's class in Oxford in three months and I would really like to be aware of everything.”

Anton was chewing some food and listening intently as she spoke. Like everyone, the narrative in his head was also churning through what she said, processing what and how to reply. She caught the references to the WOW project and his thoughts go through a little bit of risk analysis. She waited patiently, taking a bite of sushi and having a sip of her Perrier.

“Well, because it's you, I have no real problems, and it's not so much a secret Charlie as it is something we just don't like to talk about, dirty laundry if you like. I really don't know too much about the technical part of it beyond a basic overview I had from Professor Keffler who is head of the WOW team.”

He took another bite and paused for another short thought while chewing. “Basically we have some sort of random ‘event’ which has shown itself several times on several of the experiment's detectors. It's been happening since the first detector came online from what I can gather. We recently however found out that the Fermi-Lab neutrino detector was detecting a similar event.”

“The team is about thirty people, three scientists and a small bunch of mostly technicians and data analyst types from what I remember, and if you had asked me this a few weeks ago I would have struggled to remember any of it, but since Fermi-Lab have got in touch and agreed to pool resources it has gotten more prominent.

One of our other departments led by Professor Bonito thinks they can now identify the event better from the noise, enough that we may be able to trace the source a bit easier. It will basically cause a sigh of relief as we are looking at the next big expansion of the collider ring and this was one of the issues we were worried about.”

“You said neutrino detectors, but most of the detectors here are not looking for neutrinos. Do you know which particle it is affecting or how the event manifests itself?” Asked Charlie curiously.

“Not really Charlie, I have only taken a passing interest in this particular project and like I say we prefer to keep it a bit quiet, but I can certainly get a few hours of Professor Keffler’s time and maybe get him to show you around..”

“No, that's not really necessary just yet Anton, but if you had a technical summary I could read and maybe have the option to take you up on the tour that would be perfect. I would of course treat the document with the usual utmost discretion.”

“Well, I can certainly do that, if you walk back to my office with me after lunch I will let my technical manager know and he can dig it up for you.”

“Perfect, I can read it here if you are worried about security.”

“Ah yes, that wonderful mind of yours!” He smiled, “what I wouldn't give for one of those.”

“Oh I’m sure your mind is still as sharp as a pin Anton,” she laughed. and diverted the conversation on to other less interesting topics for the rest of their lunch.

* * *

She had dinner with her mother that night at home having spent two hours going through the paper Anton mentioned, then tracing through several other reference papers. She saw who the team were and where they fitted into the little puzzle, Michael and Anne-Marie were in some sort of adjacent team not directly involved, but Professor Bonito their lead scientist was quite happy to heap praise on his three staff for the breakthrough pinpointing the signal. He sounded like a nice boss.

A random quantum event, detectable by what was believed to be increased neutrino activity. She shook her head, that was obviously a guess based on the fact that the detectors would be shielded from normal interference. Neutrinos also explained Michael’s reference to the ghost team as the particles were one of those freaky things in physics that blew people's minds and detecting them was nigh on impossible.

Subatomic physics was one of those areas she did not like to get too deep into. Without ways to experimentally test a lot of the theories they were just that, theories. Sometimes not even that, sometimes more like best-guesses, and that did not sit well with her or her mother. She did however agree with experimentally testing, not because it might find the right answer, she doubted that, but that it might one day come up with the right questions.

She sighed, it would have been nice to just phone Michael up, and just ask.

“Penny for your thoughts,” said Gerty from across the table where she was doing a word puzzle.

Mothers ‘classical’ music was playing from the speakers in the background, one of the bootleg tapes she used to buy from the markets, mainly live bands from the Jazz festival who played in the last thirty years. For a woman who once held the highest justice position in the country she could sometimes be a bit of a hypocrite.

“Nothing really Mama, I spent the afternoon at CERN looking over a little private puzzle..”

“Oh Charlie, you know I love puzzles, do tell!”

“Not really that kind of puzzle Mama,” she smiled, “but I guess I could dumb it down a little for you.”

“Charlie, just because I don't pretend to understand all that gobbledegook does not mean I have lost my faculties.”

“OK, well there are these ‘events’ for want of a better word which occur randomly, yet they cannot track them against known solar activity, man-made events, or any known phenomena..”

“What do you mean by random? Do you just not have enough data points to form a pattern?”

“Not really, there are nine dates of the bigger events and with Anne-Marie's new filter algorithm, and Michaels ability to search the new data, possibly hundreds of smaller events they will be able to detect going back through the data.”

“When did they start?” She said, her head still buried in the crossword.

“The first event was possibly in 1972, the 12th May, it was very isolated and weak, but we cannot really assume anything as they were using different equipment and methods to record things..”

Gerty looked up from her paper. “Er.. What time?” Charlie looked at her mother funny, she did not need to look at her thoughts to see the question was asked with some trepidation.

“Eleven oh four AM..”

Gerty dropped the paper and got up.

“What is it Mama?” Charlie got up too, concern for her mother evident in her face. There was an unwritten rule that they would not look into each other's minds but Charlie couldn't help see what was there, her mothers thoughts were clear without even trying.

“Oh my God, that was the time you were with that serial killer psychologist..”

“That event is burned into my memory, Charlie,” her mother said slowly, “the appointment was at 11 a.m. and we were on time. It was the day I first saw evil, and the day I lost my last remaining innocence about this world.”

“Don’t be silly Mama, it is just a coincidence. The gift, it only sees thoughts, you don't send anything, and certainly nothing that could be picked up on a hundred tonne particle detector five kilometres away.”

“If there is one thing I have learnt Charlie, it is that coincidences are only coincidences when they don’t mean anything, and you are making an assumption on the latter statement based on the false premise that anybody understands how all this works..”

“But Mama, like I say, how can it? Why, it would mean your reaction caused a number of particles to be affected, it just, it.. Well it’s impossible.”

“Aren't all those stupid particle quantum things impossible? How can something be in two places at once? How can a cat be dead and not dead? All of it is circumstantial evidence and if it was presented in court or law it would be thrown out.”

“Admit it Charlie, science is plateauing, in some cases completely broken. Physics is no better than biology, they have no clue how this,” Gerty touched her head, “works even for a normal person. What we do is totally impossible under both biology and physics, what is the medium that thoughts travel in? Why can you and I store a million times more data than a normal person? Charlie, you have the equivalent of masters degrees in eighteen subjects, I speak fifteen languages better than most natives can, how does anything you have learnt in physics or biology explain that?”

“Well, yes, but there are so many gaps in our knowledge, that's why CERN is there in the first place..”

“Then you have answered your own question, it is probably another one of those gaps we are seeing.”

“But Mama, are you not curious how..”

“Every second of my life I analyse it, every new fact I learn makes me re-ask why. But one thing I do know is that the first rule of science is that a theory must be testable and reproducible. A bunch of scientists pulling fairy stories from their bottoms that cannot be tested is just that, theory. You are the scientist, tell me I am wrong! Now give me another of those dates.”

“Oh my god Mama, you are so cynical at times,” she shrugged, it was a regular argument for them, and one she actually agreed on. “OK, the third of January 2000, that's my birth date!”

“What time?” Gerty was calm and composed.

“Six forty two AM..”

“My memory of having you was obviously not clear to the exact time, but I went into labour at Six twelve in the morning and had you in my arms for the first time at six forty four AM.”

Charlie got up and walked around the room, not saying anything for a few seconds, then looking at her mother. “The event in CERN Mama, it is you!”

“It *could* be me,” she corrected, her voice calm. “How many dates do you have?”

“I must admit Mama I skimmed the rest of the data, but the text summary said there was nothing significant for several years, and until after the upgraded detectors came online. I did not look at any dates, only the first two, but I do know from what I got from Michael that there was one on the second of October 2012, which was the date I heard that voice from my head in the library at the university.. Oh god Mama, I need to read the rest of that data properly, I need to talk to Michael..”

[...] [you wake] [...]

[Not now!] she said forcefully.

Her mother flinched, then walked over to where she was standing by a chair. “The voice in your mind is still troubling you honey?” She said in a motherly calm tone, touching Charlie's hand softly.

“It is not troubling me Mama, it is always there, but it stays quiet now,” she said, wrapping both arms around her, “it only appears when I get anxious, but I have learnt to ignore it, I have enough going on in my head without talking to myself as well.”

Charlie unwrapped her arms from her mothers neck, stepped back and smiled a little. Her mother smiled back.

“You know you cannot go to this Michael guy, or anyone else about this don't you?” Said her mother deadpan.

“Yes, I guess I do Mama, but something might be happening we are not aware of, we might be onto something very important that might change our understanding of our gift and maybe even physics itself.”

“Then you research it in private, and, like everything we do, have a perfectly good explanation that points away from our gift.”

Her voice went serious. “You only have to think back to what they did to women they thought were witches in the middle ages, do not for one second think that this world is so civilised that you and I would be left alone as normal. You have not seen a hundredth of the evil I have and I want it to stay that way.”

“I know that Mama, your teaching was very thorough in that regard and I have learnt enough that I know everything you say is true.. Oh my!”

“What is it darling?”

“The woman, Michael's colleague, Anne-Marie, I got the thought from him that she wanted to see me to ask me a question about the university incident, do you think she has figured this out too.”

Charlie felt her dragon stirring again and tempered it down.

“Not a chance,” said her mother, the same time Charlie realised the answer herself. “How would she know our dates and times? She may possibly have a date from something public, maybe the event in Geneva University library, but there are thousands of people who reported something, including you and me. I would not worry. It is most likely she will ask the question but our gift should allow us the foresight to give her an acceptable alternative answer.”

“You are right again Mama, I guess I need to keep my emotions under control, but we also need to figure this out before they do.”

“I know. But you cannot be seen to take too much of an interest in the phenomena too.”

“Well, it’s a little bit late for that unfortunately. I spent an hour at lunch with the Director of CERN in view of a hundred people in the restaurant, then I spent two further hours in one of the meeting rooms reading the reports.”

“OK,” said Gerty, “but they haven't put two-and-two together yet and may never do it as they only have one half of the puzzle, how are you going to get more information without raising suspicion?”

“Anne-Marie would seem the perfect person, she knows about the phenomena and wants to talk to me. Unfortunately Michael and Anne-Marie cannot make it to uncle's party on Saturday, Michael sent me an email yesterday, and mentioned Anne-Marie is uncomfortable with crowds.”

“That’s a bit of a shame, I think I would like to see this young man who has taken so much of my little angel's attention.”

“Oh, it's not like that Mama, he’s not my type. I can see he really likes me though, and it would be very easy to make it romantic, but I get a sense he likes this Anne-Marie more, but I am not the kind of woman who could do that to another.”

“Charlie, you are a French woman!.” Gerty said mischievously.

“Correction Mama, I am a Swiss woman, Michael is nice but he is not the one for me,” she paused and changed to English, “close but no cigar!”

“So vulgar!”

Both the women laughed.

“Anyway,” Charlie said, “I think I have a plan.”

# Secrets Within the Vines

“ARE YOU SURE YOU ARE OK with this Anne-Marie?” Said Michael with a slightly worried voice.

“Yes, Michael, it might be fun just the three of us.”

Anne-Marie, really wasn’t sure however. Michael had said that his relationship with Charlie was platonic and he had described to her in detail everything of their fondue evening, it sure sounded pleasant. But having another young woman, beautiful, intelligent, rich, and apparently exciting as the main topic of conversation for the last few weeks was a lot to take in. Yet why wasn’t she jealous?

“Oh, maybe this is her now,” said Michael, first excitedly, then tempering off as confusion kicked in.

The old VW Beetle convertible bounced slowly twice as it went over the small speed bump and pulled into a spare car park, badly straddling the parking line and taking up two car spaces. A young woman in cut-off jeans, t-shirt, trainers and a baseball cap got out and walked over to them.

“Er, Charlie?” Said Michael.

“Michael,” she said with a pleasant smile and little ceremony, taking his hand and shaking it. She turned to Anne-Marie who was in a pretty floral dress cut off at the knee and looking quite stunning.

“And you must be Anne-Marie, Michael has said so much about you, and you are even more beautiful than I imagined, no wonder he cannot stop talking about you.”

[Michael cannot stop talking about me?]

Michael's mind was all confusion looking from Charlie, to Anne-Marie to the old car and back. Charlie ignored him, this gig was all for Anne-Marie.

“Enchante Anne-Marie. I am Charlie.” Charlie came in and kissed both cheeks very lightly, sensing Anne-Marie was relaxing even more. She was of course cheating a little bit by sending out the feeling of ‘safe’ she used with animals and she knew rubbed off a little on people when they were very close.

“I hope you two don't mind sitting in the back together, I have Max in the front, he is so sweet but likes to sit down on the floor well.”

[She has a dog?]

“Max is a cocker spaniel, I just picked him up from the doggy salon. I am minding it for my mother’s friend Cissy. I thought we could take him for a walk around the vineyard, and then we can go have a nice lunch. It will just be the four of us!”

Well the five of us. Thought Charlie. Carl was close by, somewhere. The vineyard was considered a safe area and a plain clothed FIS Agent was going to be there today as there were a few dignitaries going there before Joerg’s party.

[a cocker spaniel just like Bonbon! oh my, I hope he is friendly]

“Now Max is a bit sloppy so let's keep him away from your nice clothes Anne-Marie, or you may get covered in drool. Come on, let’s get in, it's a ten kilometre drive.

Max was the cutest thing on four legs for a dozen kilometres and the only one that they could find that fit the description of the dog that was in the picture of Anne-Marie from when she was a teenager on her step-mothers Facebook page. Alice spent an hour on Friday morning phoning friends and associates asking if they knew of anyone with a cocker spaniel, Alice’s neighbour Cissy was happy for someone to use Max for a doggy photo-shoot for the day, one that would pay a hundred Euros.

“Er, Charlie, you seem a different woman today,” said Michael suspiciously.

[What the hell is she up to?] [Well at least Anne-Marie seems happy enough]

“Oh Michael, you have only seen one side of me, I’m just a normal ol’ girl at heart.”

Did the Swiss girl just do an American cowboy accent! He shook his head.

Charlie turned to Anne-Marie who was just looking down the passenger seat at the dog, and said something very fast in French, that Michael only got two words of and none of it made sense. Anne-Marie giggled and smiled at Charlie, then Michael, then turned her attention back to the dog.

“Oh Charlie, he is so cute, I used to have a dog just like him when I was a teenager, can I hold him?”

“Well like I say as long as you don't mind him slobbering all over you, you have been warned!” She smiled at Anne-Marie, picking up Max, projecting a little more of the ‘safety’ emotion into his mind, and passing her to Anne-Marie, who did not hesitate to pick up Max and sit in the front seat with him.

“Can I stay here, I think he likes me?”

“Of course, you can sit up front with Max and myself, and Michael can sit in the back.”

Michael finally twigged what Charlie was up to, oh god, he had never seen Anne-Marie so happy.

Anne-Marie was indeed happy, Charlie was quite nice, and funny like Michael. Her little joke that Anne-Marie should make Michael roll over so she could tickle his tummy like Max probably was a bit fast for Michael to get. Come to think of it, Charlie had used Anne-Marie's dialect for some of the words, and she had the same dog as Anne-Marie had, how could anyone not like Charlie.

Charlie drove the ten kilometres to the vineyard that was run by cousin Eliot. The two girls were sheltered from the wind by the windscreen, Michael however was getting blown about for the fifteen minute drive and he had a really bad hair day when he arrived. Charlie made another joke about it to Anne-Marie who sniggered again.

It was Eliot’s day off, he was at home getting ready for his fathers party, but he had reserved the area of the patio of their Michelin star restaurant for Charlie and told the staff to put everything on the family tab. While Charlie was the official owner, she had no use for the winery or the vineyards, and winemaking was the kind of thing Eliot was really good at.

Charlie parked in Eliot's spot in the mostly empty car park, the tasting room had just opened but it would not really get busy until the restaurant opened in a few hours. Before they did all that though, there was a little matter of walking Max.

Poor Michael was not getting a word in edgeways. The two girls walked together, Anne-Marie holding Max’s lead and they talked and talked, and he still couldn't understand half the pronunciations. The fact that the scenery was beautiful and the weather perfect made up for the fact that he now felt like a fifth leg on a donkey.

“Oh Michael, Anne-Marie is the nicest person I have ever met, you are such a lucky man to have such a woman.” Charlie threw the comment over her shoulder at Michael several metres back, and he was resigned to the fact he was not going to be in this conversation while Charlie was working whatever magic she was doing.

“Oh, we are only friends Charlie,” said Anne-Marie, quietly to Charlie so that Michael could not hear.

“Impossible!” Said Charlie, “the way he talks about you, the way he looks at you, he definitely fancies you! Me, well I prefer the darker hot blooded Italian men to the cute English ones, but he is so nice to talk to.”

“Aren't you Michael?” She shouted in English over her shoulder.

“Er, aren’t I what?” He said, confused.

“So quaint,” she said, smiling at Anne-Marie. “You know Anne-Marie, we should go to the cellars, they have an amazing history, we must try some of the wines first though, my cousin runs the cellar.”

They stopped while Max took a lazy crap, then Charlie handed Michael a plastic bag, winked, gave him Max’s lead, and the two girls moved on, laughing at another joke.

Michael sighed! They were almost back to the vineyard, hopefully his day would get better.

“Oh poor Michael,” said Anne-Marie, “we have neglected him so much and the poor man probably has not understood a word we said.”

“You are right Anne-Marie, we have tortured him enough.”

Then just like that he understood what they were saying. “We are sorry Michael, it is my fault, Anne-Marie and I have quite a lot in common after all, I hope you don't mind me sharing her with you.”

“Er, of course, what was that language you were speaking?”

“Anne-Marie is from Toulouse, they have a few weird words and pronunciations, but you and her have been talking together for weeks so I expect you know that.”

Charlie really needed to stop baiting Michael, but he was one of those rare nice guys who never took anything personal, the kind of man who looked like he was not over-compensating for deficiencies in other areas. Yes Anne-Marie was a lucky woman. Also it wasn't really deception, Charlie told herself. She played many roles, she did like animals, and she really had come to like Anne-Marie a lot. Even though they had only been talking for a few hours, she felt somehow familiar, like she had known her a lot longer. Having an insight into her soul was an unfair advantage but it was not something she could turn off, only choose to ignore.

Anne-Marie’s mind was another thing entirely, it was in fact beautiful. The thoughts were tidy and precise, and you needed to be almost next to her to read her, Anne-Marie could weirdly compartmentalise logic and emotions, almost like a committee rather than a person.

They were the same age but with completely different backgrounds and upbringings, the more she bonded with Anne-Marie the more she saw her grandmother in her. A genius, an introvert, and beautiful, and while one was cruelly used and abused, the other was finding friendship and love. The emotion was overwhelming and a tear came to her eyes from nowhere.

Anne-Marie stopped and looked at her, the emotional part of her coming to the fore, concerned. “Are you OK Charlie?”

Charlie smiled, “I am Anne-Marie, it is just that, you are really how I pictured my grandmother as a young girl. I really wish I had known her. I really think we could have been good friends too.”

Anne-Marie did something that shocked Michael, she moved in to Charlie and was hugging her. The hug went on for a good thirty seconds, both Michael and Max stood there unmoving and not understanding.

Anne-Marie was also feeling conflicting emotions, holding Charlie like this just felt like the right thing to do and it felt so good, she felt a tear too, hers. The warmth of Charlie's body and the tears of both of them now mixing on her cheek. And yet they were not tears of sadness for Anne-Marie, they were tears of joy.

Finally Michael said something. “Are you guys OK?”

Charlie felt his concern, first for Anne-Marie, then for Charlie. For thirty seconds both Michael and Anne-Marie’s minds scrolled through a narrative of reflection to what had got them to this point. It was very overpowering for Charlie to listen to; but she could not unlisten when people were standing right next to her, that was her cross to bear.

Almost every one of her girlfriends had been through the same path with boys, family, careers, health issues, the list went on, and Charlie was happy to be a shoulder to cry on. But, apart from her mother and grandfather, it was very rare she needed somebody to hug her.

“Thank you,” said Charlie, dampening the tears from her eyes with her sleeve, and slowly breaking off the hug, “I really needed that Anne-Marie.”

Charlie bent down and fluffed Max’s floppy ears, then all smiled winked at Anne-Marie and said, “men like that too!”

She got up. They were almost back to the car-park.

“Why don't we go and do a little wine tasting and I will give you a little tour around the wine cellars and show you all the secrets. Unfortunately Max is not allowed in the winery but I am sure we can find someone to check he is OK for an hour or two until lunchtime.”

She saw a tree in the shade with an old bike welded to a platform as some rustic statue, she tied his lead securely around a heavy piece of metal and went into a nearby door. Two minutes later she returned walking very slowly with a big water bowl which she put next to Max. When she was alone with Max, Charlie got the usual vibes from a dog, when it felt safe and was ready for a nap.

“Now stay there and be a good doggy Max,” she fluffed his ears again and the dog sunk like a wet towel onto the ground.

“I really wish I could have a dog again, my lease does not allow them though,” said Anne-Marie, looking longingly at the ball of brown fluff on the ground under the tree.

“You are always welcome to my house, we have two dogs there which are always happy for someone to give them some attention.” Technically they had three dogs at the chateau, but the gamekeeper's dog, Butch, would rip the arm off anyone who tried to pat him, Charlie for some strange reason being the only exception.

The girls slowly brought Michael back into the conversation. Wine-tasting was one of those mostly subjective things, like music. Some wines were just great, like the Beatles were great, but they also had bad vintages, like the Beatles had some crap songs, yet some people still liked their crap songs. It turned out Michael's palette was useless and Anne-Marie’s not that much better.

“Taste is like every muscle, guys, it needs to be exercised over years. There is no magic to it.” Charlie said.

She was decidedly average when it came to telling the difference between some wines, but she always knew a grand-master when she saw one, and a good wine when she tasted one. Anne-Marie opened out a lot, Charlie could see she was enjoying it and she was making little quips as they tried the various wines.

“Oh Charlie, I think my shyness around people will be the death of me. I am so sorry we did not go to your uncle's party, Michael was so enthusiastic but..”

This time it was Michael's turn to put his arm around her. “I’m not sorry, this has been far more fun if you ask me.”

“Michael's right, that is why I wanted to bring you two along. All those old people in their fancy clothes, trying to one-up everybody else in how interesting or important they are.. It is nowhere near as nice as this!”

“And look at you Anne-Marie, smiling, talking, laughing, hugging, yet only two hours ago you looked like you were about to go to the dentist for root canal surgery. It shows that if you make the effort with people it can sometimes go quite well.”

“See that good looking chap over there who is coming over? What is there possibly to fear from him? I have never seen him before in my life, yet I find I want to know him a lot more.”

She winked at Anne-Marie in the way she knew she found funny.

“I will let you see how the master handles the situation and you, my dear apprentice, should watch and learn.”

A handsome, well dressed, Mediterranean-tanned guy with dark hair and a smile to die for walked up to them.
