She could feel no apprehension at all from Anne-Marie at the approaching man, Michael however was feeling a tinge of jealousy. If Charlie was brutally honest, and based on looks alone, he probably had every reason to be..

Wait, What! Charlie was shocked for a moment, the man’s thoughts were on Anne-Marie not her! This had never happened to Charlie before, maybe she had overdone the dressing down a bit. Anne-Marie certainly looked and acted much more sophisticated, and she had zero anxiety as she was probably expecting Charlie to do all the talking.

He was about to ask Anne-Marie about the wine and thinking of how to work the fact that he was driving (his fathers) Lamborghini into the conversation. Seriously, she thought, with looks like that, you have to act pretentious!

Just like astronomers classed stars, Charlie immediately classed him as a harmless predator, then sub-classed him as a dick. Though a justifiably good looking one.

Anne-Marie could thank her for this later.

“You know,” Charlie said in Italian a millisecond before he turned to Anne-Marie to deliver his line, “I was just telling Anne-Marie and her boyfriend here, that you looked like somebody who appreciated good wine.”

The new man turned to her, his thoughts, annoyed that the beautiful woman had such an average boyfriend, such a pale skinned and weak looking one at that. Then the fact that the other girl was actually quite pretty under the ghastly clothes and hair.

“You are Italian? From Milan?” He said.

“Oh no, I lived there for two years, I think the accent must have rubbed off a little.”

She saw him looking at her arms, her neck and ankles, filling in the gaps in his mind, then thinking again of Anne-Marie and her weak boyfriend.

“You should join us. We are celebrating Anne-Marie’s pregnancy, but you know her boyfriend does not know yet. Doesn't her skin glow so beautifully?”

It was like she had thrown a bucket of water over his mental image of Anne-Marie. His thoughts immediately backtracking from the hunt to how he could get away from this group without looking stupid. Then thinking maybe the badly dressed woman might be good for one night, so long as his friends didn't see him. Charlie quietly fumed, wanting so badly to kick him where it hurt, or even better have Carl..

“I know plenty about wine, fortunately for you..”

She saw his mind going through the three, no four actual facts he knew, and the more impressive stuff he was about to make up for these amateurs.

“Oh, we should all speak in French, my friends here do not speak Italian.” She said it in French and she saw his mind parsing the correct verbs from Italian to French before speaking, discarding those sentences he could not translate and still working on how to get away.

“As I was saying to the mademoiselle here,” he continued in terrible French to all three of them, “the red wine here is very average compared to the Aglianico from the region of Valpontida.”

“Valpontida, what a coincidence I was there during the 2019 vintage. I did not know they grew Aglianico there, but the Merlot from there is indeed nice, but I only sampled a few of the vineyards, which one do you recommend?”

He was making everything up. He knew the small region but had never actually been to any wineries there and knew nothing, damn, er, the phone trick.

“Mi Scusi! I have a phone call..” [on silent] “..the phone is on silent..”

He pulled out his phone with the screen facing away from them.

“Ah, monsieur, mademoiselles, I will have to cut our wine discussion short, I have some urgent business.”

He walked away pretending one half of a conversation in Italian.

“Oh he seemed like a nice man!” Said Anne-Marie, as he went out of earshot.

Charlie could not believe it, Anne-Marie genuinely bought the whole act. She sometimes wished she could give the gift to women like Anne-Marie at times like this.”

“He wasn’t exactly telling the truth, Anne-Marie, I think he was just making something up to talk to us. Sometimes men do that. They first weaken your defences with their good looks and charm, then overwhelm you with their romantic words, then soften you with talk of l’amour, then they pounce!” She put her hands out in fake claws. Next you are waking in their bed and they are asking you if there is somewhere else you need to be!”

“Err.. still here!” Said Michael weakly in English and causing both girls to laugh.

“Present company excluded.” Charlie said in English.

“So Anne-Marie, sometimes it is wise to do your due diligence before you give in to their charms.” She looked at Michael, “but I think you are safe with him.”

“Hey, I can do that l’amour stuff you know,” said Michael, though nobody seemed to be listening.

“Intelligent, charming, can talk, and l’amour,” I think he is the one for you Anne-Marie she said in her dialect, “I hope the l’amour part comes soon for you.”

Anne-Marie blushed and Charlie caught a little bit of apprehension, she could see Anne-Marie had never been with a man and was scared to go to the next step; sex terrified her but only because she thought she would be inadequate. Oh my girl, have you got a lot to learn?

“Hey Michael, could you please check on Max while we go and get the key to the cellars.”

Michael popped off to the exit grateful for something to do while Charlie hooked her arm through Anne-Marie’s and led her in the opposite direction.

“You know Anne-Marie,” Charlie continued, “I remember my first real boyfriend, I was so scared the first time that it would not be perfect, I did not know what to do.”

She had Anne-Marie's rapt attention.

“The first kiss was wonderful, a man who is a good kisser should be number one on any girls list. But love is like a mountain, you climb to the first level then you take a look around and if you like what you see, you climb up to the next bit, until finally you see the top, and like a mountain this seems the scary part.”

“I was scared, I was terrified. But what my friend told me, and what I should have realised is that passion is born into all women, especially French women, and when the time came, with the right man, reaching the summit was so beautiful. A woman should always do what makes her feel good, a good man will always accommodate that, as it is their ultimate goal to make you satisfied, and to get you to that amazing place where the world explodes for you. I still relive my first time with Thomas Mancini in my mind, thinking how I would have done it differently, and I realised I would have done everything exactly the same.”

Could she really do that with Michael? Charlie made it sound so beautiful, not scary, and to do that with Michael, would be so.. So.. ohhh.. ohhh.. Oh my!

Oh my indeed! Thought Charlie.

“Er, I just need to go to the ladies room!” Said Anne-Marie in a squeaky, embarrassed voice, as she moved away quickly.

Well that had never happened before! Charlie sighed, but at least Anne-Marie seemed to be getting the idea now.

Another man came over, one of the undercover FIS agents, and handed Charlie the key. Michael came back and they waited for Anne-Marie.

“Right, where were we,” said Charlie, hooking her arm through Anne-Marie’s again, “oh yes, and I think you both might find this next part really interesting.”

* * *

At the start of the second world war, the threat of invasion to Switzerland, despite its neutrality, was very real. Many of the bigger buildings engineered underground fortifications, to form part of the country's defence if it was ever invaded by Germany. The vineyard was no different, and in 1938 no fewer than forty local labourers excavated a forty metre tunnel into the hillside under the vineyard with six separate rooms for armouries, bunks, bathrooms, kitchens and even a small medical facility, enough for twenty men.

Subsequently at the end of the second world war, and the height of the cold war, the bunker was incorporated into the network of nuclear fall-out shelters the Swiss were provisioning for every citizen. Even to this day these facilities were expected to be maintained and stocked, though many had started to consider it a waste of time.

With some of this latter thinking, five of these rooms had been converted to wine storage, a hundred barrels and tens of thousands of wine bottles, a labyrinth of shelves, now filled these rooms.

“This is amazing!” Said Michael who was leading the two girls by a few metres. Many of the lights were still incandescent and due to the density of the shelving it was not very bright.

“Twelve thousand six hundred and four bottles, and ninety seven French oak barrels of wine.” She walked to the entrance to one of the rooms stacked three high with barrels, then led them into it.

“A 2018 Chardonnay, six years old and almost ready to be bottled and sent to a wine merchant in Singapore,” she flicked the little label over to show a barcode. “Some are put up for auction after the tastings and blendings, and contrary to what our good looking, but slightly intellectually challenged Italian gigolo said earlier, they are usually very high premium.”

“It feels a bit cool in here,” said Anne-Marie.

Michael used the excuse to get in closer to Anne-Marie and put his arm around her, she leaned into him, and if she was a cat she would be purring. Charlie knew they had a small locker at the entrance with some overcoats, but she didn't want to interfere.

“It’s kept at a constant sixteen degrees all year around, changes in temperature are to be avoided at all costs.”

“So it’s your uncle that owns this, or does he just run it?” Michael asked.

Charlie knew that Michael was just making conversation but he was getting a bit close, and she wouldn't lie.

“He runs it..”

[So who owns..]

“.. My mother actually owns most of it,” she dropped casually. It wasn't quite a lie, she and her mother shared everything so technically her mother owned half.

“She was a very successful lawyer in her day.”

“Oh my!” Said Anne-Marie, her next question forming in her mind.

“So what do you actually do Charlie?”

[Michael was realising he also had asked that question but never really got a satisfactory answer]

“I work with my mother, I am a negotiator.”

[what’s a..] both of them were about to ask the same question.

“Wherever there are two parties who want to make a contract, we are the neutral third party. It pays well, the hours are good, and it is quite rewarding.”

She could see a million questions bubbling to the surface of both their minds. Mostly questions a technical or scientific mind, rather than a business mind would ask, like ‘why would anybody pay for that?’, she had better move the topic to something else.

“Now enough of the boring work talk, let me give you the part of the tour that nobody ever sees.”

The millions of questions about Charlie's job were immediately forgotten as Charlie stopped at a locked iron door with a keypad. Instead of using the keypad she put all five fingers on a flat pad next to it, it stayed red for a few seconds then changed to green and they heard a click and a motor start up, the iron door moved inwards opening into a corridor. At the same time there was a loud click back down the passage where they came from.

“Wow, that's a bit James Bond isn't it?” Said Michael as they started walking.

“There is a good reason,” she said as she started down a narrow well lit passage which sank deeper into the earth. “When it was part of the Swiss Defence force, this passage was considered suitable for storing some of the city and government archives.”

“All the official documents were moved out of here six years ago to a special underground storage area near Bern and we decided it would make an ideal place for some of the Bergmann family archives and historical stuff. The constant temperature and humidity was ideal for preserving them.”

“Wow,” said Anne-Marie, after they had been walking for a minute, “we must have travelled a hundred metres.”

“Seventy two metres horizontally and five vertically to be exact,” she said as she came to a second door with the same security arrangement.

“The first door we went through won't open if the bunker main door is open and once it opens a steel door shuts the whole cellar off from the outside world.”

Her other hand went on the second pad and it also turned green and the second metal door proceeded to open.

“I’m so glad I am not.. claustrophobic,” said Michael, emphasising the last word, not knowing the French word so saying it in English.

“It is the same word in French, but we pronounce it much nicer than you,” said Charlie. She could see Anne-Marie had formed the same answer in her mind but was not sure to say it, she was now wishing she had said it. Charlie resolved to not be so quick in future.

“Double-Wow!” Said Michael as the door opened out into a very large room full of shelves, filing cabinets, furniture and old books, the light was bright but a weird off-white.

“The light is filtered to remove any wavelengths damaging to the old documents.” She walked over to a very specific framed faded colour photograph of a pretty teenage girl, and handed it to Anne-Marie.

“My Grandmother Heidi Bergmann. She died when my mother was born, it was a very sad story and a very sad episode for the family, even though she was loved by everyone we prefer to keep it to ourselves.”

Charlie's voice had stopped being its usual sparkling happy self and had turned morose.

“And these books next to it?” Said Anne-Marie, not quite sure what had happened to Charlie's demeanour.

“Her favourite books. See this one,” Charlie pulled out the small faded book which was in German but said the word Heidi on it. “Heidi is a very famous Swiss book and it is who my grandmother was named after. You see when my grandmother arrived in Switzerland she was only about two years old and my great grandfather who had brought her across the Alps from Germany died the day she arrived. I read the book growing up, but it is too painful for me to read now.”

The change in Charlie's demeanour was to say the least dramatic, Michael had picked up on it too and there was a new clarity and maturity to Charlie’s voice.

“That is so sad,” said Anne-Marie.

Charlie could see Michael had also felt the same way but he had just turned around, hiding his emotions like men tended to do, and began leafing through the shelf.

“My great-grandfather was not a good man. A Nazi. Nobody had any sympathy for him and nobody missed him. Heidi was the total opposite, but due to the journey he put her through we think she was malnourished. The poor girl had so many mental issues. But she read books and played the piano. She was a genius, a wunderkind, but stayed a child emotionally. She died before her eighteenth birthday giving birth to my mother.”

Charlie dropped down on an old seat next to a large oak desk, put her hands to her face and cried.

Dragon reared its voice back into her head briefly and clearly before she slapped it down softly.

[Leave me alone, please!]

Anne-Marie, who was rushing to Charlie, looked around confused at Michael, then was once again on her knees and holding her. Michael would have done the same, but in a few short hours, Anne-Marie’s bond to Charlie seemed to have become even greater than his. For some reason the thought of that made him happy not sad, or would have if Charlie wasn't crying.

Michael wandered around the desk, still slightly uncomfortable and out of his depth with the emotions on display. The two girls said nothing, hugging each other, Charlie's sobs the only sound in the room. The desk looked hundreds of years old, he guessed they brought it down here to preserve it like the rest or it was left when the government archive was relocated.

He idly pulled a small dusty old black book off another shelf, opening it and watching some scraps of paper fall out.

[Oh shit, I hope I can get these back in before Charlie notices, I don't want to upset her further]

He looked at one of the sheets, trying to match the order to put it back in. The writing was strange, maybe Austrian? Was that different from German? He was intrigued, dying to ask Charlie what it was. No wait it was no language, they were Roman characters but some of these words were just gibberish, not even pronounceable. Ah! It was a cipher code of some sort.. I wonder if.. Oh wait, this is Charlie's history she probably already knows..

Charlie’s head came up, sniffing away the tears and a very weak smile forming as she looked deep into Anne-Marie’s blue eyes, “you are good at this Anne-Marie, if you ever need a shoulder to cry on you at least know where to find me. I’m sorry, this always happens when I come to this room, I see my grandmother's picture and her troubled life and I just lose it, it's probably one of the reasons I rarely come here.”

She turned to Michael. “What's that you have Michael?”

“I’m not sure, sorry they fell out, and I was trying to find the order they go back in, but they are some sort of cipher.” He passed one of the sheets to Charlie and she held it so that both she and Anne-Marie could read it.

“I studied ciphers as part of my mathematics degree,” said Anne-Marie, very interested in the paper. “They had many ciphers throughout history, from simple letter substitution to one time pads. If I knew the date I could take a guess at which one and maybe..”

“Can I see the book?” Charlie said, holding her hand out to Michael.

He put it carefully in her hand and she looked at the front, a swastika that had been scribbled over!

“It is my great-grandfather's journal from 1945, I think nobody in my family cares for his secrets.” She gave it back to him unopened..

“But what about your great-grandmother or grandmother, they were probably innocent of all of this, maybe even victims, don’t they deserve to be remembered?” Said Anne-Marie’s concerned voice.

Anne-Marie's words were like a steel spike driven straight into Charlie’s heart, she wanted to cry again, wanted to shout at Anne-Marie, but the anger died instantly. She found she could not argue with her, the concern in Anne-Marie’s voice and in her head were only to complete the story, to help Charlie and her mother, and she thought she had seen a way to help.

Charlie sank back down in the seat.

“The history of the family is very complicated, Anne-Marie. The family spent decades whitewashing my great-grandfather’s legacy from their history and giving back to the community in partial recompense for something none of them believed in or did. Much of the school, university and hospital lands around here were donated by my step-grandfather, a percentage of the profits of the companies we all own go to the Red-Cross and other charities, there are scholarships for science, art, law all funded by us. None of us want to open old wounds.”

“You can trust me, Charlie,” Anne-Marie said touching her arm, “I may not be able to find anything, as the cipher may require a key I do not have, but anything I find I will keep to myself and take to my grave. However, if there is anything about your mother or grandmother I will tell you.”

And Charlie knew she could trust Anne-Marie, because it was written not just in her thoughts but in her heart.

* * *

They retrieved Max, and Anne-Marie sat with him on her lap, stroking his ears and eating and drinking with one hand, Michael keeping the conversation away from Charlie’s history, so as not to upset her again. Inevitably though the conversation strayed to their work at CERN.

Charlie contemplated on what was to come next. Something about Anne-Marie felt right, it felt impossible, but she had never formed an attachment so quickly or intensely to anyone. She just couldn’t explain it, it wasn't love, it felt even stronger, like a bond, yet every time she looked into Anne-Marie’s mind it only increased. Michael was nice, but the bond wasn't with him, yet he was also bound to Anne-Marie somehow.

She had instructed the staff to close this part for the next hour at least until she left, Carl and the FIS agent would enforce it if necessary, but it never was. She apologised to the maitre-d for the disruption.

This part was going to be painful but needed to be done in private, it was only fair for true friends to know the person they were becoming friends with.

Neither Michael nor Anne-Marie had yet questioned why they had the only table, nor why the whole outside dining area was now cordoned off with reserved signs, nor the large blond-haired man standing guard by the door.

“It is interesting how you guys don't really talk about what you do,” she said straight, “you make for very weird bedfellows, but you do know what I do don't you?”

“Well you said a negotiator, but it is a bit vague,” said Anne-Marie giving Max’s ear a tickle.

“Well let's ask Michael,” she turned to him, “I may have let slip a little bit of my skills during our first meeting, so what do you think a negotiator does?”

“Er, well, er, well you seemed to know everything about me, almost before I said anything.”

“Good, that’s a small start, but let’s see, we have Anne-Marie here with a doctorate in mathematics and a paper on chaos theory, and Michael here with his doctorate in Computer science and three papers on large data set analysis. I’m sure two brilliant minds can do a little better than that.”

When they realised she was being serious, nobody said anything, she could sense a little apprehension forming.

“Michael, do you remember what shirt you were wearing that day we first talked?” Charlie said.

“Er, maybe my blue one?”

She looked at him seriously.

“It was a white long-sleeved shirt with the top two buttons undone. You also wore light brown trousers and a part leather, part fabric light grey belt. Your shoes were tan Hush Puppies with short white socks. You wore a short sleeved patterned shirt the day before with a white tee-shirt underneath and wore a tan and green base-ball cap which read ‘Tennis club’, but spelt wrong with a single ‘n’. Both times you took the same grey rucksack, the same iPhone in a black wallet case, the same Casio watch and the same MacBook-Air laptop loaded with FreeBSD. You have a neutral southern England accent, but two of your word pronunciations are only common with people from northern England. I could tell you exactly every word you said to me if you wanted.”

Anne-Marie had stopped stroking Max’s ear rapt at attention, the fork halfway back to the plate. Michael almost choked on the wine he had just sipped, spilling half his glass.

“Careful Michael,” she smiled, “that’s quite good wine you’re spilling.”

“How do..” Started Michael, ignoring his soggy shorts.

“You have an Eidetic memory.” Said Anne-Marie slowly, aghast, “but surely photographic memories are an urban legend?”

Charlie picked up the pen and paper the waitress had left on the table, she put the paper down on the table and wrote a very large mathematical equation, then under it another, then continued writing.

Anne-Marie gasped, shocked. Everything she ever saw in her small world collapsed and now started to pale into insignificance as Charlie wrote down the first three equations of her doctorate.

“You made a mistake on page three by the way, a mistake which your review board never found, reference seven was linked to an old paper by Clarence Johnstone, not his current one.”

The two were still speechless, Michael was getting very anxious, Anne-Marie a bit scared.

Do you guys think you know what a negotiator is now? She said again, still without humour..

“Well you probably don't because I am seeing two people now who are looking at me like an alien.” A tear came to her eye. “Do you guys think you know what a negotiator is now?” She said a third time but more softly.

“Me and my mother are in effect Switzerland's secret weapon, ensuring fairness and neutrality for any negotiation involving two parties. We carry diplomatic passports, and usually have secret service agents like the man by the door, never more than a shout away. I personally have negotiated multi-billion dollar deals, peace treaties between countries, and my mother and I also officially act as the highest court in Switzerland. But for all that I am still a woman, I still have feelings, and I still need friends.”

Anne-Marie was about to cry, as was Michael.

Charlie softened and took one of their arms in each of hers. “I don't count many people as friends, those I do need to accept me as a normal person, not question why or what I do. Anne-Marie, you have your secrets and I think you understand me from a perspective of being that person. Michael does not, but I see in Michael a very empathic man, a little bit besotted by a Swiss-girl, but actually very much in love with a French one.”

Anne-Marie cracked first, bursting into tears, throwing off Max getting up and falling into Charlie's lap, her face streaming tears as Charlie hugged her back.

“I didn’t have any real friends until a few weeks ago,” said Anne-Marie between sniffles. “When I met you today it felt like I have known you forever, and I cannot imagine a future where you are not in my life. Michael, here is the only reason I am not sitting at home now reading another book on my own, why I now have friends like you who I love to be with, and why I now have a real will to live life to the full. I wish I had met all of you years before, she went back onto Charlie's shoulder crying but softer this time.

“You guys need to know everything if you are to be my friends,” said Charlie, holding Anne-Marie close to her. “You need to know why I am mysterious, why I disappear often, why I don't have any history or public profile and why I can never have one. I have many friends, but they only know the other me. Sometimes I meet special people I do not want to carry that deception with, I don’t really understand why, but you two are both those special people.”

Michael came out of his stupor. “I always knew you were special in some way, that is what attracted me to you,” he eventually said, his voice uncharacteristically sombre. “This new information does not change anything or my feelings of friendship towards you Charlie. I am just glad you are in my life.” he looked at Anne-Marie. “In our life.”

Charlie felt the change in Anne-Marie as Michael said the last three words, but she remained on her shoulder.

“I can see why you are good at your job Charlie,” continued Michael, “you saw right through me, even to my love for Anne-Marie, though I wish you would have given me time to tell her myself first. Any chance I can get in there too?”

Charlie waved him in and he also wrapped his arms around the two of them. They stayed like that for almost a minute, the intense love between the two people was overpowering everything around her. Max just looked up at the three of them just feeling safe but very, very confused.

“I think you two need some alone time together,” Charlie said eventually, “and I think you should both enjoy the rest of the afternoon. I will ask one of the staff to have a car ready and somebody to take you home when you are finished. Take as long as you want and order anything you want, it is my special treat.”

Charlie turned and walked off to the exit, Max trundling after her, unbidden, she turned around after a few steps.

“Oh and please be gentle with her Michael, I feel like I have known Anne-Marie forever, and she is my friend too.”

The large blond-haired man moved to the side as Charlie and Max walked out, then followed. A minute later another man walked out and began taking down the ropes that had cordoned off the area. The two had watched Charlie until she left, then they looked at each other, their eyes still moist.

Anne-Marie's first kiss was everything Charlie and Sophie had said it would be and much, much more.

* * *

Charlie's short drive home took only five minutes, and she never left the vineyard grounds. Surprisingly she had only drank a half a glass of wine, though she remembered each and every tiny sip.

It was nearly one o’clock. She smiled, as she briefly thought of turning up to her uncle’s birthday dressed as she was, but unfortunately for her it would be very much an official function, one she would be expected to play her small part. At least Anne-Marie and Michael were probably happy.

It was the normal smart, debonair and sophisticated Charlie that emerged from her bedroom at two o’clock. She knocked on her mothers room and entered. Gerty was standing next to a full length mirror with a very beautiful long white dress with a light green sash belt and attempting to put some earrings in.

“Hi Mama,” she said. “Wow, you look great for someone at retirement age!”

“Humph! Retirement age! The term I think you were looking for my dear, is golden-girl. Now, come over here and help me put these blasted earrings in.”

“Thank you my dear, well, good men are hard to find when they don't know who you are, once they know you are rich and infamous you always attract the wrong type. Johan, your father, was so random, we crashed on the snow, and as I was about to give him a piece of my mind when he smiled and I felt how pure and deep his thoughts were..” She sighed and Charlie did not need to look any further to know what she was thinking.

“I’ve heard all that before, Mama, but there must be at least one other man out there like dad. I have seen one or two older men, who have all the attributes you say dad had and who would look nice in your arms.”

“I hope you are not matching me up with somebody!”

“Never, I’m just saying the option is there if you want to look.”

“Ha! That's never going to happen. What about you, what about this Michael guy?”

“He is really sweet, but I would rather have two good friends than one lover, and he isn't Italian.”

“He’s British, like Hugh Grant..”

“Urghh Mama, Hugh Grant is like a hundred years old, and Michael is way nicer than Hugh Grant.”

“Maybe if Hugh Grant was available.” Gerty clasped her hands together in a fake dreamy pose, “hmm do you think he would make a nice father in law Charlie?”

She walked out the door, “Mama, you are incredulous!” She said in English.

“I’m pretty sure that is not the right word..” Her mother shouted back in English, just as the door closed.

Gerty walked into the living room five minutes later and looked at Max curled up on her best Persian rug. Four of Gerty’s friends were out on the patio talking very loudly, she knew five others had already left for the party..

“Charlie, what is that dog doing here, and on my best rug?” She screamed.

“Calm down Mama, that’s Max. Remember the stunt-dog we were looking for, and he is so cute, I couldn't leave him outside. Mimi said she would drive him back home later this afternoon, and he played his part perfectly. The dog got a walk, Michael got his girl, Anne-Marie got her man, and I got two good friends..”

“Oh and Mama, what do you know about my great-grandfather's Nazi journal? It was the first time I ever saw what was in it, but it is all in some sort of cipher?”

“I thought they’d burnt that bastard's stuff,” she said with a bit more vitriol than required.

“No, it's still there albeit falling apart, I’m going to try and decode the journal, there should be enough processing power now that it could be done locally and not in any cloud systems.

“You need to be careful. There is nothing I can see that would be in there that could do any more damage to the family than has already been done, but you will not be popular with anyone for reopening old wounds,”

“I know that Mama, I have no interest in that bastard's racist rantings, but I have come to believe my grandmother and great-grandmother may have also been the victims here too, and you and I deserve to know their full story.”

Gerty sighed, putting her arms around Charlie. “I spent a few days when I was around your age, and in secret doing the same, I tried a few obvious substitution and rotation ciphers but obviously I had no way to scan them in, nor did I have anywhere near the computing you have now; plus the scars were still fresh enough to make me pause.” She shrugged and waved it off. “But I have no objections if you take precautions.”

“Thank you Mama.”

* * *

The Priest was frustrated, he was supposed to take out the old woman once her daughter had been eliminated in America, but it never happened. Now the house and grounds were covered by police and security, as a lot of new guests had begun to arrive for some other event.

He couldn't even get near the house. A no fly zone was in place and his five hundred dollar drone had been shot down into the lake two days before. Unless somebody passed his hidden camera by the gate he had now been reduced to waiting and not knowing who was where.

He knew another team was now being brought in by the ElderNacht and they had something big planned. His only clear instruction was to be a glorified lookout for them. It was demeaning, but it was also going to be his last job, and if he was smart he could use them as a distraction for his own retirement plan. He just hoped that the bitch had enough loot in that vault of hers to finance it.

# Code Breakers

WHEN ANNE-MARIE ARRIVED at the entrance on Monday morning, the guard told her she had a package waiting for her in the administration office she needed to sign for. She had never had anything like this happen, but her initial worry soon faded as she walked over to the head office building, after all it was only a package.

She walked up to the receptionist and said she was here to sign for a package and the receptionist's eyes went wide and then she pointed to the end of the waiting room where a solid, bald black-man in a black suit waited with a briefcase. Anne-Marie almost fainted there and then on the spot, but realised two things, firstly she was in the reception of the head office of CERN, not some murderous Marseilles back-alley, and secondly, she wasn't actually really that afraid of meeting this new person, in fact it might be fun.

She walked over trying not to show the small anxiety she was feeling as she got closer.

“Miss Verne?” He said in English.

“Er Yes sir.” She said sheepishly.

“It’s just Simon, you don’t ever have to call me sir. But can I please see some ID ma'am before I hand this over?”

“Er, yes of course,” she said more relaxed now that he had given his name, called her ma’am, and said please. She fumbled out her employee badge and driving licence both of which the man examined against her face in great detail.

“I must say Miss Verne,” he said smiling, “you look much nicer in person than your ID badges,”

She blushed as he handed her the suitcase and took out an electronic tablet to sign.

“My instructions are to tell you this,” he looked at his tablet. “Charlie says the document in this is the one you discussed. It must not be shown to anyone. It must not go out of your possession and you must not photocopy it in any way or if you do, then all copies must be verifiable destroyed. The briefcase also has a secure laptop loaded with the latest Ubuntu Linux distro, all and any processing must be done only on this. The only network connection possible is through a secure messenger application which will provide a direct connection to Charlie.” He paused.

“The Directorate office has been instructed to provide you with safe storage for the briefcase as you should not take it off site. The Director or his personal secretary are the only other people authorised to handle this. All other questions can be directed to and answered by Charlie using the secure messaging application. Any time you wish to return it, just send a message and I will be back to pick it up.”

“Well that's the message, a copy is inside the case, do you have any questions before you sign?”

“Er what if I lose it?”

The man smiled, “come now Anne-Marie, Charlie says you are way too smart for that.”

She signed and took the briefcase. She was then handed a key, “remember keep it safe.”

He walked out, the two receptionists following him with their eyes, both girls' eyes then falling on Anne-Marie and the suitcase.

“Well that could have gone a lot worse,” she said to herself and walked out of the door just as the big black Mercedes silently glided out of the car park on its powerful electric motors.

* * *

“Anne-Marie,” shouted Luc, immediately as she stepped out of the lift.

“Come, come.” He waved her over to where he, Michael, and Carlos and one of the technicians were looking at the big monitor, the guys were all in animated discussion.

Michael looked over, smiled, winked and turned back to the screen waiting for her to join them.

“That Fermi-lab team arrived over the weekend from America so they are asking for all available resources to help get the data from all the various systems into the same format. We are well placed to help them so it is going to be our first priority this morning,” said Carlos.

Luc picked up the narrative, pointing at the big screen.

“It also looks like the WOW team had two big events over the weekend, similar to the one on Wednesday. The one on Saturday morning was twice the magnitude of any previous one.”

“Do they have any data on the direction?” Anne-Marie said as she put her bag and the briefcase down next to her workstation.

“Not yet,” said Carlos. “None of us are really sure yet how well this is all going to play out as the Fermi-lab detectors are not much better than ours, the best either of us can do is a bunch of concentric circles which are likely to be wildly inaccurate. What they are hoping is that when they get them synchronised correctly then the resolution should improve and they can finally get some sort of location or vector.”

“It’s progress though,” said Luc. “It has been getting a bit boring around here recently and it is nice to get a bit of excitement.” He laughed, “though it would be a real shame if they trace it all back to some schoolkids home fusion reactor that he turns on once in a blue moon!”

Luc kind of wished he hadn't added that last little joke. Instead of laughing, everybody was thinking about the disappointment for the WOW team, and the dozens of years and millions of euros wasted.

The guys broke up, Luc, Carlos and the technician going to the coffee machine and Michael walking over to Anne-Marie, grabbing her around her waist and giving her a deep kiss.

“You look stunning this morning ma Cherie.” He said smiling. His smile was infectious, and after the wonderful weekend absolutely no news about the experiments they were doing could really interest her.

“What's with the briefcase?” Said Michael looking at the expensive leather item that looked totally out of place with Anne-Marie.

“It’s from Charlie, some big scary man dropped it off at the main office building this morning and would not let me have it until I had signed my life away!”

Michael looked at it. “Do you suppose it’s the pages from her great-grandfather's journal with the cipher code on it?”

“That's my guess, but there are some very serious instructions that come with it and I am not sure I can follow them. I think Charlie works in a different world from you and me, one we cannot imagine. She has given me a secure laptop and a messaging app so I will contact her today and see if, maybe, she has a safe space where I can work from out of hours. Securing this thing in and out every evening was not really what I signed up for.”

* * *

The message to Charlie from Anne-Marie came a little later than expected, and of course Charlie had the perfect safe space.

*Hi Anne-Marie*

*Sorry for all the restrictions, mother insisted :-)*

*Of course I have a place where you can work on all this safely, my library, it has good facilities and means we can see each other for a few hours a week. I can send a car around anytime you want, it’s about a fifteen minute drive from your office. Even better, why don't you time it for dinner and you can join us. My next two days are free. Here is a cell number, call it thirty minutes before or reply to this email and let them know where to pick you up. Really looking forward to seeing you again. So I can hear all the gossip on Michael.*

She copied the number into her phone, and hit reply on the email to say this evening after work would be great.

“Well that was easy,” said Anne-Marie, “I’m going around Charlie’s house for dinner tonight, and so we can have a look at the cipher. She is picking me up from here at four.”

“Works for me, we’re going to be here late. I thought we would have some more solid results, but nothing really makes sense.” Michael sounded a little down and hardly looked up from his workstation for the next three hours. Anne-Marie eventually brought him a cup of tea and a chocolate danish and sat talking with him until he finished both and she finally saw a smile. She then sat watching over his shoulder what he was doing until finally understanding the problem he and the team were stuck on.

“Hey Michael.” said Carlos, who had come over with Luc and one of the WOW scientists. "You may as well stop. The boffins have run the calculations and basically said it is unsolvable with our current setup. Everyone is attending a brainstorming session in the main meeting room upstairs to see if they can find a better approach. We are all invited and they are laying on a buffet for anyone who attends.” He looked at Anne-Marie, “but it is optional.”

Anne-Marie briefly thought about attending, but she hated all the ego’s and in-fighting, anyway the problem did not look that hard.

The next four hours were blissful, the whole area was empty, just her and Carlos’s soft Jazz playlist. The lift opened at 3:30 p.m. just as Annne-Marie was getting ready to leave, five people walked out, Jez and David disappeared towards the server racks and Carlos, Michael and Luc slumped themselves back down at their workstations.

She grabbed the briefcase, and her bag and looked across at them, singling out Michael as he looked her way. His eyes looked tired, his beautiful smile again missing.

“Michael, I’m off to see Charlie. Please get some rest and something to eat, you look really tired.”

“Oh Anne-Marie, ma Cherie, I’m really sorry I’ve been ignoring you. I’m never usually like this, but the WOW team promised they would have the data ready for the Fermi-lab guys on Thursday morning, and the only way we figured we could do it was if we went through the data manually. The older programs will allow us to split it evenly and everyone is chipping in, it's going to still take all week and some long nights. I really hope you and Charlie have fun, and I haven't forgotten our double-date with Sophie on Friday for pizza. I promise that takes all priority, I am not disappointing you and I am really looking forward to meeting her.”

She bent down and kissed him full on the lips, the passion in it totally breaking his concentration. Luc and Carlos, who were looking on unnoticed by Anne-Marie and Michael, were speechless.

“Oh, and don’t worry about your problem, my program is running through the complete project WOW datasets now, you should have all the data in the correct format ready to go in about an hour. I think you should let everyone know they can have an early night, I am sure there will still be plenty of other stuff to figure out tomorrow.”

She smiled at him and did one of her silly winks she was still trying to perfect, then she turned and left. All three men followed her silently transfixed as she walked into the lift and disappeared.

Jez ran into the area a few seconds after she exited and before anybody could say anything. “Hey guys I don't want to worry you, but all the GPU clusters in the data centre are running at a hundred percent, I’ve never seen that before, are you doing something?”

* * *

It was a completely different Charlie waiting just outside the building's exit. Gone was the tomboy from Saturday morning, the woman in front of her now was immaculately dressed, and immaculately presented, making Anne-Marie’s efforts to date seem comically amateur in comparison. The happy expression on Charlie’s face though was the same woman she remembered, and Anne-Marie smiled back.

“Anne-Marie,” she said, kissing both cheeks warmly and then hugging her. “You look wonderful! Are you ready for a bit of code-breaking?”

She took the briefcase off her and they walked together to her car, pointing back. “You know I remember that building from my first tour, it used to contain the tape and disk archives and then it was refurbished for the WOW project, mother had so much to do at the time we never really got to see much.”

Anne-Marie remembered who Charlie was and what she did as a negotiator, she guessed little minor projects like this wouldn't even register on Charlie's security scale.

“I remember reading the project treatise in 2018, it was to do with some anomalous particle signatures affecting some of the detectors. I remember thinking the project name was stupid, then my mother told me it was named after the anomalous signal that SETI discovered in 1977 which they thought was alien, but never repeated. When I learnt that, I thought it was even more stupid, as anyone making the connection would now probably assume CERN was looking for aliens!”

“I remember the goal was to trace the source of the anomaly, but also I wasn’t that interested. I stopped my study into theoretical physics at the older standard model because there was too much speculation, and I can only deal in facts not theories in my job.”

Anne-Marie was a bit stunned at how much Charlie actually knew, she really was a walking encyclopedia. How the hell did her mind work, to fit all that information, organised and indexed for instant retrieval? What would it be like to have Wikipedia in your head instead of on your phone? Charlie had stopped talking and was looking at her, was she expected to say something?

“If it’s any consolation,” said Charlie, “I thought project WOW would be one of the better jobs here as I much prefer detective work over proving a theory somebody else made up.”

“OK here we are,” she said, stopping at her car and throwing the briefcase on the back seat.

Anne-Marie, wasn't really a car person but this one was quite pretty, she got into the passenger seat noticing the crisp new car smell and comfy leather, it must be really expensive, what it must be like to never have to worry about money, she thought.

“You know, Anne-Marie, I’ve been looking forward to this all day, I have purposely not learned anything about ciphers so we can do this together, it should be fun.”

“Well I did have a look over my old notes yesterday, I really should have brought them but they are over in my apartment in Chevry..”

“Oh that's only a little out of the way, we can stop off..”

She could see the doubt in Anne-Marie’s mind, her apartment was so small and dingy, what would she think? Charlie sighed inside, yes it was nice not having to worry about money, but Anne-Marie could be bunking in a tent for all she cared.

“Er, I guess we could stop and I could rush out and get it, but we don't have to.”

“No it’s fine, it is only you and me this evening, and I would love to see where my friend lives. Also if we go to the other side of the airport it should be a better route anyway, just let me know where to turn off when we get close.”

She put the car in drive and silently glided off on Rte Gregory to the French exit.

“This is a nice car.” She said after they had driven about fifty metres.

“I got it because it looked cute and had an automatic gearbox, I’m a rubbish driver.” She glanced at Anne-Marie before returning her eyes to the road.

“Have you ever driven?”

“Yes, I got my driving licence but I left my little Renault Clio at home with my step-mother when I came here, everybody told me how expensive everything would be, and that public transport here is quite good. Mine was an automatic too, I guess I’m not much of a car person either. Michael told me of his little old sports car in the UK. It would terrify me driving on the wrong side of the road.”

“Yes, he mentioned it to me as well, the picture he showed me on his phone looked like a bucket of rust on four wheels, and looked like an accident waiting to happen.”

“Yes it was a bit ghastly,” Anne-Marie agreed smiling.

Anne-Marie was calming down, she was quite pleasant to talk to, her mind was so logical, like all the distractions were handled by another part of it.

“They do let us book the cars and bikes out at CERN, but they are quite popular and sometimes there is a bit of a waiting list. The second time I went out with Michael he hired a car and he drove us to the cable car at Fierney, he is not very confident driving here yet..”

Charlie eased on the accelerator seeing the route in Anne-Marie’s mind.

“Oh, take the next right and it's about seventy metres on the left, you don't have to get out, my place is a bit of a mess..”

“Anne-Marie, you worry so much about the littlest things, just because I am who I am does not mean I don't want to see where my friend lives, I’m sure it is really sweet.”

Charlie pulled into an empty parking area Anne-Marie pointed out was reserved for her apartment. She cracked the door and turned to Charlie who was already taking off her seatbelt.

“Are you sure you want to come up, it will only take me a minute?”

“Of course silly, we could have a coffee and you can show me your place.”

She followed Anne-Marie up a concrete stairway and along a terrace to an older white door where she got out a little gold key and opened it. The room was small but very tidy, not many personal items, no real ornaments or pictures apart from her framed degree and doctorate. There was a little Bluetooth speaker on a table along with a picture of a good looking man and young woman.

Charlie sat on the sofa. “Oh this is quite nice for a little bachelorette apartment and Chevry is quite a nice little village.” She looked at the picture. “Is that your mother and father?”

“Yes, it is the only picture I have of both of them from before I was born. They never married and father never stuck around. Mama died a few years after I was born. I lived with my aunt and grandmother after that.”

Anne-Marie walked over to another little table, picked up a picture of a different looking woman and a small girl and handed it to her. “This is my step-mother and step-sister Ariane.”

Charlie looked at the picture not seeing any family resemblance, then looked around not seeing any other pictures, and wondered where everyone else was.

“Does your family still live in Toulouse?”

“Yes, grandmother died six years ago, but my step-mother and sister still live in the family house, in a small village about twelve kilometres out of the city. My step-sister is five years younger than me, and engaged to a local boy. She was my only friend growing up, but she changed as a teenager and wasn’t the same person when I left for university. I think my aunt really wants grand-children, not a scientist step-daughter.”

She sighed a little. “We still talk a little, and I send some money home to her to help them both out, but I do not like to go back any more as it feels awkward, and I really do not fit into their world.”

Charlie saw that her thoughts were not hateful, just sad.

“Relationships go through phases, I am sure your step-mother and step-sister still love and miss you, and when you are ready to see them again, they will be there for you. My mother only has me, we have our disagreements, but it is hard not to love her. I also think she has given up on grandchildren, though if I found the right man..”

Anne-Marie was having pleasant thoughts of a little baby with Michael, wow their relationship really was moving on a bit quickly.

Charlie got up and walked to the little kitchen and dining room with a small bathroom to the side, again both were very basic but tidy and ordered. There was one other room, a medium-sized bedroom with a small double bed, again tidy and made up. Anne-Marie did not have a wardrobe, but a small set of drawers and a clothes rack frame, with six pretty dresses and three pairs of shoes.

“Oh this one is gorgeous, Anne-Marie,” she said, running her hand through a light yellow cotton dress, “you should definitely wear it next time we go out.”

“My new friend Sophie chose it for me,” she said happily, “she is a beautician. I meet with Sophie a couple of times a week now to walk or go for a coffee. Michael and I will have our first double date with her and her boyfriend on Friday. I'm a little bit scared of meeting him, but Sophie says he is nice..”

Charlie sensed happiness that she had found Michael, then found Sophie and now found Charlie, she could also sense the anxiety that at any moment she might lose one or all of them and go back to her quiet monotonous existence.

“I think your life is only going to get better with friends like Sophie and a handsome boyfriend like Michael. I hope he is your boyfriend now, I will be very unhappy with him if he hasn't asked you yet.”

“He asked, and I said yes, Saturday was the best day of my life and I really have you to thank.”

“He would have asked you anyway, it had nothing to do with me, I saw it in his eyes the first moment he mentioned you, and I may add he has very good taste. You should invite him here for coffee, maybe some take-away food, once you are ready to take the next step and you have a very nice place. Michael probably has nothing as nice as this.”

“I guess..”

“Don't be so ashamed of where you live my dear, it is very nice, and everyone has to start somewhere. Now where is the coffee?” Charlie knew the answer before Anne-Marie said it.

“I, I only have one cup and instant coffee,” she said sheepishly, “but I can make you one if you want.”

“Oh Anne-Marie, you really need to get your friend Sophie around here, you can learn a lot from her, you have all the basics but it is just lacking a little love.”

She shrugged, “OK you win. Grab your notes and let's get going.”

* * *

Simon had followed discreetly in an older, blue, well maintained Mercedes E class. He passed Charlie's car and casually looped around the street pulling up a hundred metres away. Neither he or Carl had detected anything in the past few days since they went on a higher alert status, but they remained vigilant, his eyes covering every inch of the road and scrutinising every vehicle that passed.

* * *

They drove the ten kilometres to her and her mothers Chateau, it was next to their vineyard and Anne-Marie made the connection that she was going there.

“You live at the same vineyard?”

“No, the winery and vineyards are part of the larger estate, the house is just through the gates ahead, under a kilometre from the winery.

They crested the hill and Anne-Marie saw the delightful fairy-tale castle and gasped. “You live in a palace?”

“Oh don't be so shocked Anne-Marie, I still live with my mother. Would you have moved out of your step-mothers house if it looked like this? Anyway it is not really a palace, though it was built by a cousin of King Louis the fifteenth, who was unfortunately captured and guillotined after France invaded in 1798. At least your apartment is probably not haunted, this is, and the place creaks like crazy!”

Anne-Marie’s logical mind did not believe in ghosts as they were scientifically impossible, but she was also reconciling the fact that she was not treating quantum consciousness with the same level of scrutiny. Strangely she was also concerned that Charlie might be genuinely scared of ghosts. It was touching!

They pulled into a large parking area near the west wing of the house, which had a dozen other nice cars and a large black limousine which looked to have a driver waiting in it.

“There's a garage around the back of the west wing with a big apartment on top where Simon, our driver and bodyguard, lives with his partner Mimi. He has been with us for nearly twenty years.”

They both got out, stepping onto a white gravel driveway, Charlie paused a second while they both got out then continued.

“Simon was mothers protection detail when she was doing a job in England. She thought he was so awesome, that she stole him from the British army, and he came to work for her here. He has been here ever since, and he is a pussycat as long as you're not a bad guy.”

“Mimi helps with lots around the house, but has passions to be an artist and has her workshop studio in the garage, that is why the cars are parked outside. There is also Carl, who has an apartment at my Uncle's place about four kilometres that way,” she pointed towards her left.

“Our positions are considered low-risk by the Swiss government so the two of them are assigned to cover the security for both properties as my uncle quite often has VIP’s stay. Of course with only two of them, it is an almost impossible task.”

“Is Simon the one who gave me the briefcase?”

“That's him, I bet you were a little scared. I told him you seem to have gotten over your fear of strangers, but there was also a chance that you might also probably faint before you said one word. I’m glad you didn't."

“I nearly did!” Anne-Marie smiled.

The two girls walked up the wide stone staircase, Charlie carrying the briefcase and Anne-Marie carrying her notes. They got to the large double oak door and Charlie opened it and they walked inside.

“Anyone, everyone, I’m home and I have a friend!” She shouted into the large void.

The hallway was large, with a big double staircase directly in front of them. Almost everything you could see, except for the grey stone floor, was either mahogany or looked like it came from a museum; Tapestries, suits of armour, watercolours and every other item that defined an old castle trope, was either standing, hanging or perched on something.

“The whole building has A-object list protected status, not as fun as you think living here. My uncle's castle where I grew up is much bigger, and had the same protected historical status. Imagine being a kid growing up in that kind of environment, where you dare not touch anything for fear of breaking it!”

A small Asian woman walked out of a room behind the staircase.

“Is that you Charlie?” She shouted in English, just before she saw the two girls.

“Hi Mimi, this is my friend Anne-Marie.” Charlie replied in French.

“Oh Hi Anne-Marie, are you the one staying for dinner?”, the small Asian woman continued in English.

“Yes, if that is OK.”

“Of course it is darling, I am making stir-fried vegetables and chicken, but I can make it without chicken if you are vegetarian.”

“Chicken is fine.. Er.. Mrs, er Mimi!” Replied Anne-Marie in English.

“Just Mimi.” She smiled at Anne-Marie, then turned to Charlie, “and Charlie, your mum is in the library with a few of her friends, and some guy from the music department at her university. Not bad looking but he doesn't look her type.”

“Shame! Are you sure you don't know some nice guy back home you can hook her up with? She says she likes Hugh Grant, you must know plenty of guys that fit that description back in England.”

“Home is here now dearie. Anyway, maybe she should just ask the actual Hugh Grant, it’s not as if she’s out of his league!”

She looked at her watch, “what time do you want to eat?”

“Are mother and her friends staying for dinner?”

“I don't think so. I think the man has come to take her friends out to the opera. I think your mother is having a rain check after drinking wine until god knows when last night.”

“OK, well if mother is eating here too then we can eat at the same time, Anne-Marie and myself are going to the lab, just let us know when it's ready, thanks Mimi.”

They walked to the right and into a long corridor blocked with a door at the end, another one of those entry pads was on the wall and Charlie held her hand to it until it turned green, clicked and the door opened. The room was large and looked like the research library at her old university.

“This is our private library and data centre, where we both come to research things. We only know what we have learnt and there is still a lot we don’t know or don’t have time to read in detail, hence our technical library. We also have our own little off-line data-centre and all the computers on the network have full access to read everything, a few others have access to write and execute.”

Charlie waved her hands vaguely. “The whole room is isolated and screened so no Wi-Fi or cellular signal can reach in or out, so your phone won't work here. Unfortunately it is a requirement if we are to process government or business secrets.”

“Wow, it is a much better work area than my apartment.”

“Stop knocking your apartment girl, it is nice.. just deficient in cups!”

Anne-Marie grinned. “OK so where do we start?”

They emptied the briefcase onto a large work table, the old notebook was in pieces and bound with a string bow to keep it together. Charlie waved it around carelessly, then threw it into the drawer under the table.

“I cut the spine off last night and fed the pages into the professional document scanner we have. There really is no sentimental value to anybody, nor history. I would have just shredded it after, but I held off just in case there was something physical in the pages that I missed, or something that didn't scan correctly.”

“That will simplify everything,” said Anne-Marie, “especially if we can put the pages through an Optical Character Reader.”

“That is done too. The pages are saved as consecutively numbered images, then I ran them through the OCR, concatenated them and saved them as a single text file.”

“So we are ready to go.” Anne-Marie said impressed. “Efficient. OK I have a few Python programs from my university days which should be able to analyse the structure.”

“Ha! Well that eliminates mothers' older workstation. She had a computer professor from Zurich as a computer teacher in the mid eighties. He designed and wrote the operating system and the programming language it was written in. Mother hates black-boxes so decided she had to know it inside out. It is kind of genius but also as dated as hell. Don't tell her I said that though.”

She smiled, walking past an older looking computer, patting it fondly, and then moving on and stopping next to a pair of large thirty inch flat screens. Anne-Marie followed and stopped with her.

“We probably need to feed my algorithm with some keywords to help, but we should be easily able to decode everything as long as it was not produced with a one-time-pad; Though I have also got the most popular works of fiction that are out of copyright and the bible digitised and my program will brute force even a one-time-pad if it uses any of these as a key.”

“Do you have any German?” Charlie asked and analysed her thought patterns. It seemed she knew a tiny bit, but it was totally useless for this purpose.

“None, sorry!”

“That is OK I can do that part, I have my step-grandfather's diaries, which were digitised after his death, he had some references to his father, and brother and other events at the time.”

“The program can do different languages and can use alternate dictionary files for its analysis.” Anne-Marie added.

“OK, I will start on a German and French keywords text file, and you get your analysis program downloaded. These two workstations here have the highest specifications and should have a recent development environment loaded, they can also both access the GPU cluster which I just got for the new AI I am training.”

“You will however need to go to the computer over in that corner which is isolated from the rest of the system because it has the only connection to the internet. It also has some special scanning software on it to check for viruses and will automatically download and check the checksums of any code you need from git repositories.”

Anne-Marie was a little bit impressed by everything. She imagined if they had someone like Charlie working on the WOW project, they would probably have finished it years ago, she must be amazing at her job, no wonder she was considered so precious by everyone.

Her mind wandered off again to how her ability was actually physically possible. Charlie’s mind must have some crazy wiring. DNA was an amazingly dense storage media and was structured in 3D, not 2D like modern memory chips. Technically the brain had trillions and trillions of bytes of storage, a whole data-centres worth. Biology however just didn't work that way.

DNA was not something you could just encode a random memory in at will, and databases, like any library of information, were all about indexes which needed to be updated constantly. Without an index, you had to flip through each memory for a match, like trying to find a book in a jumbled up library, Anne-Marie couldn’t imagine any kind of eidetic memory working without one. Biology, normal computers, and quantum computers worked way different to each other, and none of them fit how Charlie’s mind operated.

Anne-Marie's thoughts returned to the quantum consciousness arguments and theories, they really were just speculation with no hard facts. But, as she downloaded her code at the isolated computer station, she wondered if it was possible there was some massive part of the picture that science was missing.

Charlie had already catalogued the probable keywords, then added some other very specific, wartime and medical words which her great grandfather, as a doctor, and a Nazi, may have used at the time. She then also added another thirty one digitised books he may have had available. She was however more interested in Anne-Marie’s insights.

Quantum consciousness was another one of those blue-sky theories Charlie had dismissed almost immediately. However Anne-Marie was using it as a glue to connect the event at Geneva University to the event detected by CERN, and her scientific curiosity was still trying to figure out if it was simply a coincidence. She didn't believe in coincidences without rational computable explanations, but also did not want to be labelled a crackpot.

Charlie felt some sympathy for Anne-Marie, and it made her angry that she was too scared to ask the questions for fear of repercussions. You should always be allowed to ask any question.

“How can I transfer my code to the internal network Charlie?” Charlie almost missed Anne-Marie's quiet voice in all her musings, very unlike her, and she showed her the approved transfer process, developed by the Swiss security services.

“Wow, you really do take security seriously Charlie.”

“Well, as I said, you do have to remember the kind of data we are sometimes asked to deal with here. My mother and I are sometimes trusted with the fate of large corporations or even people, our contracts are very specific about total privacy, and we only ever work once all stakeholders have signed them, I’m sure you understand.”

“I don't know how you look and act so normal Charlie, you are a very remarkable woman.”

“Don’t sell yourself short Anne-Marie, so are you, look at what you have, not what you do not have, I am pretty sure you are smarter than me, prettier than me, and have a nicer boyfriend than me!”

“I am not so sure about the first two Charlie, but possibly the last one. I really hope Michael is the one.” She said the last bit in a little sad voice that Charlie found deeply moving.

“He is, trust me. Just keep being yourself and as you grow as a woman you will see this too..”

Anne-Marie was so grateful Charlie was now in her life, she made everything so clear and answered every question so confidently it was almost like she could read her mind. Her reassurances about Michael, felt like they were said from someone who knew it as fact, like someone who already knew Michael's inner feelings, analysed them, and sealed them in a contract with a gold ribbon wrapped around.

“I hope you are right.”

The two girls worked in silence for a few minutes, as Anne-Marie worked through the notes she made to initialise the programs. Charlie then gave her the keyword file, the additional books, and the older German and French dictionaries which excluded any word from after 1945.

“Well here goes nothing.” Anne-Marie began the script to start the decode programs. It was pretty anti-climatic, they could see the processor usage begin to rocket and a minute later a few more fans began to spin up.

There was a knock on the entrance door and Mimi entered.

“Dinner is ready, Gerty’s friends have gone to the opera so it will be quiet for a few hours.”

She then shut the door without waiting for a reply.

“Mimi is trusted here too?” Said Anne-Marie as her eyes scanned the program's startup log.

“Of course, Mimi, Simon and Carl are considered family, plus as I said, as each project is completed we delete everything to do with it, nothing is never written down, secrets rarely stay here long. The journal is the only secret here at the moment.. Anyway, we should meet my mother, let’s go.”

They walked back the way they came, Anne-Marie getting a little more talkative, and strangely not apprehensive at meeting Gerty.

“Is Mimi English then?”

“She was born in Hong Kong in 1975. She met Simon there when he was a sergeant in the British army stationed out there before it was handed back over to China, they married in 1997 and she lived in Herefordshire in England where Simon did his training. She got her British citizenship in 2000. She is nearly fifty. I hope I look that good at fifty.”

Anne-Marie forgot she was talking to a walking library, there was no ‘I think’ or ‘maybe’ in her vocabulary, unless Charlie intentionally wanted it to sound vague. She bet Charlie was even more infuriated about how her brain worked than her.

“Does she speak French at all?”

“Like a native, she has been here sixteen years, but mother and myself are fluent and don’t mind what she speaks. My mother and I actually speak Cantonese much better than Mimi now. Language for normal people is like any muscle, if you don't use it, it gets weaker, it is a little embarrassing for Mimi though.”

They arrived in a big official looking dining room, the table was set for five at the top end of a really long table and a brown haired, beautiful older woman, who looked like an older Charlie, was sitting at the head of the table. Mimi sat between her and Simon, the imposing black man she remembered. Simon was just saying something they could not hear while spooning some rice onto a plate.

Gerty looked up to the two women approaching. “And this must be Anne-Marie, come to help my darling daughter with her little secret agent project.”

“Anne-Marie, meet my mother Gerty. You have already met Simon, and of course Mimi.”

Anne-Marie was grateful they had changed back to French. While she was quite proficient in basic English, quite proficient and trying to understand people speaking it natively with all its idioms and weird pronunciations was very hard work.”

Gerty got up and approached Anne-Marie kissing her on both cheeks. “Enchante Anne-Marie.”

Mimi did the same.

“I think I’m still a bit scary for all that kissing stuff, but hi Anne-Marie,” said Simon, not moving, but waving his hand instead.

“I am really pleased to meet you all, I hope Charlie has told you I am a little bit shy, but I am getting better, the last three weeks have been quite exciting, but I am finally realising that people are not that scary when you get to know them, and can be very interesting.”

Charlie was impressed. From Anne-Marie’s thoughts, it sounded like it was the longest sentence she had ever said in front of strangers, and none of it was rehearsed, way to go girl.

“Is that a Toulouse accent I detect on you Anne-Marie?”

“Yes Mrs.. er Gerty, I have lost some of it since I have been working at the research centre, also I never really spoke much to people before.”

“And Charlie says you are a mathematician, working with the guys on the WOW project, that must be very exciting?”

[Mama!]

“..But let’s not talk about boring work shall we. The joy in life is not to work but to savour and contribute to its beauty and richness in the brief time we have here.” Gerty smiled as Charlie rolled her eyes, then Gerty handed the pot of rice to Anne-Marie who had just sat down to her right.

“Here you go Anne-Marie, your turn, I actually prefer it with the pak-choi but the local vegetables are not too bad either.”

“They do sell pak-choi at one of the local Asian shops,” said Mimi. “I think there are some locals who cultivate it here. I should have bought some yesterday but I forgot as I’ve been so busy getting ready for the water-colour exhibition.”

Mimi was putting a tiny amount of rice, some vegetables, and a piece of chicken on her plate as she talked. Simon had at least four times more.

“Oh, my bad I forgot the wine,” said Mimi getting ready to get up. Simon held her down, “let me get the drinks.”

He went around the table, realising most were happy with just water, and Gerty was still hurting from the excesses of drinking the day before. He poured everyone a glass of water instead.

“The water-colour exhibition is this weekend isn't it?” Said Gerty, knowing full well it was, what time it was, where the venue was and every other detail about it. Anne-Marie suspected it was just to be polite like Charlie.

“The grand opening is this Friday, so I won't be here then, and I told Paula I would be available all Saturday afternoon, possibly Sunday afternoon for the closing speeches, but they will probably be pretty boring and pretentious.”

“How many paintings are you displaying?” Said Anne-Marie.

“Only four Anne-Marie, the gallery is fairly busy, and lots of artists want to exhibit because two top European art critics will be there. These are guys who can make or break careers.”

Charlie was right, thought Anne-Marie. Mimi spoke French like a native, well a native of Switzerland.

The dinner was nothing formal, Simon, who had said next to nothing, bowed off as soon as he had finished another course of chicken, though the girls all knew the real reason was that he just wasn't really a people person.

Mimi related some scandal she had heard about some top car presenter not being invited back to the Geneva motor show next year due to improprieties with one of the organisers wives. Everyone then laughed over why they always said ‘improprieties’ instead of ‘sex’. Then of course Gerty and Charlie gave a brief rundown of the exciting parts of the Count’s birthday party, where there were no ‘improprieties’ they could recall.

“Oh Mimi, I’m heading off to Bern Thursday morning in case Simon forgot to mention it,” said Charlie.

“We probably won't return until after seven so it will be just you and Mama for dinner unless you want to invite Carl, or have a late supper. It’s to do with the American Interpol agent I am meeting on Wednesday afternoon, the university archives in Bern contain some historical records that aren’t digitised I think we will need to reference, also I will need to talk to professor Patterson..”

“Watch him, and wear trousers or he will be too busy looking at your legs, he’s a lecherous old git,” said Gerty.

“Thanks for the tip Mama..”

“Why is Simon taking you, not Carl?” Interrupted Mimi.

“He has set-up a meeting with the FIS that day, and Simon is more talkative and diplomatic than Carl.”

Nobody disagreed.

[Is this Interpol agent the one you think might be the same man from San Francisco?]

[I believe so, Mama. He called earlier saying he would arrive Wednesday morning, we will be using the meeting room in the town office, Alice has offered to chaperone us, though I doubt she will be required, he sounded quite sincere on the phone]

[They always do, dear!]

“Anyway,” said Gerty, getting up, “I’m off to play my piano, read a book, then hide from my rowdy friends, who hopefully should not be too rowdy when they return, as my man is under strict orders to bring them back knackered!”

Anne-Marie was still processing the last word which Gerty said in English when she looked her way.

“Anyway, it was nice to meet you Anne-Marie. Hopefully we will be seeing you around here a bit more often, and thanks for the dinner Mimi.”

[Good choice Charlie, what an amazing mind Anne-Marie has, let me know if you guys make any progress on the journal]

“Good evening Mrs Schneider," said Anne-Marie.

“Leave the clearing up to me,” said Mimi, “you guys can get back to your secret project.”

“Thank you Mimi,” both the girls said simultaneously, walking back towards the lab.

“It’s seven o’clock now Anne-Marie, let's check the progress of the programs you started and then I will drive you home, this little project won't go anywhere and we can get back to it next time you are free, I must admit I am quite looking forward to seeing if we can crack it.”

“Me too, can we do tomorrow? I think I am going to take the next few days off, it really is too busy in the office, and this is more the kind of problem I really like.”

She stopped deep in thought. “You know I really wanted to be a code breaker, like that movie a few years ago about Alan Turing. It was so sad what they did to the poor man.”

“I never got to see the movie, maybe we can watch it together sometime, and yes, tomorrow is still completely free for me, just come around whenever you want.” Said Charlie, surprised at how verbose Anne-Marie was.

They entered the lab and checked the program. The first program had eliminated one cipher code then unfortunately crashed on the second. Anne-Marie’s face fell a little, it was her program after all. Her mind was already running through why and feeling a little disappointed she had let Charlie down.

“You learn so much more when something does not work, than when it does, that is what makes solving puzzles so much fun. At least your second program is running along nicely.”

Anne-Marie had a look. The second program was three percent through the laborious task of running the cipher text against a key and checking how well the output matched the German dictionary and keyword file. Such a task was impossible if the key was truly random, but an amateur would generally use paragraphs out of a famous book both parties had to encode the secret. There were still a vast number of combinations but modern computers were incredibly fast and Charlie and Gerty had the best.

“I really thought we might have gotten something by now,” said Anne-Marie disappointed, “maybe if I look at the log..”

“I think it is a great start, Anne-Marie, let your second program do its stuff overnight, and we can fix the error with the first program tomorrow. I think you need some rest and I do too, remember you’ve been at work all day and tired people make mistakes. Let me drive you home before it gets dark and we can look at the logs tomorrow morning.”

“I can drive here tomorrow, if a pool car is available, it is a bit far for me to cycle.”

“Well you are welcome to take the car I brought you in, it's a government car anyway and as you are technically working with me, you are by association also working for the Swiss government.”

To Anne-Marie it was Charlie’s car and she could sense her hesitation..

“You will have to get over your guilt about using these things my dear, a car is a tool just like a laptop. A high-end car is just like a high-end laptop, and you would have no problem using one of those if I gave it to you!”

“..But if you prefer a pool car I will make sure one is available for you. I have a teeny bit of influence with the CERN admin.”

“Maybe a pool car, I would feel horrible if I scratched your nice car.” She smiled, “but thanks.”

The drive home was all about her program and why she thought it crashed, Charlie dropped her off home thanking her for a great evening again and handing her an access card to get in through the entrance gate tomorrow.

* * *
