Carl followed Charlie while she dropped Anne-Marie off home, then back to the chateau, trailing two hundred metres behind until she was safely in the grounds. He then messaged Simon and went back to the large house for a workout at the well-equipped gym they had.

If it was up to him he would be next to her everywhere, but he knew she valued her freedom and understood that men like him and Simon could be a little intimidating to her friends. Charlie had been a little less reticent with her previous bodyguard, Harriet, as a woman she could dress down and blend in a bit more with the kind of people Charlie usually mixed with. Carl or Simon could never do that.

He still sometimes had difficulty working with the same values as Gerty or Charlie. Unfortunately their spooky abilities to detect and identify problems was sometimes not supported by the ability of the legal systems to prosecute the offenders. Once there was absolutely no doubt, Carl always preferred a more direct form of justice.

Carl had panicked a few times this week when he thought he had lost Charlie. The tracker in her Porsche installed by the government spooks helped a bit, but he wished at times like this that she carried a normal cell phone like everybody else.

He thought of his sister and mother, Charlie had offered to fly them here and let them stay at the Chateau anytime they wanted, but his mother was comfortable where she lived. They would never leave Norway, they had their friends there, and neither of them spoke anything but Norwegian, and understood a little bit of English.

His government's arrangement of loaning him to Charlie was for four years, the money he earned would set him and his family up for life. He did kind of like this job, and the guys, but once it was over he was going to go home, and live the quiet life.

* * *

The Priest had moved to a cheap rental house on the French side of lake Geneva opposite the chateau for the past week. He had dumped the hire car in the small garage attached to the rental and had hired a small motor boat for a week which fitted better with his plan. He was not taking any chances with being spotted too early and he had spent the last few days planning how he was going to get in that vault.

From his occasional motor boat excursions to the other side of the lake, and through his binoculars he knew that most of the guests were leaving and it should get easier, but he did not fancy tangling with the two male bodyguards that randomly seemed to appear. He had however noticed that there was a gap in their coverage. Only the black guy lived in the woman's castle, the other one also covered the bigger castle, four kilometres along the shore of the lake. If he could time it so the one bodyguard was at the big castle and the other was away with the daughter he would have a clear window where he could get in, kill the old bitch, rob the vault, get out by boat and leave through France before anyone was the wiser. He hoped that window happened soon before someone caught up to him.

* * *

At the same time in the meeting room in The ElderNacht’s Caribbean base, the head of The ElderNacht, group-leader Doctor Henry Franke, was fuming. His worst nightmare was coming true just near the fruition of his organisation’s great project.

The day before he was made aware from the lead scientist that both Fermi-lab and CERN were close to physically linking their detectors. The ElderNacht had known for some time about the CERN efforts and their lack of any progress, it had worried the group-leader so much at the time that they had blackmailed one of the lower level engineers in the area to keep them informed of what was happening. The engineer of course, was not really given a choice, and exposing his drug dependencies would be the least that would happen to him if he did not comply.

They however were not aware of the similar efforts by the American team at Fermi-lab until recently, and they had no informer at Fermi-lab so were in the dark. Now, the CERN informant seemed to think they may get some good results very soon due to some improved software they were now using to filter and format the datasets. The group-leader was worried that between the two teams they may have come up with a way of detecting the dragon, and if so, it could lead them right here to his base of operations.

The bad news had come on other news of the loss of another ‘test subject’. Her mind fried like all the others, and with zero progress to understand it. She would be quietly disposed of, even the men in the barracks wouldn’t touch her now.

After the execution of one of the scientists in front of the others last week as a lesson that he did not like failure, he was now thinking that fresh minds might be needed to understand this and all the recent optimism might be for naught. The old texts said the witches had controlled the artefact, the witch the Nazis had captured had used it to destroy herself and the second dragon rather than give it to them. The Vatican texts that were stolen with their dragon artefact said there were only two and that any demon under the control of Satan could control it, yet all they had was a trail of dead bodies.

Then there was the failure to kidnap and interrogate the negotiator woman at the airport and the unfortunate termination of another good man, Crane. If this negotiator had been captured a lot of his questions may have been answered. Instead now all the agencies were cooperating with Interpol, and yet another agent was looking into it. And now this final revelation!

“How do you know the negotiator's visit to CERN was to do with the WOW Project?” The old man asked his son again very carefully.

“My agent followed her a week ago, he was fairly distant but his equipment caught her meeting with the director and a follow up enquiry with our asset there said she had been permitted to look at the archived reports on the WOW project.”

“How did she make the connection between the Munich Operation and the WOW project? How much did Mendez know of the Artefact?”

He directed his angry response straight at Matheus.

“Mendez knew nothing that I am aware of, father,” came the reply over the screen a few seconds delayed.

“I think my brother is covering for some leaks in his operations there,” said Ahmed with some vitriol. “Our liaison with the jihadi group was a simple swap, money and weapons for the holy documents they looted in Baghdad thirty years ago. Mendez was smart, he would have known The ElderNacht would need to distance itself from the terrorists, I expect he knew a lot more than Matheus is letting on.”

“Fuck you, Ahmed!” Came the delayed reply.

“I do not care how it happened!” Screamed the old man, silencing both of his sons immediately. “The negotiator and the CERN operation are now linked for some reason, and have become an immediate threat to us. We need to get control of this immediately.”

He put his finger up in the air, silencing everybody.

“OK, here is what will happen. The two teams will go to Geneva to take out the CERN WOW facility as per the plan we agreed. I don't care if you make it look like an accident or an act of terrorism, I just want the CERN detector and everyone at that facility including the Fermi-lab scientists eliminated immediately.”

“Then, and only then, I want the team to take out the negotiators. I want the young one brought here to see how much she knows, and kill everybody else. Also Matheus, your thug in Geneva might now know too much, get rid of him too! It's a house in an isolated vineyard close to the French and Italian border so we should be able to get our team out afterwards easily. Just make sure that the next time we meet in five days, both CERN and the Negotiator problems are gone!”

“I will direct the operation myself, father,” said Matheus defiantly. “It will be as you say.”

# The Witch and The Dragon

ANNE-MARIE HAD BEEN surprised when a pool car was delivered to her apartment the next morning at nine AM. There was none of the usual rigmarole, and as soon as the man verified her licence she was handed the keys without any of the usual signature or paperwork and told to return it whenever she pleased. She queried the guy on what that actually meant, and he read an email from his iPad.

“It does not specify a return date, just that the bill was being picked up by the ministry of justice. So I guess just bring it back in a week, a month, whenever.”

The car was not one of the usual tiny Fiats either, it was a brand new white Audi sports car! Welcome to Charlie’s world she thought smiling, then wondering if she could keep it until the end of the weekend and take Michael out somewhere nice.

The guy showed her how to operate it, how to link her phone and said it had a full charge so should be good for five hundred kilometres and there was a charger at the CERN visitor centre she could leave it plugged into when she was not using it.

Anne-Marie thanked the guy and carefully drove the short distance to the Chateau just before lunchtime. She had briefly wondered whether to phone Charlie and see if she wanted a burger or sandwich picked up, then dismissed the idea as stupid. She could not ever imagine Charlie eating a burger and fries, and hated the thought of getting the brand new car, which had only travelled two hundred kilometres, smelly.

“Hi Anne-Marie,” said Mimi as she walked up the large stone stairs to the door. Mimi was obviously expecting her.

“When the gate opens, I get a warning buzzer in the kitchen so I know somebody is arriving or leaving,” she said smiling. “Charlie told me you were coming and what the car was, and I have just called her so she should be down any minute.”

On cue Charlie walked down the stairs behind Mimi. “Hi Anne-Marie, Do you want to grab a coffee before we start?”

“Aurelien was out early and picked up some bread and treats from the patisserie this morning. I can bring something out for you before I disappear into my studio,” said Mimi.

“That would be wonderful Mimi, thanks,” said Charlie as she and Anne-Marie walked through to the large living room and then out through a large set of patio doors into a beautiful patio overlooking the lake a hundred metres away. Anne-Marie looked back just two weeks ago, how her life had changed.

Charlie noticed Anne-Marie's dreamy countenance, picking up on the deep love for Michael, the friendship she was feeling towards her, and then changing as she reviewed the code changes she had made this morning in her head.

“I take it everything is going well with Michael?” Charlie asked.

“Oh, he and the other guys are still very busy. I talked to him last night, and the managers wanted me to go in this morning to explain the code I wrote yesterday, but I already have the leave booked for the next two days and I said I am seeing you. He said not to worry, he thought they just wanted to understand how I did it, anyway the time I bought them has now been filled with a hundred other things. So he is back on another deadline and is probably working much too hard.”

Charlie listened intrigued, catching the fact Anne-Marie was worried about all the excitement she had left in her wake. She thought she was helping Michael, and he said the code she wrote worked perfectly, it just might have annoyed those who said it couldn't be done. Carlos blankly refused to call her in off leave to explain to a bunch of sceptics, saying it worked, and she would probably write something up when she got back off leave, she always did. She was still worried however that she might be in trouble.

Charlie wanted to tell her not to worry, that she had a hundred contacts who would snap Anne-Marie up on the spot, but it looked like her manager had it under control.

Also Charlie was not worried about mother or her being somehow the source of this signal, as they never really stayed in one place much, so the locations they found would likely be all over the world. She also listened to Anne-Marie's beautiful logical mind and wondered if Anne-Marie really knew how special she was.

“I also think I found the problem with the code, one of the external maths libraries I use has a bug which I uploaded a fix for this morning..”

She was interrupted as Mimi appeared with a tray with two coffees and two scones with side dishes of cream and jam. “I also made some sandwiches in the kitchen for if you guys or Carl get hungry later. Oh, Anne-Marie, are you staying for dinner?”

“Of course she is Mimi,” said Charlie, “this is our big project day, and as they said in *The Martian*, we are going to science the shit out of it!” She said the quote in English making both the other women laugh.

“Well good luck to you both. I am making salad for dinner, it will be a bit of a buffet, and I think it might just be the three of us unless I can persuade Carl to Join us as your mother has gone to Lyon with two of her friends shopping, Simon is driving them.”

“Thanks Mimi, I doubt you will get Carl to join us, but we should invite him.” Charlie got up and walked about twenty metres into the garden and towards the lake, then opened her senses. Yes Carl was here somewhere, she moved to the left the same distance, to the right the same, then turned around to the house and shouted, “CARL!”

A large blond haired man in a suit and sunglasses appeared from around the side of the house where Charlie was looking and walked up next to Charlie.

“Carl,” she said, facing the two women. “Firstly this is Anne-Marie, I am sure you have met her, but she has not yet met you. Anne-Marie, this is Carl, my amazing and under-appreciated bodyguard,” she said loudly in English so the two women could hear.

Carl shook his head, nodded at Mimi and looked at Anne-Marie, giving the faintest of smiles for Charlie's sake. “Pleased to meet you Ma’am.”

“Pleased to meet you Carl,” replied Anne-Marie in her usual demure fashion.

“And secondly Carl, would you like to join us for dinner tonight? With Simon not here us three ladies are in need of a bit of protection.. Please!”

“I need to..” He started.

“Please Carl,” she said quietly, “you never join us, and you really don't have to say or do anything, and I would really like you there instead of hiding away. If you do, I promise I will carry that little tracking thingy in my backpack for the next week.” She touched his arm.

“OK Ma’am, but I need to get back to checking the grounds if it is OK.” His eyes strayed left and right like he was looking for something.

“Thank you Carl. Anne-Marie and myself will be in the secure library all afternoon and Mimi is going back to her studio.”

He nodded to Charlie, to Mimi and to Anne-Marie then walked back purposefully the way he came. Charlie walked back and sat down.

“He is so bloody handsome,” said Mimi. “He could probably have any woman he wanted, yet I have never seen him with one. Do you think he is gay?”

“Not at all,” said Charlie, “he is just a bit like Anne-Marie used to be, very shy.”

On seeing Anne-Marie’s startled look she added, “well maybe not quite like Anne-Marie, Carl has no problems with crowds of people, I guess in the same way great white sharks have no problems with shoals of fish. He just isn't a very good communicator.”

“Wow, it’s almost like that movie the bodyguard,” said Anne-Marie, “but Carl is much more handsome and scarier than Kevin Costner.”

“I don't think Carl could get a talking part in any movie,” said Mimi causing the other two to laugh hysterically.

“Anyway I am off, remember dinner at six.”

* * *

“It’s got a match!” Said Anne-Marie a little excitedly.

Charlie had been examining the program code with explanations from Anne-Marie on the history of hand-written ciphers. It seems it was a little passion of hers and Charlie seriously thought this girl could get ten times the money she was on at CERN. She actually found herself hoping that CERN did screw up and she could get her something a lot better, as the computer program she had written was probably the most well formed program she had ever seen, and Charlie had analysed a lot of computer code.

Anne-Marie had wheeled her chair over to the large dual screen system and was bringing up a text document on the second screen.

“It's the second program,” Anne-Marie said, “the one that has been running overnight, looks like he used a book to encode his journal. It’s a German book I’ve never heard of.”

Charlie was so excited that for one of the first times in her life she actually forgot that Anne-Marie could not read German.

“Oh sorry Anne-Marie, let me bring up a French translation.. Oh it only does German to English and English to French, not German to French,” she said going through the menu options, “that's a bit crappy! OK let's try it and I can fill the gaps on any of the mistakes.”

The translator was actually a bit rubbish, but Anne-Marie said she could live with the English translation which only had a few easily correctable mistakes.

“Oh My!” Said Anne-Marie.

* * *

*Writing my journal in code has become very necessary at this point as I realise I have made a very big mistake. The Nazi ideals I originally eschewed have turned into a nightmare and are going against all the medical ethics I have ever learnt. I now see that my family, especially my father, were right and I hope and pray one day he will understand and forgive me.*

“This is not what I expected,” said Charlie quietly, slumping into her chair, her eyes focussed on the original German text not the translation that Anne-Marie was looking at.

“Is the text I am reading the same?”

“Yes, it is mostly grammatical errors, but where it says Nazi in the original text, it has substituted German, that is sloppy and insulting, here let me.”

Charlie moved the keyboard over in front of her and pulled up some menus then scrolled through a list, “OK, that should fix that.”

She hit the button and the text was replaced in the translation, she scanned the first page. “Good enough.” Then the two women continued to read.

*I have not heard from my family for two years now. The German war effort continues to be unstoppable and to get away from the ghastly experiments some of my fellow doctors have been doing, or aiding the war effort I took the assignment at Schloss Neuschwanstein as the facilities doctor. The castle has been re-purposed by the Nazi ERR branch to collect all the cultural artefacts it has been looting from its conquered lands. The people here are mainly scholars, but tension is high and a lot of high ranking SS people are coming and going and nobody trusts anybody so I still need to be careful.*

“Schloss Neuschwanstein?” Said Anne-Marie.

“It is a castle in southern Austria. Mother and I went to a wedding there when I was six years old. I remember it was a beautiful building, but I think they must have kept this part of its history from the guests at the time.. Especially the six year olds.”

The two women scanned through the rest of the page where it talked about the rooms in the castle where he ran his surgery from then scrolled forward a screen.

“This is interesting!” Said Charlie, still looking at the original German and realising she was reading a bit faster than Anne-Marie whose native language was not English. “At the bottom.” She pointed at the English paragraph at the bottom of Anne-Marie’s document.

*There is great excitement with some of the scholars. The leader of the ERR, Alfred Rosenberg, returned today from Yugoslavia with five truck loads of artefacts looted from some ancient castle there. Apparently there was some great battle there and a lot of German troops and SS troops were killed, they are saying by magic. The SS are very interested because the castle was occupied by a witch who was protected from the magic.*

*The Fuhrer was informed and demanded her capture alive, his obsession with the occult borders on fanatical, but of course nobody would say that or think to disobey him. It is said he is going to come here once she has been put under control. There are rumours already that some of the artefacts found are also magical. Total nonsense, but these are the times we now live in when science is secondary to logic.*

“Magic? A witch? Is this another translation problem?” Said Anne-Marie looking at Charlie for clarification.

Charlie was looking and re-reading the paragraph, then shrugged. “No, he actually used the words witch and magic, but he is talking about what other people were saying, not what he thought. History does bear out that Hitler was obsessed with the occult, and as I remember it was the whole premise of the first Indiana Jones movie.”

The two continued reading, Charlie was reading faster and waited for Anne-Marie to catch up to scroll to the next page.

*They have asked me to examine the so-called witch. None of the soldiers will go near her despite her great beauty, It seems there have been several attempted rapes of the woman by her guards. I am ashamed of being a man when I hear such tales, but it seems all the men who tried to abuse her were left screaming wrecks. One of her jailers jumped out of the window, another picked up a gun and shot his friend in the arm before shooting himself. The injured one was locked in a cell in the deepest depths, still screaming. I have been asked to examine him after I have looked at this so-called witch.*

*I am so ashamed to be human. A naked woman was brought into my surgery in chains, by two armed SS guards. Her body is scared and bruised from repeated abuse. They padlocked her to the operating table, gave me the key then left the room, it seems nobody wants to be near this woman. I have wrapped a sheet around her and treated the wounds as best I could, but she speaks no German. And has not said a word since arriving.*

*I figured out a temporary solution and let the guards know she may be carrying some new strain of venereal disease, which should keep them and those in charge away for a while. I do not need to examine her though, it is clear the woman has been abused and beaten. I am writing my journal now with her watching me. Her beauty is difficult to see through the abuse but I imagine behind those dark eyes, ripped clothing, filthy skin and the wild brown tangled hair she may indeed be a fair maiden.*

“Oh my god, the poor woman! What brutes.” Anne-Marie was getting a little upset, as was Charlie. A bad feeling had also started to form in Charlie’s mind and she almost dreaded going on.

“Anne-Marie, I think this is going to get much, much worse. I think I can guess who the woman is and the ending, and it is not going to be pleasant. Even I am having difficulty reading this and I expect I will not sleep well tonight.”

“Also my mother needs to read this before us, she is closer to it, and she is a lot more hardened than you or me. What say you pat yourself on the back for a job well done and we go and walk the dogs around the vineyard and soak in the rest of the day until dinner?”

The faint nods, and still slightly disturbed face of Anne-Marie said that this was most definitely the option she would prefer.

* * *

They had a pleasant walk. Anne-Marie talked mostly for once as she went over the ingenuity of some of the older ciphers, she went into a lot of detail about one she designed when she was an undergraduate that she thought was unbreakable. Charlie suspected the world had a lot of Anne-Marie’s, closet geniuses, content to work on problems, never noticed by anyone and paid peanuts, or their talents wasted working out how to get a few more clicks on a web site.

Dinner was pleasant, nobody pressured Carl to say anything and he was content to listen as they all spoke in English with the occasional translation of something for Anne-Marie. Carl’s French language skills were still terrible even after one year, and he seemed to default to English when people had even a splattering of it. Charlie kind of blamed herself for dragging him away from Norway, she tried to work French into their brief conversations and she even engaged a tutor for a while, but she just had to face the fact, Carl was great at everything but was not a talker.

She had caught a thought from him a little earlier, and during a small pause near the end she had asked him about maybe doing some self-defence training. His answer surprised everyone, when he laughed heartily and said quite flatly NO. Also, in what she considered his first ever joke, he suggested she take up running instead.

* * *

After Anne-Marie left, Charlie spent the rest of the evening reading a bit more of the journal and tidying up some minor grammar issues. She had copied the original German text to a tablet and sat in the living room on the sofa as her mother walked in just after nine pm.

“Finally they’re all gone and we have the house to ourselves again.”

Gerty looked at Charlie's curious smile, trying to parse what it meant. As she got nearer, her eyes lit up.

“The journal?”

“All of it! One of Anne-Marie’s custom written programs cracked the code, the woman deserves a medal. I think you are going to be surprised and shocked, but for once in a good way.”

Gerty sat down next to her and read the document up to the part Charlie and Anne-Marie had gotten to, all the time with tears in her eyes, and cursing herself that she had not tried this before.

“Do not beat yourself up Mama, the cipher Gustov had used was based on a one-time-pad and technically uncrackable. But in the sweetest of ironies, Gustov used Hitler’s own book ‘Mein Kampf’ to encode it, Anne-Marie’s program had to work through dozens of other books and several billion permutations to get there. When you looked at doing this in the late seventies no computer would have stood a chance, even if you had a hint on what cipher type it was.”

Gerty damped down the tears with her handkerchief. “Anne-Marie has done our family an amazing service, I doubt I can ever thank her enough. Knowing my grandfather might not have been the monster everybody thought he was should help me sleep better at night..”

“Oh Mama,” Charlie grinned, “you haven’t even gotten to the good parts yet, keep reading.”

*July 1941*

*My talk of her having some form of disease has allowed me to lock the woman into one of the spare patient rooms, and it has also kept the guards and the scholars away. I think they are actually quite happy she is somebody else's problem.*

*I have made her as comfortable as I can, dressed the wounds and given her some clothing, soap and water, and started her on simple meals of fruit, soup and bread. I keep the room locked not to imprison the woman but to ensure nobody else tries to harm her.*

*Her eyes, so full of fear and hate when she first arrived, have turned to sadness and resignation. I don't know how to communicate with her but I think she understands I mean her no harm.*

*The man she is said to have cursed, is totally mad by any definition and I doubt anything can be done for him. How he became mad is another matter. The guards who went into the woman’s cell to check the screaming found the two dead guards and the third guard curled in the corner, rocking himself screaming, and holding his injured arm. The woman was naked, unchained and just staring at them with those eyes. That seems to be when they brought her to me.*

*There is no room for mad people in the new Nazi Germany, so the mad guard was shot yesterday, and his body thrown off the high wall to the forest below. The wild pigs there are not fussy about the mental status of their food.*

*The commandant visited my surgery today. I told him I was keeping her locked up and under mild sedation most of the time, and that some of her symptoms were the same as the man that went mad, but probably only contagious if she has sex or she scratches somebody. I also told him plainly that I have not seen any ‘witch-like powers’ whatever they are supposed to be.*

*He wants me to remain in charge of her health saying the fuhrer would be very unhappy if she died, he also wants to take her soon to one of the rooms with some of the magical artefacts. It was one of the artefacts in particular, the one that she was found with, and which he said killed thirty men with some sort of curse. Again I am sceptical and reluctant to get involved in any of this Nazi horseshit but I am between a rock and a hard place, and until I can work our escape plan I need to at least look to be complying.*

*I cannot communicate with her. I have tried to teach her German by saying the words for the different foods as I gave them to her or the parts of the body as I dressed her wounds. The only words she had ever uttered to anyone else it seems were screams.*

*I am afraid of what will happen if they try to take her from here, but the commandant and the director are away for another three days in Rome. I have been told when they return they will be accompanied by a senior Nazi and expert on occult matters. I was also told his visit is specifically to see the witch and the artefact she used to kill the men, but also that she must be ready when he arrives. Nobody will discuss with me the artefact in question, it is in the most secure and remote place in the castle, the deepest dungeon, a place even more secure than the vaults with the Rembrandt’s and the Michelangelo’s.*

*I used a Slavic dictionary to try and tell her not to kill anybody, that I would try and keep her safe, and that when she was finished she would come back here with me, but she made no word, no indication she had understood anything. A sudden thought hit me, that maybe the woman was mad too, that I was seeing another symptom of madness and that nothing I ever did or said would be understood. At that thought I wept, and the woman, in the first ever sign of recognition, looked into my eyes and touched my arm. To this day I cannot explain the emotion of calmness that came over me, and the feeling of love I had for her. It was not a lust or a desire, but that of a great friend comforting me. We stayed like this for maybe an hour until my orderly arrived with the evening meal.*

“What do you make of her being able to affect my great-grandfather like this, and to make other men go mad?” Said Charlie, "do you think it could be similar to my ability to project emotions?”

“It may be,” said Gerty, “people within half a kilometre felt your emotions when a mouse ran out in front of you. If god forbid, you ever suffered the way this woman had I can't imagine what people close to you would feel.

“It was a rat Mama, a really big scary rat!” Her mother always made her episode into a joke, but even Charlie found it funny looking back.

“So it looks like this woman may somehow be my grandmother,” said Gerty. “She shows both the symptoms of my mother and the abilities we have.”

“Then it is likely she could read my great-grandfather's mind and somehow sensed he was trying to help her, and without language it was her way of telling him she knew he was trying to help.”

“We are making assumptions Charlie, let us read to the end and we can speculate then.”

“You are right as usual mother, it's just that knowing this story does not have a happy ending makes me want to stop now.”

Gerty touched her hand and together they read on.

*Today I received the word Marija when we touched, it is an old Slavic girl's name and when I spoke it out aloud to her, I received the faintest of smiles and I felt it was right. Despite the Slavic name, there are no words in the Slavic dictionary I have spoken that she responds to. I have however been able to add several other words to our little vocabulary using simple point, show and tell exercises and the emotions she returned to me. Marija is neither mad, nor stupid, in fact she seems extremely smart.*

*I cannot believe any of this is magic, there is logically some scientific explanation like a radio transmitting with invisible waves, or another similar phenomenon. I also cannot reveal any of this to the Germans, in fact I have a duty to ensure this woman never gets to be used by the Nazi’s as who knows what they will be able to do with her. We really need to escape soon.*

*Today took a massive step back, Marija is near death. A bullet from a guard's pistol pierced her side causing massive blood loss and possible internal injuries. The Director is livid with the guards and I was given an ultimatum by the SS gruppenführer, that if the witch dies then so will I.*

*It happened when Marija became very agitated as the SS guards burst through the surgery door, and demanded she be chained up for the march to the artefact chamber. My protests did not just fall on deaf ears but one of the guards actually came up to me and struck me with his rifle. I did not know what happened after that until some smelling salts were administered.*

*They say only five minutes passed, but when I woke Marija was bleeding from massive blood loss, one of the SS guards was dead and the other was half-crazy, the man who died looked to have repeatedly battered his head against the brick wall, the other guard who went crazy shot Marija before fleeing, then shooting another soldier before getting shot himself.*

*The wound was serious. I stayed by her for the next twelve hours, repeatedly setting up transfusions using the small stock of O positive blood and plasma we had. She also has a fever, and I administered sulfonamide in the hope it will stave off any infection. I fell asleep at her bedside exhausted and not knowing if she, or I would be alive or dead the next day.*

*I awoke with a warm feeling of love permeating my brain and a pounding on the door to the surgery which I had locked. Marija is alive but weak, and will likely need weeks to recover. The SS gruppenführer impatiently waiting at the door is relieved, he now doesn't have to tell the fuhrer of her death, and he instructed me to stay with her until she is fully recovered.*

*They are now sending my normal patients to the doctor in Alterschrofen three kilometres away and I have been able to write my journal for the last few days in relative peace while looking after Marija.*

*Her vocabulary is amazing, I tell her a word once and she never forgets it, and though she never speaks, she understands and she tells me yes, or no through the same simple emotions she has been able to transfer to me. She is also healing amazingly fast, I have told her to pretend to be more ill than she is so the bad men stay away, thankfully she understands I am not one of them. Nobody will come near her now, the guard who went mad made a big impression on everyone how scary she could be, but it also means her reputation is getting to some more serious ears in the Gestapo and SS. I fear she will be taken away if we don't escape soon.*

*I woke up this morning in the same bed as Marija and naked. I am shocked and appalled at my lack of self control. I tried, god how I tried to walk away from her, but it was like my thoughts kept collapsing into a single emotion, one of lust. Marija would not let me go holding on to me throughout, her body more passionate and sensual than any lover I have had before. While I love the woman with all my heart all I feel is a deep sense of shame for my actions. Yet it was only when she fell asleep in my arms that I felt my muscles finally able to untangle themselves from her embrace, yet I did not move from her side and we both slept ..*

“Oh my!” Said Gerty, “could Marija really have had that kind of power over my grandfather or was this a case of the truth is always written by those left alive?”

“I think it is the truth Mama, sex is an even greater emotion than pain and I believe it would be possible if she felt it strongly enough.. And don’t look at me like that Mama, yes I could probably do it, but the thought of forcing someone to have sex like that would sicken me.”

Gerty nodded a little ashamed at her thought. “If the journal entry is true, we have no idea what was going through the woman's mind. If she was like my mother Heidi, she may have been totally ignorant of sex, or mentally immature, and not have even known what she was doing when she initiated it. Based on the fact she could neither talk, nor really understand Gustov, this may actually have been the case.”

“Let us continue..” I said, impatient to get off the subject.

*Marija is most certainly twenty or more years old and a fully grown and beautiful woman, though it doesn't help me stop feeling revulsion. She cannot understand why I am not as happy as her, she does not seem to understand the concept that two people should both consent to the act. Yet I cannot hate her, I just cannot, so I find I must hate myself instead.*

*She will not sleep unless I hold her, so I do, and I now sleep hearing her dreams, like static or hisses, like a voice on a badly tuned radio one you hear through crackles and static, yet the noise is also somehow comforting because I know it is somehow her language.*

“This is getting mighty weird,” said Gerty, “it feels like the man is tripping on something or is not all there..”

“Shh Mama, keep reading.”

*Yesterday we visited the artefact room with the director, a researcher, the senior Nazi in charge of the occult program, and, of course, the gruppenführer. Two better disciplined guards had been picked for our escort, but told to keep their distance from us and not to interfere unless it was life or death. I can see they are very uneasy in her company. Marija accepted the new arrangement while I stood next to her, if I moved more than a metre distant she would scream.*

*It is hard to describe what I am seeing, it is a very large, obsidian black, elongated skull with three ridges on the top and attached to part of a spine that seems to be from some sort of large reptile like a large Nile crocodile. The head however is too big, and the spine too long, more like a neck than a body, it runs two metres from the skull then looks to be roughly severed as though hacked. It looked heavy like stone, probably some sort of sculpture they looted, and must have taken three or four men to move.*

*The sculpture stands three metres back from a guard rail and I am told this is the occult object that had killed so many of the soldiers. I ignored the stupid statement and asked the director what animal the sculpture was modelled after, and he laughed at me, replying it was not a sculpture, but the skeleton of a dragon..*

“A dragon!” Said Gerty.

“Yes,” said Charlie, “this is where I stopped and had to wait for you.”

“A dragon? It could be a coincidence you know darling, lots of old fossils and such were identified as dragons, and it may also have been a sculpture and they misidentified it..”

“Mama, The Nazi’s were evil sons of bitches, but you have to remember the castle was also full of scholars from some of the best universities. I’m pretty sure one of the scholars would have known if it was made of bone or what skull it was from, but whether they would then speak up to counter the argument of a sicko Nazi with a gun is another matter. I have been trying to parse all the facts in my head up to this point and up until you arrived. There is something here, something that is not yet adding up, something I think to do with that voice in my head..”

“Now Charlie we promised we would keep the speculation until the end, without proof all we have here is a story that you are trying to read something into, circumstantial evidence is *not* evidence as I have told you many times.”

Charlie sighed, capitulating to her mother.

*The director was serious, a dragon. I think I actually might have laughed if not for the look of hate on my Marija’s face as she looked from the skull to the men in the room. Then she looked at me and the hate dissipated a little, changing to what I could see was a cold calculating dark stare.*

*She stepped back from the skull holding me and pulling me back with her. It must have looked to the men standing there that it was a type of primitive fear of the skull, one which their modern scientific, superior, Aryan minds refused to acknowledge. I saw the senior Nazi smile, a smile I never wanted to see again as long as I lived and he spoke.*

*“Ah, it seems our little witch is scared of the nasty dragon! I wonder if she knows of the second one in the Vatican, or that my brothers in the ElderNacht are even now trying to unravel its secrets. Either way, I am sure our witch here can be persuaded to help.”*

Charlie shuddered, her composure rattled by the mention of The ElderNacht and a second dragon in the Vatican. Her mother wrapped her arms around her. But neither spoke as they read on.

*It was all I heard. Eight metres from the men with our backs to the brick wall I felt my eyes close involuntarily, then I felt a kind of delirium wash over me, before a type of unconsciousness. Suddenly the back of my eyelids exploded in pain and I was again awake. Minutes must have passed in that blink. My screams cut off immediately, but those of the five other men continued through my head, they were the screams of madmen, the screams of the dying, staying there for minutes, then all cutting off abruptly to a deep silence, replaced by the hissing and clicking that I knew were the thoughts of my Marija.*

*I opened my eyes to the sight and smell of hell.*

*Marija is holding me and weeping openly, she feels she has done something terrible, has become evil herself. I do what I can to comfort her. She is now my world, the only thing I care about, the clicks and crackles from her mind change to those of waves rolling up a stony sea shore and I find that I am weeping too.*

*Nothing I am writing down makes sense even to me, nobody reading this journal in my future will believe my sanity, but that day I witnessed real magic. I try to reconcile it with science, but the five bodies I look at, look like they died of fright, died screaming, eyes open, teeth bared, fingers ridged like they were ready to claw something. My science has no answer, it has totally failed me.*

*Marija walks to the skull, I try to hold her back but I am just a spectator in my own body. She pushes over the weak guard rail walking right up to the skull and putting her hand deep into the open eye socket, she pulls it back holding what looks like an obsidian stone. It was smooth and round, and looked to me like a black Faberge egg, or some expensive Christmas tree bauble.*

*Before my eyes I then saw the skeleton reduce to a black dust. Like a dry sandcastle on a windy day, but a hundred times faster. The sculpture dissolves, first to particles, then to a pile of black ash on the floor. My last doubts that what I am seeing is magic are gone. Still holding my hand, and the round object, Marija turns and we walk out of the vault.*

*There is nobody alive anywhere. Bodies line the passages and rooms as I make my way back to my surgery, and despite the revulsion of so many deaths, I know our chance has come to escape.*

“What the hell are we reading here Mama!” Said Charlie's slow, shattered voice.

“I don't know my darling, there are only a few more pages, let us get the whole story before we go on.”

A brief nod of the head was all that Charlie could manage, mother was right, they needed the whole context. The two women moved their attention back to the next line.

*Without any further hesitation I grab the two packs I have prepared for this case, pushing all the food, extra clothing and sleeping packs I can fit. In the keep I steal a small unobtrusive pool car, throwing a few more bedding items, clothes and books quickly into the back seat, then we leave the castle in a rush for there will surely be someone on their way here.*

*We pass nobody as we wind down the rocky road, and I know in my mind that the fairy-tale castle behind me has just added a new fairy story to its history.*

*It is by luck rather than planning that we found ourselves two hours later in an empty house near the tiny Austrian mountain town of Nenzing. The house is a few kilometres from the actual town and a little run-down, looking to have been unoccupied for maybe a year. It had a small vegetable garden, a living room, a kitchen and a single bedroom, there was an outside toilet and a small barn. It has water, there are animals we can use for food.*

*The one old neighbour said it used to belong to a Jewish family who had been taken one night. I shudder having some insight into their probable fate. I say I am his brother and Marija is my wife trying to flee also. The woman seems sympathetic, but many are and the next thing the SS are kicking in your door. Marija just nods at me after looking at her with her dark eyes, and I find myself trusting the old woman will not tell.*

* * *

*There are no dates I can put in my journal any more, we have lost all sense of time, just the slow changes of the seasons. This small house becomes our refuge, it is where we stay hidden from the madness of the world, a madness that must surely one day end.*

*I don't know the fallout from the massacre at the castle, but I can be certain the Nazi’s now know that an occult weapon existed and may still exist, and if they have any leads from the disaster they will make all and every effort to recover it. Even if there is another one in the Vatican, and I dread there is not, I must keep this one out of their hands, I must also keep my journal encrypted because none of this can ever fall into the hands of the Nazi’s.*

*Marija and I still can only talk by me saying or showing something, and her reacting to it. I don't know if the muteness is a physical or mental thing, but nothing I do makes her talk. It is a slow form of communication but I now have all the time in the world for her.*

*She, it seems, is thirty years old, not the twenty I first guessed, older even than me. I have thrown off my initial guilt from our first coupling, I am very much in love with her, she with me. It is not how I expected my life to be. I wonder how my brother is doing, how my mother is doing. From my last communications with my father, there is no home for me in Geneva any longer. I made a mistake I can never reconcile, I rolled a dice and got a one, but maybe this life is for the better, a simple happiness with a woman I love.*

Gerty and Charlie were weeping openly as they read, holding and supporting each other. There was no need for words between them. The talking about fantastical magical artefacts, The ElderNacht, and the killing of hundreds was almost secondary to the sadness and guilt both women were now feeling. They continued through the last few pages.

*She told me the ghost of the dragon was their teacher, their jailer, and also their protector from the bad people. They lived alone in the castle, locals would bring food and also ask for their protection, for the reputation of the witches was great. It had been the case for her mother and her grandmothers before her, for hundreds of years.*

*When a suitable male appeared he would be taken as a mate, but the males would always die within a year or two of the child being born and the child would always be female; That is why there were no males in the castle when both her mother and grandmother were murdered by the German soldiers during the looting.*

*I try to parse the communication Marija is giving me. Am I destined to die too like these males? The thoughts seem to disturb Marija, I can see she does not want this to be true, and the thought of losing me is very upsetting to her. I too do not want to die, I want to be with my Marija, to see my unborn daughter live and grow. I however have something these other men did not, knowledge of the medical sciences, whatever this curse on the male was, it may not be inevitable.*

*She tells me the object is the dragon's eye, but without a vocabulary I get no further, just the scratches and static she would send to my mind. Marija keeps it next to her body all the time, getting strangely angry if I ask to see, or touch it.*

*I held it once, following the exhaustive and difficult birth of our daughter Ana, and when Marija was too mentally and physically exhausted to put up a struggle. The eye scares me to death. My first analogy to a faberge egg was about right, it is a perfect black sphere, slightly bigger than a tennis ball, and with a set of imperceptible markings around the centre that change colour, like oil sitting on a pool of water. It defies logic weighing literally nothing yet It is incredibly strong resisting any form of scratching, compression or disassembly.*

*The thing that scared me the most though is what it did to my head maybe a minute after I touched it, it was like my brain was electrocuted, every thought was turned into a sea of static and crackles and there was no conscious action I could perform even to release it. Once you touch the eye, you cannot un-touch it, it must be taken from you.*

*Marija took it from me maybe an hour later, every second holding the object seemed like a lifetime. The concern in her eyes however told me that her possessiveness of the object was to prevent what just happened, and she now seems sad, resigned to something. My scientific mind is being slowly eroded by my belief in the occult and maybe the gods for what else could have created such a thing as a dragon.*

*Our baby daughter, Ana, is as beautiful as her mother, but like Marija she is strangely silent, and there is not even any crying as she nestles in her bosom. I am concerned the artefact is kept so close to my daughter and I wonder what it is doing to her unformed infant brain. But I also trust my Marija, whatever it is, she has been through it before. I am unfortunately in uncharted territory and do not know what would happen should there be any interference by me.*

*I have fashioned the eye into the body of a small stuffed dragon I made. Marija is happy, and I see one of her rare smiles as I show her how it fitted into the small stuffed toy and then I sewed it up, wrapped in sheep wool for padding. She immediately placed it next to Ana who seemed healthy and content in its company.*

*I myself can never be content, I live a fugitive in a country whose ideas I abhor, unable to talk to my own wife and child, prisoner of an artefact I do not understand, and probably hunted as both a Nazi, and not a Nazi. I also sense a weakness in my body that was not there before, it could be our diet though, simple meats and root vegetables with the occasional fruits the old woman can give. I sense the country is not as abundant as it once was.*

*Patrols have become more frequent, we have been searched more than once, but we have been lucky so far, the soldiers doing the searching were not the brutes that worship the violence of the Nazi ideals, but men like me put in an unwinnable situation. The soldiers see a simple herder, not a threat, and Marija always eyes them closely as she now has a child to protect. I also know these soldiers were only one wrong thought away from a horrible death. Apparently things are now not going so well for the German army. Our old friendly neighbour keeps an illegal radio receiver and the woman tells me when we meet how the Americans have now joined the war, and the thrust into Russia has faltered badly. We both pray the Nazi disease will be excised soon, both our hopes is that its cancer will not destroy the body that is the German people before it is removed.*

* * *

*I scribble these last notes as I sit in my old bedroom at my old family house in Geneva. Marija is dead, shot by a fleeing band of deserters four weeks ago while she protected our daughter and me. I heard the screams and shots from under the hay in the barn where I hid like a coward with my baby while the woman I loved stood up, and using the dragon, killed eight men after receiving the fatal shot. There were no words I could say, nothing I could have done for I am the shadow of the man I was. A weakness has been taking over my body for the last six months that I do not understand, and for the past week I have barely been able to walk. I know I now only have days to live.*

*The journey to Switzerland was my final act. My journey from there to my family, my swansong. The travel was predicated by the kindness of strangers, Germans, Americans and Swiss. I have passed through towns full of misery, full of death; this will be the true legacy of the Nazi party and of Adolph Hitler.*

*Getting my beautiful daughter to my family was my last hope for a normal life for her, and will most likely be my final journey. I have not eaten for ten days, everything I had I gave my daughter but she is also weak and I recognise the symptoms of malnutrition.*

*I do not want the legacy of the dragon to haunt my daughter or to make her different. The dragon doll was with her the whole journey, I could sense the distress if I ever tried to take it, and with little else to comfort her I left it with her. Now I am here, she was too weak to resist when I took it off her; my last task before I exit this world is to put it in a strongbox and hide it securely in the secret place only Kurt and I know about. I am hoping it will never be found.*

*It may be too late for my Ana, but after this I will pen my last request of my family, to take her in, to help her, and to forget their son ever existed. I can only hope she has a normal life, a life where she is loved, like she was loved by me, and by Marija, and If these notes are ever decrypted, maybe someone can find it in their hearts in the future to forgive me.*

* * *

The tablet fell from Gerty’s hand to the floor as she read the last sentence, tears streaming from her eyes. Charlie nestled herself into her mother's chest and the two women stayed like this for several minutes until both their tears dried up.

It was her mother who finally lifted Charlie off her and got up. It was now almost midnight but neither woman was tired. Gerty disappeared for a few minutes then came back with two cups of tea and sat down next to her daughter.

“What are we going to do?” She said to Charlie in a resigned sad voice, a voice Charlie had never once heard her mother use. Gerty had always known what to do, as had Charlie, neither of them had ever imagined they would be so far out of their depth.

“I don't know Mama,” she said honestly, taking the large steaming mug, “the journal could have been penned by the brothers Grimm, yet the facts we know about bear out..”

“But dragon’s Charlie! magical artefacts that can wipe out a castle full of men!” There is nothing in the history I have read about which says that hundreds of Germans were wiped out at Neuschwanstein Castle. Nothing about a dragon in the Vatican. Yes history says the Nazi’s stored looted artefacts at these places, but..”

“We don't know everything,” said Charlie, “and there is an easy way to verify this.”

[DRAGON!]

Gerty felt her daughter’s thoughts rip powerfully into her mind and shuddered a little, spilling some of her tea.

“Sorry Mama, I should have warned you.”

[Yes] came the strange voice of her dragon. The dragon?

[What are you?]

[...] [...] [...] [...] [..]

“I have asked this question before of the dragon,” she said to her mother, simultaneously as the dragon spoke into her mind, “but listen to my mind, static, hissing, crackles. They are the same internal voice of Marija that Gustov described that he heard in his head.”

She knew the dragon would stop in a few seconds, but for once let the hypnotic static wash over her as her mother listened.

“Why not me though Charlie?” Said Gerty, confused. “If anything I was closer to my mother than you were.”

“I don’t know Mama, I just don’t know. But if the journal is to be believed, the dragon is not part of me, it is an object somewhere that works on the same principles as our telepathy. Not magical, as magic is just science we do not understand, and we both know from our own abilities that science still has a lot to explain.”

“And there are two of them, and the mention of The ElderNacht. There are too many coincidences Mama, too many questions.”

“No Charlie, I was aware of the criminal organisation called The ElderNacht before this, just not its origins. We are fitting together pieces of a jigsaw puzzle, and a lot of pieces are still missing, there is nothing more. A mystery is only a mystery until you find all the pieces..”

She stopped suddenly, getting up.

“Oh My God! the dragon doll!” said Gerty, startled.

“When you were a baby you slept in Gustav's old room. It was refurbished and repainted when I was pregnant and set up for a nursery. You had a wet nurse who looked after you and lived with you there, as I could not produce enough milk. I lived in the room next door and you would stay with me when I was home, but between you would live in the nursery with the nurse and her child. I remember they used to surround you with all sorts of weird stuffed toys when you slept. What if they found his secret hiding place and the dragon toy during the refurbishment and stuck it in with your other toys.”

“I don't really have any memories as a baby,” she said, “but I went into that room three years ago and nothing had changed, they still had the old large crib from when I was a baby. Samantha never changes or disturbs any of the family's bedrooms unless it is necessary, the dragon is probably still in the big old toy box.”

“It’s too late now, and I think we could all do with some rest, let’s sleep on it until morning then go visit.

# Hidden in Plain Sight

IT WAS A FITFUL SLEEP for Charlie, and she got up early for a walk down to the lakeside to clear her head. A soft breeze blew in from the mountains in the distance reminding her that autumn was just around the corner. She had been standing there staring out of the lake for twenty minutes when she felt her mothers presence.

“Having trouble sleeping?” said Gerty as she stepped next to her looking out at the same lake vista. Charlie felt her mother's arms around her waist.

“It's a lot to take in Mama. I am worried that this criminal organisation may be playing with very dangerous forces and that our well organised little world is about to become a lot more complex and dangerous.”

“You took away a different perspective from the journal to me dear. For me it felt a lot more like closure, that a lot more of the pieces of the puzzle fell into place..”

“But the puzzle also got bigger, Mama, a lot, lot bigger.”

“It did,” she said calmly. “So like any jigsaw we just need to find all the pieces before we can solve it. Why don't we grab a coffee and drive to the castle early. Sam is an early riser and I’m sure it wouldn't take too much to get ourselves invited to breakfast.”

Charlie sighed, finally moving her gaze from the lake to her mother.

“I guess I just don't want things to change Mama.”

“Change is unfortunately an inevitable part of life,” Gerty sighed. “However I think helping your American Interpol agent, and finding out more about this criminal organisation just moved itself to the top of the queue.”

Charlie gave her mother a weak smile and they both turned and walked back to the house.

Simon was already awake watching them from the house as was Mimi who was just making coffee. Simon was getting ready to go and meet Carl and another guy at the Castle gymnasium for sparring practice, but delayed it so the women could have coffee and get dressed. He was impressed how quickly they both got ready, and got them both to the castle just before eight.

On arrival the two women were immediately accosted by Samantha and herded out to the conservatory for breakfast, and the inevitable gossip about recent events. It was nine thirty by the time they extracted themselves from Sam and they could go up to Charlie's old nursery room.

A hand-painted sign in yellow and green flowery letters on a wooden oval surround spelled the word ‘Nursery’ and the room was as innocuous as all the others in the corridor. Unusually for the castle though the room was kept locked.

A large old key was in the lock, it seemed to be stuck, and required a firm turn to open. As Charlie had said, apart from the odd dusting, the room seemed to be untouched in the twenty three years since it was last used.

The inside was dark. The one small window covered in a thick curtain, the pink walls ominously dark red. Gerty clicked on the light switch and the two obsolete CFL light bulbs illuminated the room throwing off a much harsher light than the older incandescent or newer LED ones.

The room was very small for the castle and had no real view through the narrow window, for that reason alone it would never be used for more than a nursery or store room.

The double-crib with its over-the-top wooden fairy-tale carvings and ornate designs stood empty of bedding and pride of place in the centre of the room. A single bed, meant for a nanny, or wet-nurse as in Charlie’s case, was also unmade and sat against the far wall with a small table next to it devoid of any personal items.

The large toy box in the corner of the room with a doll's house on it, was the only other item, and completed the picture of a room which had not seen much love for a long time.

“I don’t remember it being this ghastly!” said Gerty through gritted teeth as she led Charlie inside and looked around, “Marie always kept the room so warm and homely when she was here.”

It was almost disappointing. They found the old dragon doll wedged down the bottom of the box between about three other stuffed toys, old and worn but clean. Gerty picked it up slowly as though it was a bomb that might explode.

The doll was about the size of a cat, both comical and sad looking, made of some sort of green sack cloth, with two button eyes and stitching for the mouth and nostrils. She squeezed it carefully, finally finding some resistance in its body, and feeling a small shiver as she did.

“I know what you are thinking Mama but remember this thing could be very dangerous. I think we should be very careful when handling it.”

Gerty put it on Charlie’s old bed and both women sat down about a metre away.

“Can you talk to it?” Said Gerty, “if this is the artefact then try very softly at first.”

[Dragon] she thought very softly.

[...] [...] [...] [...]

“Oh my, I can hear it Charlie, I can hear it too!” Gerty said excitedly.

“No wait Mama. I am picking up weird images, with the words, it is like remembering a dream, it was just a few brief images that faded.”

Charlie again repeated the same soft thought to Dragon.

“Yes, there are weak images or something. Mama let me hold it for a second, it could just be I need to be in contact, remember your grandmother kept it next to her body.”

“She did,” replied Gerty, “but we also still have no idea how it works, it could be radioactive or anything. What say we get it home first and at least do some basic checks and set up some simple precautions.”

Charlie agreed. The library at home had a basic lab area with a few pieces of test equipment and there was a hand-held Geiger-counter in the basement shelter. Knowing the danger, it seemed prudent to do any further checks at home where there were only a few people, rather than the hundreds who lived in and around the castle.

* * *

The short journey back was in silence, a strange feedback loop of each woman listening to the other listening to the other, the dragon doll comically sitting on Gerty’s lap like a sick cat. Simon’s thoughts, for some reason, inaudible.

They restricted the tests to the more obvious ones, the radiation counter actually registering less than the background radiation as it was put next to the toy. The old AM/FM radio Mimi used in the kitchen tuned through the bands without picking up any interference over the usual stations, and their cell phones still connected and worked when placed next to it. They did all this while Charlie repeatedly woke it and listened to its soothing thoughts. Satisfied, they went back to the library.

“OK Let's see what this thing is,” said Gerty, theatrically putting on some rubber kitchen gloves and giving Charlie a wink. She cut the doll open carefully with some sharp scissors and slowly extracted a black object from inside.

It was beautiful, exactly as the journal described. Obsidian black, the size of a Christmas tree bauble, or a tennis ball, and with a strange pattern moving around its middle, like clouds just visible on a dark night.

“Gustov was right,” said Gerty, “this weighs next to nothing.” She held it lightly, moving it over onto the small scale on the bench top. “Thirty four grams, it must be made of a light element or not very dense at all.”

“Or maybe it is hollow.” Added Charlie. “It would be good to have a proper lab with an X-Ray..”

“It wouldn't really help,” said her mother quietly, before Charlie could elaborate further. “Unless it revealed a little leprechaun or fairy trapped inside, we are unlikely to understand what we are seeing. I think we need to bite the bullet, but as your mother I am super-reluctant to let you hold that thing.”

“As am I Mama. Have you even tried to talk to it yet?”

“Good point, let me try, I’m not as experienced as you but I did hear it so it might be worth a try.”

[dragon?]

[...] [your] [...] [is] [...] [dragon will not] [...]

Gerty moved it closer to her head as if trying to listen to it. “I just hear the same static but now with a few barely decipherable French words, are you still getting the images?”

“Yes Mama, different ones to before though, and just flashes that fade before I register them.”

Gerty took the glove off and held the dragon.

“Oh, I see the images now, but they go too quickly for me to register, I also cannot hear your thoughts in my mind anymore, it is quite calming like when I am out of range of everybody.”

“Ask it some questions Mama. Simple ones.”

Charlie heard some static which seemed to replace the usual thoughts from her mother, and waited for a few seconds ready with her rubber gloves to take it out of her mothers hands if anything should happen. But nothing did. Apart from the new factor of the images for Charlie it was the same as before.

“Well it doesn't really want to talk to me, but I think we have established it's quite safe to hold, give me the gloves and you can have a go.”

Gerty put the dragon down and put on the gloves again, then Charlie picked it up. The second she held the ball there was total clarity, but it was not French or any language Charlie had ever spoken it was distractingly calming.

[Entity Charlie, we are connected]

[Your name is Dragon? you are the entity I am holding?]

[I am not an entity. I am] [...] [...] [I respond to the designator dragon from entity Charlie] [...] [...] [...]

[Why did you not respond to my mother?]

[...] [...]

She wondered if the strange words were another language, they were not like the crackles or static she heard before she held it but seemed real. Maybe there was a translation issue, she could simplify the question maybe.

[There is another entity next to us, she recently tried to contact you, why did you not answer?]

[...] [New Entity Gerty] [...] [unconfigured] [...] [...]

Well that made no sense.

[What are you?]

This time there was no translation, just a series of symbols, some mathematical, some vectors, many she did not recognise, there were obviously still massive translation issues, they continued faster, blurring until she interrupted.

[Can you describe your function in a language I understand?]

[no] [interface] [no] [force] [no] [matter] [no] [compute]

“You are quiet Mama, what do you make of all this?”

Gerty looked at her strangely. “Make of what? What are you talking about sweet? You only just picked it a second ago, then there was some of that static language, and the briefest discontinuity in your thoughts.”

“Interesting Mama.” Charlie related the brief conversation, one she thought lasted maybe two minutes.

“So is it biology, technology or what?” Asked Gerty.

Charlie looked to be thinking, the static appeared again, along with a slightly longer discontinuity from her daughter’s mind. It was only a couple of seconds, then she replied.

“Mostly technology, a silicon, carbon [...] processing [...] matrix [...] [...] field [...] [...] [...] [...] [...] transceivers [...] vacuum [...] source [...] [...] nine-state [..] [...] [...] ..”

Gerty held her head up in surprise, interrupting Charlie. “What the hell are you talking about Charlie! Your thoughts stopped for a second, now half your words are meaningless techno-babble.”

“What? Dragon and I have been talking for nearly an hour Mama, you just stood there still while..”

“No you haven't Charlie!” said Gerty, concerned and looking at the black ball with some apprehension. “You replied to my question almost instantly, maybe two seconds at most, and your thoughts disappeared during that time.”

“Well it's definitely a technology, the [...] of a [...] ..”

Gerty took the ball off Charlie, a worried look on her face as she slowly placed it on the workbench. Charlie's eyes followed her first in a stupor, then suddenly awake.

“Now my dear,” she said seriously, “in plain French, what the hell is that thing?”

Charlie's voice and demeanour changed from her serious one to a more relaxed mother daughter one.

“This is going to sound unbelievable Mama, but the story I got suggests the dragon is some sort of probe.”

“A wha..”

Charlie sat down heavily on the seat.

“Hear me out mother,” she said, in an unusually confused voice. “I’m not saying I understood this properly, or even at all, but if we put aside the idea of magic and limit it to what sounds like science, then that was my interpretation.”

“Here let me give you a bit more detail.”

“I got the impression of two weird looking creatures, they were big and terrifying, like dragonflies but bigger than a great white shark, with six large legs that came out the sides. They had no wings but they flew, and they could disappear and reappear. They were not animals, nor any creature ever recorded by biologists, they were some kind of organic machines that moved across dimensions.”

“A what?” Started Gerty.

“Please Mama! I could just as easily give you an alternative explanation with magic and gods, heaven and hell, but, the pictures were of a second dragon and the image was through the eyes of the first dragon, this dragon. They were black, some kind of scales covered them with large ridges of hard bone rising over the head, like three tall mohicans.”

“Most of the conversation before this stage was maths, then science as it described the table of elements, and something like the standard model of physics, but physics with a different set of forces I could not comprehend. Then I started skipping it as the maths and the science got too difficult to follow, and I moved into their history. That is when I saw one of the machines dead, unmoving on sand, desert I think. I got no references to how it died, or their purpose other than for the entity to gather knowledge.”

“I skipped through time to where this dragon alighted on some sort of mediaeval fortress and was trapped by humans. It was, it seems, surprised by the actions as the creatures had usually fled from it in the past if they ever saw it. I saw images of soldiers with large battle axes hacking off parts of its body, of much death, finally of large men dragging what was left to a keep. It was caged in a dungeon for a long time.”

“The entity I think was the brain and all the semi-organic and organic parts of the creature, but it had died and rotted hundreds of years before, leaving just the technology. What Marija extracted from the skull is some sort of processor that channels forces across dimensions and into matter. Basically it is able to draw, store and manipulate energies from basically well nowhere!”

She could see her mother having a hard time believing her, thinking she had somehow misinterpreted, but she held her hand up pausing any response until she had finished.

“When it was extracted from the skull, the forces that bound the molecular structure of the skeleton were removed, and reverted back to their constituent elements. It tried to explain it to me but it made no sense Mama, I felt like a caveman being shown an iPhone, it would have been much easier to explain it as magic.”

“The communication with us is by one of those forces it tried to explain, all sentience modulates this force in a tiny way at an atomic distance, and me, you, and the other witches, as it calls us, perceive and modulate these resonances at larger distances because of a mutated neuron type we have. The dragon can also use this force across dimensions to both communicate and as a defence mechanism which it used many times to protect its entity.”

“The dragon itself however is not sentient, it does not consider itself an entity, just a device. A device which is more powerful than anything we will have for probably hundreds of thousands of years, but one which still requires sentience to operate.”

“Eventually after many years it was able to alter a human and detected a basic sentience. This was the entity it had no name for, there were many such unnamed entities, finally it perceived one called Es, then Alana, then came Casandra, Marija, Ana, who was Grandma, me, and I think now you.”

“You got all that from it in less than a second?” Said Gerty exasperated.

Charlie nodded, she looked and sounded tired, “I think the big question Mama is not that, but everything else, you know, the whole sentient alien machine probe thing, and the fact that the other dragon is likely in the hands of a narcissistic criminal organisation..”

Gerty sighed. “You're right Charlie, I think however we don't do any more until you get some rest as obviously it is doing something to your metabolism when you communicate like that.”

Charlie grabbed the dragon and put it in her bag, then went back outside with her mother to the sofa under the chestnut tree. They both drank hot chocolates and shared a large Toblerone chocolate bar, Charlie attempting to fill in the gaps in the story in her mind before she fell asleep exhausted.

* * *

Charlie was woken up by Mimi, still on the sofa an hour later.

“Hey Charlie, Anne-Marie is here. Her car is just pulling into the driveway.”

“Thanks Mimi, where is mother?”

“She’s in your library on a phone call with Alice, and has been for nearly an hour, I took a coffee out to her thirty minutes ago.”

Charlie thanked her and got up feeling surprisingly refreshed after her rest. She met Anne-Maria at the door and they walked through to the outside patio knowing the big question on her mind about what happened in the journal. Charlie had fallen asleep last night with this exact dilemma running through her mind, how much should she tell Anne-Marie. She didn't want to lie, but telling the full story would open up a million awkward questions.

“Anne-Marie, I’m glad you made it,” she said, sharing a hug. Anne-Marie was standing there in her pretty yellow dress, the one she had seen in her bedroom and a white hat, shoes, belt and sunglasses that were simple, yet looked quite elegant on her. She popped her sunglasses up onto her hat.

“I wanted to make sure you were alright. Also you did say I should come around so I thought I would come and invite you out for a walk and maybe we could get lunch somewhere..”

“Oh what a nice thought, Mimi was just making some iced tea and sandwiches for lunch, and I was going to have it on the patio, she said she would make enough for you but I can get her to stop if you have somewhere in mind to go..”

She saw Anne-Marie hesitate, she didn't really have anything specific in mind, just to see Charlie. “..Or you can just join me here for a light lunch and we can do something after that, my meeting with the Interpol guy is not until four PM.”

“Oh, I forgot you had a meeting.. I wouldn't want to..”

Charlie smiled, linking her arm through Anne-Marie’s and dragging her through to the rear patio.

“Oh Anne-Marie, you really have no idea what an amazing person you are and what an amazing service you performed for mother and I, in fact for the whole Bergmann family, do you?”

Charlie sat her down, under the umbrella, grabbing her sunglasses from her bag and sliding them on.

“Mother is so excited, so happy to find out that her grandfather was not the monster she thought. There are a few parts of the journal which are still a bit personal to reveal to people outside of the family, but if it was up to her she would adopt you as her daughter tomorrow.”

Charlie could tell that Anne-Marie felt really good that she was able to help, at no point did any other thought come up about wondering what was in the diary, and Charlie relaxed a bit inside, knowing Anne-Marie would not push it further, well at least for today.

“So Anne-Marie, tell me all about what the excitement is about at work and why those men don't need the smartest woman in CERN on the case?”

Anne-Marie smiled, about to say something when Mimi walked out with a large tray with iced teas and two plates of sandwiches. After laying everything out on the table, she left excusing herself that she had something else to do.

“It’s basically gotten too crowded for me. The other guys are fine in that environment but I really don't like all the new people around, so I took two days off. The other three guys are better at all that talking and networking stuff and I guess they will tell me if anything interesting happens or the other teams find something, but I want it to just go back to normal.”

“And what is the end goal of finding the location of this phenomenon?” Said Charlie as she moved an ice tea to Anne-Marie, and took one for herself.

“Oh..” Anne-Marie looked a little confused, Charlie could see in her mind that she wasn't sure. “I don't actually know. I guess they want to locate it so they can stop it, so it won't affect the experiments.”

“And what if the phenomena is natural?”

“Then I guess to understand it, see what it is, how it is produced, why it is so random, how they can reduce its effect on the experiments..”

“Interesting,” said Charlie, “that nobody has discussed this part of it with you. My work is all about conclusions, experiments demand conclusions..”

[...] [...] [...] [...] [entity Anne-Marie] [...] [reconnect] [...] [...]

Anne-Marie screamed at the same time as the unknown words from the dragon were filling Charlie’s mind. Anne-Marie was holding her head, and Charlie saw the problem immediately, she had heard the dragon. No. The dragon had addressed her!

“What is it Anne-Marie?” Charlie said, moving around the table and wrapping her arms around her.

“I, I, thought I heard somebody, something, but it was in my head, in another voice!”

Carl appeared around the corner of the house concerned.

“What is it?” He said. He had a small gun in his hand, pointed down, his eyes examining every angle. Anne-Marie's head was still buried in Charlie's chest and Carl quickly put the gun away before Charlie or Anne-Marie saw it. Mimi came out a few seconds later and asked the same question.

“Hopefully nothing,” said Charlie to the two of them. “Did any of you two feel or hear anything just a few moments ago?”

Both Mimi and Carl shook their heads. Confused.

“No. Just Anne-Marie’s scream.” Said Mimi.

“Same here, Nothing,” said Carl, wary.

“OK then I need to take Anne-Marie to the library to see mother. There is hopefully nothing to get alarmed about. Anne-Marie probably needs a little rest, and mother is better qualified medically than me.”

Charlie began guiding Anne-Marie into the house.

“Anne-Marie, you are OK, I’m here," she repeated softly to her as she walked with her.

“It was a voice, Charlie, not mine, in my head, it said my name.”
