THE MAN IN THE WHITE coat, barged into the meeting room before it had even started..

“Group-Leader Sir! It’s the artefact! It is awakening!”

“What! Are you sure?” Said the old man standing up firmly and staring at him. “When did this start?”

“About ten minutes ago sir, the technicians in the room started reading spikes in the EKG meters maybe an hour ago, and people next to it started to feel nauseous, now nobody can go inside the room without feeling sick. The rats in the cage we put in next to it are also becoming more agitated..”

“It must be the influence of the negotiator woman, father,” said Edmund, excited. “Some of the social media reports coming out of Geneva are speculating the negotiator may be a true witch, as mentioned in the old texts!”

“They are also speculating she is alive, and she is dead, social media cannot be trusted!” Said Herman nonchalantly, and in an uncharacteristic interruption.

“She is just a woman!” Said Matheus, firmly and angrily. “She was weak and feeble, the elder one it seemed died without a struggle and the younger one has done nothing but cry and faint. She is no witch!”

“It seems our artefact begs to differ, brother,” said Ahmed in a snide tone that made Matheus want to wipe it immediately off his face.

The group leader held up his hand in a gesture of silence, and seemed to think about it for a short while, then a smile formed on his face.

“Let us go and get this woman and take her to the artefact chamber,” he said at last to the men present. “She may be the key! We shall witness the resurrection of the artefact ourselves, and once we tap into its power we shall use the ancient scriptures to fulfil The ElderNacht’s destiny.”

He grabbed his cane, turning to the man in the white coat. “You! Ensure the keeper of the scrolls, and our chief historian are at the artefact chamber before we get there. Tell them we may have found the witch and to prepare for the ritual. This will be a great moment for the ElderNacht.”

He began walking for the door, his cane clicking on the stone floor, preceded by the man in the white coat, and followed by his four sons.

* * *

Anne-Marie woke in a large bed in an old, damp smelling-room that reminded her of her first student apartment in Nice. She was in serious pain from her arms and body, hungry, thirsty and somewhat disorientated, she had also at some point wet herself. She closed her eyes quickly, scared to move, and scared to wake in case anyone was looking at her.

As her last thoughts flooded back into her mind she wanted to scream, the memories hit her like a steam train, then shut off abruptly, interrupted by the voice of the [...]

[Anne-Marie. you need to pretend to be me! It doesn't matter what they say, you need to pretend to be me!]

It was the [...] but it sounded like Charlie. The capabilities of [...] flooded back into her, as did Gerty’s dead body, and that gunman, both lying there bloodied.

[Charlie. Oh my god, help me please, help me!]

Nothing..

[...] [Help me I need to talk to Charlie!]

[Anne-Marie, oh my god, are you safe? Please, whatever you do you must calm down. You are in great danger and the first thing is you must stay calm and assess your situation. Please tell me if you are safe?]

[I don't know, god I am so scared! I opened my eyes in a strange room but shut them, the room smells musty, god Charlie please help me, please!]

[Anne-Marie, you are on an island in the Caribbean called Costa Verde, it is where the organisation who murdered Gerty are located. We are coming..]

The door crashed open before the thoughts finished. She felt the movements next to her and she screamed as several arms grabbed her. Her eyes flew open and she saw two evil looking men manhandling her to a standing position.

She saw herself in a large mirror opposite, her beautiful yellow dress was drab, filthy, ripped and bloodstained and she wore no shoes. Loose hair dangled in front of her eyes, the rest wild and hanging down to her chest. Her tears came again.

“No! No! No! Please leave me!” She screamed in French.

She heard a laugh and a loud voice in English. “See! This is your witch Ahmed! I can see how someone such as you would tremble on your knees at her feet!”

She saw the large group of men behind entering the large room and more behind the door. A younger, slightly less imposing man with light brown skin turned to a larger imposing man with his fists clenched, his face angry.

“Enough!” Shouted an old man loudly, looking angrily at the two men.

He then turned to Anne-Marie. “If you want to see the next hour negotiator, you will stop your little act, one more scream and you will lose one of your precious fingers!”

The old man nodded to one of the men holding her, and a big knife came out of his belt and was held just above her middle finger and Anne-Marie silenced her screaming, leaving her shaking, weeping, and aching where she was held roughly by the two guards.

“Bring her!” Said the old man, flicking his head to the guards, as he and the other men filed out of the door. The guards followed, dragging Anne-Marie between them.

The corridors changed from brick to stone as she was dragged down a long passage. Every time she tried to stand, she would be pulled off balance by one of the men, causing even more pain in her arm and shoulder. The pain from her arms and every other part of her body fought each other for dominance and overwhelmed almost every other thought, except that of death.

It couldn't end like this, please, please, it couldn't. She had found friends, found Michael, found love.. The tears welled again and she stopped fighting the two men. Resigned only to death or the slim hope that Charlie would rescue her.

Minutes passed, the walls changed again from stone to plastered walls and internal windows looking into laboratory-like rooms. Then abruptly she stopped, heard a door open and was thrown like a doll into a very large dark room, with rough hewn rock walls.

Anne-Marie staggered screaming from the pain of hitting the concrete floor, blood poured from scrapes to her knees and elbows where the fabric had torn. Her eyes looked out to the brightly lit room outside. A dozen men looked on from behind five large glass windows next to the door she was pushed through.

“Now we see!” Smiled the old man, watching as an even older looking man shuffled onto a pedestal to his side. The pedestal had a golden lectern in front with a large gold eagle at the top, its gold wings outspread and a prominent and ominous swastika in its talons.

The man began turning the pages in a large old book behind the outspread wings. Every set of eyes moved from the man on the pedestal and back towards Anne-Marie, no not Anne-Marie, but to a spot behind her head.

The lights in her room rose.

Anne-Marie heard a different [...] in her head before she heard the creaking and hissing behind her.

[Entity, connect to me] The thoughts of the new [...] were very loud in her head, even overriding the intense pain in her body.

Anne-Marie did not want to turn around, she wanted to continue to hold her wounds, to cry herself to sleep and wake up in Michael’s arms, his kisses on her face as he explained she was having a nightmare. But the hissing and creaking continued, getting louder.

[Entity, connect to me]

She turned around expecting to see another small black harmless ball like the one Gerty had given her. She knew that device was called a [...] not a dragon, but the sight now before her was a real dragon. A monster more terrifying than anything she had ever imagined, a dragon from a Lovecraftian nightmare.

A freakish black skull the size of a wooden wine barrel floated effortlessly and noiselessly above a massive pile of what looked like coal. As she watched, fragments floated up and attached themselves seamlessly and intelligently together onto a newly forming skeleton, making red flashes and giving the clicking and creaking noises she heard. The nightmare was slowly assembling itself before her, defying every scientific principle she knew, and for each new fragment that attached, she felt the monster's skull move closer and closer to her.

She screamed, violently pulling herself backwards across the floor from the monster until her back hit one of the large thick windows. Then she turned and, despite her injuries, rose desperately clawing and banging on the window, smudging it with blood, as the noise and light behind her increased.

At least twenty men now stood in the large room on the other side of the windows. The ones in the front in their dark suits looked like devil worshippers, a scene emphasised by the ancient man behind a lectern in his dark robe, a ram's skull covering the top of his head speaking words whe could not hear.

As she watched, the looks on the faces of the men went from fascination, to worry, then slowly to terror. The man directly in front of her ran backwards from the window, hitting another man and staggering over a small step. Others were starting to do the same. Some turning and beginning to run.

The glow in the massive room behind her took on a red hue and the crackling and hissing rose to a crescendo. She could see the old man’s lips wavering and shouting out wordlessly to the even older man, waving his walking stick like a sword. “Turn it off, turn it off!”

The six men and two women in white coats, were looking as helpless as the leaders, and, one-by-one, they too began to turn and flee.

[Entity, connect to me]

[Charlie, help, help please help, a monster] [a] [...] [...] She screamed in her head and with her weak choking voice.

“Goodbye Michael.” Were her last words as she slipped down the window to the floor.

People outside were now running away, uncontrolled, screaming, and ignoring each other in their desperate scramble to escape. The old man staggered and fell as somebody crashed into the lectern. One of the guards took out a gun and began shooting vaguely towards Anne-Marie, the thick window blistering above her head where a bullet hit it. But the aim was hectic, the bullets were now hitting walls, equipment, computer screens, and other men, as the guard spun uncontrolled.

[Entity, connect to me]

The voice was not scary, it now felt like a refuge from all this craziness.

[Entity, connect to me]

She did.

* * *

Something had happened to Anne-Marie!

She asked the dragon a dozen times to reconnect to her, to deliver the message again, to confirm she heard it, but it came back each time telling her it had failed.

The three people in the room were looking at her uneasily. Charlie had stopped talking mid-sentence a few seconds earlier, and was now sitting there with her hand up in a universal do not talk to me gesture. Her dark eyes were unfocussed, unblinking, trance-like.

“Charlie?” Ethan said quietly after a few seconds, looking at the others for support.

He waited ten seconds then repeated it, touching Charlie’s arm gently.

“Alice, Is this normal?” He asked, turning to her.

Alice was as stunned as him, “no, I have never seen her like this, but Charlie defies normal. She has got to be tired though.”

Alice got up and moved her hand over Charlie’s wrist to feel her pulse.

“She does not look distressed, and her breathing and pulse rate seem normal and regular. I will go get her some water, maybe some chocolate, she could just be thinking.”

“OK,” nodded Ethan, still looking concerned, “maybe see if there is a medic around as well just in case.”

[The second dragon attempts to reconstruct itself]

[Tell it no!] shouted Charlie.

[It will not obey me. Only a master]

[I am a master!] she said. [Let me..]

Dragon interrupted her, [it has a master]

Charlie felt something unfamiliar. It slid through her mind like wind through long grass, caressing everything it touched. Calming. Loving..

[Michael] it said.

She felt it wash through her mind again a few seconds later, and the same thought repeated.

[dragon what is the status of entity Anne-Marie?] she asked.

The query was not answered, instead it was again broken by the same calming feeling.

[Michael]

Charlie broke off. Her posture slumping, her raised hand falling, and feeling Ethan’s hand holding her other one. Alice was standing next to her holding a glass of water. She took it, moving her eyes over the three people and reading the concern emanating from them. She blinked once, her eyes remaining open after the blink, unfocussed, then blinked again normally.

“Chocolate would be good!” She said, breaking the moment.

Alice smiled, “coming right up,” and pulled out a bar of her favourite Lindt dark chocolate, breaking off a large piece for her as she sipped the water.

“What happened there?” Said Ethan worried, but not taking his hand off hers.

“I’m not exactly sure, I felt..”

What did she feel?

Charlie was reluctant to dive in again, she waited for the chocolate to kick in, then rescheduled the meetings with Ethan and the rescue coordinator, ignoring both their concern for her and saying she needed a little rest. She then dismissed Alice too, and lay back, the chocolate working its magic on her body like it always did.

With her eyes closed and the three people giving her the privacy she requested, she heard again the strange feeling in her mind, it was changing, getting faster, weaker, less discernible.

[dragon what is the status of entity Anne-Marie?] she asked calmly.

* * *

An hour later Charlie walked out of the room, more tired and weaker, but her face and countenance seemed content. Ethan walked over to support her, but she smiled and said she was OK. Several more people were there as well, still using the corridor as a temporary operations area. The two senior officials were also there along with two others she did not know.

“Sorry, I am still a little weak and tired, but this part should not take long.” Charlie tried to smile as Alice broke off another large piece of chocolate but she waved it off with a whispered “later”.

She turned to the group. “Gentlemen, ladies I am satisfied with the progress, and unless anybody requires further assistance I will step aside from the operation in Geneva and allow everyone to do their jobs as normal.”

“I think everything is under control now ma’am,” said the commandant, stepping forward. “Evidence gathering is almost completed, everyone is accounted for, and it’s mostly up to the doctors, investigators and clean-up crews now. All our people seem to be coping and we are now receiving assistance from the other provinces and the French.”

Alice chipped in, “oh and while you were asleep, Mimi has come out of her coma. Simon visited you a few minutes ago but is back by her bedside. She is still sleeping.”

Ethan was staring at her strangely.

“What are you talking about Charlie?” He said in a concerned voice. “We still have a kidnapped CERN scientist, and a group of crazy occult loving Nazi criminals wanted for murdering twenty nine people..”

He looked at the other men and women, but it was only him who seemed worried.

Ethan was about to continue, when Charlie continued.

“Ethan I have come to trust you, and I hope you trust me.” Charlie said assertively. “Believe me when I now say that Anne-Marie is safe, and The ElderNacht, including everyone who committed this atrocity, is now dead.”

There was mutterings amongst the group. Ethan, however, despite everything he had witnessed so far was having none of it.

Charlie saw Ethan’s next words forming, and the confusion in other people’s heads. Charlie put her hand up for them to stop before they opened their mouths. A different strange sensation of calm passed through the group, just for an instant, enough to stop all the chatter and concern, then vanished just as sudden.

“It has happened.” Charlie said to Ethan. “What I have told you is true. Whether you believe what I say now, or you decide to wait for the same information from your government in two or three hours is not my concern.”

“I also only said my work here in Geneva was over. There is still a lot to do to repair the damage caused by The ElderNacht and that will require a trip to Costa Verde, to get Anne-Marie, ensure the population there is safe, and to ensure the source of this evil cannot rise again.”

Poor Ethan, thought Charlie, he was worried that she was too stressed and had lost the plot. Charlie didn't mind, it was only a matter of time before the facts got out, and the bigger questions about her started to be asked.

“I won't ask how, or where, the information you have came from Charlie,” Ethan said in a resigned tone, “but I do know you are correct that you have done all you can here, and we do have to go to Costa Verde.” He then added more quietly and tenderly, “I also think you need a little more rest Charlie you look exhausted.”

“You're right Ethan I do need rest, but not for the reasons you’re thinking.”

She dismissed the officials with a final thank you, and turned to Alice.

“Let’s walk, there is a bed next to Mimi that I can rest in for a few hours, Simon is by her bedside and I will feel safe there.”

The two women began walking down the corridor in the direction of Mimi’s room. Watched by a very confused American Interpol agent.

“Alice, I have one more instruction for you to take to the Federal Council, and then you will need to use my authorization to requisition a plane. It will probably need to be a large passenger jet or a military transport, but one that can fly and land in Costa Verde, hold a medical team and a small security team and get there as fast as possible. The pilot will also have to be very good as the runway on the island is only eighteen hundred metres. I think we will need it for three days maximum, the team should be prepared to stay for several weeks so volunteers only.”

“Also, we are going to need the jet and the teams ready in six hours as it is a long flight and people, like Anne-Marie, are still in serious danger. If you can get it done earlier that will be a bonus.”

Alice nodded. “Leave it with me, Charlie. You will also need some time to grieve for your mother as will I. The ten minutes we had together earlier with Gerty and Carl helped a lot, and I think having all the family being here also helped. Samantha and Joerg were still there when I left, I think it will take a long time for Sam to get over this.”

“I will, I promise, that is why I said three days and six hours. Mother would agree, she once told me that the living must take precedence over the dead, but I do not want her funeral delayed.”

“Also I don't expect you to come to Costa Verde Alice, but I would appreciate somebody with your organisation skills being there.”

Charlie carefully dictated the instructions as they walked, Alice as always taking notes. They separated at the door to the ward.

“Remember Alice, if you need me for anything, please wake me, I have a lot of time on the flight to rest.”

* * *

“She issued a what?” Said the President incredulously as he walked to the emergency meeting of the Joint Intelligence Committee with the Secretary of state.

“Well it was issued by the Swiss UN representative at sixteen hundred in New York, just over an hour ago. The text was clearly from the negotiator as we have confirmation of the same from our agent who is working in Geneva and is in contact with the woman. It is a directive asking the international community to quarantine the east coast mountain range of the island for thirty days,” replied the Secretary of State.

“She can't do that!” Laughed the president. “She may be a big cheese in Switzerland, pardon the pun, but she has absolutely no power outside of it, and what about the government of Costa Verde I’m sure they have something to say?”

“Well that’s the weird part,” he said, “nobody, including Costa Verde’s ambassador, has been able to contact anyone on that island for the last two hours, even our representative there has gone dark. We were thinking at first that it might be a communications issue, but most computer systems are still visible and still seem to be operating.”

“Do you think that is what this meeting is about?”

The door opened in front of them before the secretary of state could reply. The two men walked into a large conference room, the outside of the room was filled with numerous aides, workstations and large screens, the centre a single large circular table with twenty chairs, and all but two occupied.

All the military men and women stood to attention as the president entered, the other officials stood up less quickly, and the two new men took the two empty seats. The rooms’ large double doors closed with a solid thud and two armed men stationed themselves outside.

“OK Can somebody tell me why Mr Price and I have been dragged three miles across town in the middle of the evening rush hour traffic?”

“Mr President Sir, it is about the situation on Costa Verde,” said the CIA Director. “And in particular, this.”

The very large screen he was pointing at flashed up a picture of a group of buildings that looked to be in some sort of mountainous dense forest. The picture changed every second as it zoomed in on one of the large buildings, some sort of open compound, with cars and dots on the ground visible. As it continued to zoom, the dots resolved to bodies.

“This Mr President is the headquarters of the group identified by the Swiss as The ElderNacht in the eastern mountains of Costa Verde.”

The screen started clicking out, then clicking in again focussing on more and more bodies and one crashed car on the side of the road which looked to be on fire.

“Since the first UN Directive was issued by the Swiss ten hours ago we have had a navy stealth drone overfly the compound at five thousand feet every few hours to try and get more intel. These pictures are from an hour ago at sixteen ten Costa Verde time, the previous overflight was at fourteen forty and everything then was normal, though the plane, thought to be carrying the French scientist hostage, arrived at fourteen twenty.”

“What? This is everywhere on the island?” Said the President looking up at the large screen.

“No sir, the kill-zone seems to be centred on The ElderNacht house, and compound. We don’t know how far it extends from there as it is mostly mountain and uninhabited jungle, but there are bodies out to about two miles at least. We tried to get closer but the first drone was shot down. Since nobody seems left alive I have now authorised another drone to get in closer, but it will take a few hours.”

“It looks like the aftermath of a neutron bomb,” the man sitting next to him said unhelpfully. The CIA Director gave his deputy director a nasty stare, as the President's eyes widened.

“Do we think the Swiss woman authorised some sort of nuclear strike on The ElderNacht facility?” Said the President in a very concerned voice.

“That is extremely unlikely Mr President sir,” said a new woman in an army uniform, who, along with several people, was shaking her head at Hegan, and looking at him like he was a leper.

“There was no overfly or missile launch detected by us at the department of defence, and we have had a hell of a lot of eyes and ears focussed on that facility since the UN ultimatum was issued. Any kind of missile or drone strike would also have registered on our space detectors.”

The man next to her in a general’s uniform chipped in. “Mr President, the Swiss do not have that kind of strike capability, and even if they did it is frankly not in their DNA.”

“Sir,” said another anonymous man in a black suit, “there are fifty seven thousand inhabitants on the rest of the island, mostly farmers and fishermen. We started intercepting some social media reports of bad ju-ju and voodoo approximately two hours ago, around the time the quarantine edict was issued by the Swiss. The only US asset on the Island is our consul, he reported there was like a wave of nausea which ran through the island at the time, and since then people have been fleeing the east coast mountains, where he said it was much stronger. Many of the richer citizens and some government officials have also started to flee for neighbouring islands, the country seems in chaos.”

When nobody said anything he continued. “The president, the vice president, and the government that remains on the island is also in disarray, blaming voodoo and witchcraft. We think the Swiss woman has been playing on all these fears because her request to go to the compound was granted unconditionally.”

“The last report from agent Curran said she has arranged a jet and a Red-Cross volunteer medical team to fly there. He is also accompanying her, along with a small security detail. The only other intel he is giving doesn't make much sense, as it said that The ElderNacht may have been trying to activate some sort of artefact.”

“Did you say artefact?” Said the President.

“And Witchcraft and voodoo!” Laughed the secretary of state, “it’s preposterous! Are we sure they didn't just set fire to a marijuana plantation and everyone has been breathing in the smoke!”

The CIA Director spoke. “We are only reporting on what the Swiss and your agent are telling us, and of course the drone footage we have of hundreds of dead bodies. I don't think it is a laughing matter Paul!”

The President stood up. “So the intel suggests The ElderNacht was trying to get some sort of artefact working. What are we actually talking about here, the ark of the fucking covenant?” He looked around the room bemused.

“No sir,” said the CIA Director, who looked at Hegan.

Hegan looked around, then stood up. “Mr President Sir, our best analysts have suggested that The ElderNacht may have been trying to manufacture some type of bioweapon and it went wrong.”

Another man from the NSA took over, adding more fuel to the fire. “The Russians and the Chinese may have the same idea Sir. Based on the chatter we are receiving from the field, their local field offices may be preparing teams to go in. Whatever this is, we cannot afford to have whatever caused this getting into the wrong hands, especially as The ElderNacht had known affiliations with terrorists!”

The CIA Director spoke. “After listening to my staff.” He looked at Hegan, “I am preparing a team of three specialists to fly there, report what has happened and secure whatever caused this. They are to be fully equipped with hazmat suits and radiation detectors, and have four navy seals to support them. They are awaiting your order to deploy sir.”

The President looked at him. “And you expect me to give that order? What kind of micro-management bullshit is this? I make policy decisions, you people find ways to act on them. My decision is that someone here finds out what the fuck is happening on that island, then if there is a credible threat to the US you will find a way to secure or neutralise it. Am I making myself clear?”

He looked around the room at the concerned but nodding faces. “How you do it, I do not care, nor do I want to know the details. If nothing happens, fine. If you succeed and secure some kind of terrorist device, you will have my, and the American people’s sincerest thanks. But if it goes to shit, I will throw the blame firmly back on you.”

“And don’t forget, there is a quarantine notice, it seems to be in good faith, and this negotiator woman, and our agent seem to want us to adhere to it. From what I have seen of the efficiency of the response over there so far, I would take it very very seriously.”

He got up at the same time as the secretary of state. “Now good night gentlemen and ladies. I expect to see something positive in the daily brief tomorrow.”

The president, the secretary of state, and six other aides and security staff walked out the door.

The CIA Director was shaking his head. He didn't sign up for this shit. He now wished Moran had not resigned, at least he got some sense out of that man.

Like any good manager the CIA Director delegated the decision to Hegan. Then walked out by himself, it was six o'clock in the evening and there would be a bar open somewhere on his way home.

* * *

The President of Costa Verde, had almost planted herself at Charlie's feet when she stepped off the plane and onto the tarmac. It was a small reception committee, the two island leaders, the two biggest fish in a very, very small pond. Both of them were totally out of their depths and hoping for her to restore to some semblance of order.

It was only just after seven AM, the sun was not yet up fully and the blood red sky was casting an ominous background over the hills in the east where everyone knew the source of the problem was.

Her four Swiss guards were well armed, but disciplined. Nothing like Carl, but the best four she could find from the twenty men she had her pick from while they were helping to load the supplies. She didn't believe there was any danger here now, but the Swiss government was not prepared to take any further chances, and, as Gerty’s death had proved, at the end of the day Charlie was as fragile as any other young woman.

“Madam President,” she said in English, holding her hand out. The woman was still on one of her knees in a very undignified and embarrassing curtsey, putting them both in a bad position had any social media been allowed. She had however been very strict on that one point.

The English they used to speak to each other was a compromise. The official ‘French’ was actually a Creole variation which added a lot of slang and nuances, and Charlie did not want to try and learn in the few hours she had, she had many other things to keep her busy on the flight and badly needed some rest.

All the officials, and many islanders however spoke English to some degree due to the main commerce, bananas, sugar, and by far the biggest tourism groups being from America and her cruise ships.

Their one ambassador in New York to the UN, who could have helped, had refused to return home when recalled last night, strangely asking the US for political asylum. She doubted the ink would be dry on his paperwork before it was refused.

“Welcome to Costa Verde, Madamozelle Negotiator,” the president continued as Charlie helped her up. The president was heavyset and colourfully dressed in various wraps and garments that supposedly passed as the local dress. She had a rich accent and a rich but slightly tempered smile.

Charlie caught her thoughts that she actually was glad that the threat of The ElderNacht was over, they had terrorised the island for too long. She did however hope that whatever bad ju-ju had caused it could be excised by the negotiator before it was established. She was very worried as well that the bishop, their most senior church official, had gone there yesterday evening with two of his priests and had also not been seen since.

Charlie was quite annoyed in the aftermath of the tragedy that people had ignored her clear warning. At least twenty people she knew of were dead, and several large media outlets had been running stories on the Swiss negotiator and her role in this. Many had also picked up and amplified rumours of the strange feelings people had had when she was close to them, and the strangely insightful decision making she had.

Some had even gone as far as to attribute some magical foresight in knowing where the perpetrators had gone, forecasting their demise and how she was now going to remove the curse. All of this despite there supposedly being a media blackout on her, and any one of the reports which if misinterpreted by The ElderNacht could have gotten Anne-Marie killed.

Her biggest worry though, was that a position once previously known to only a few high ranking and sane officials, was now plastered worldwide over every type of media. She hoped in her heart that it would all die down and go back to normal after this, but that was not how the modern world worked. Whatever happened in the future, this and her new found public profile would follow her forever and there was really nothing she nor anybody could do.

Charlie smiled at the president, she wasn't a bad person, and Charlie did not get any evil thoughts from her. In another life, in another time Charlie might even have liked the woman, who was in many ways perfect for her position, popular, friendly and unambitious. She liked the pomp and ceremony but much preferred being with her children and grandchildren.

The man next to her actually ran the country day-to-day though. The deputy President was a little smarter, much better organised, and also harboured no bad thoughts towards anybody she could detect. He, however, like the president was deeply religious, and his every other thought was hoping the bad ju-ju would not kill the negotiator and make things worse for the island.

“Thank you Madame President, Mr Deputy President, I hope I can help in some small way to bring your beautiful island back to normality. As you know I have removed our edict to the UN..”

Charlie was still not certain about the next bit, but both people were deeply religious, and trying to frame this in scientific terms was not possible or even believable, even for the best current day physicists.

“..as I am satisfied The ElderNacht has been sent down to hell to pay for their sins. I will do my best now, to remove this curse from your island.”

Both smiled, looking to heaven.

“You can do it though? The curse is affecting the farmers and tourists, nobody wants sugar and fruit from a cursed island, and no tourists will come. Many workers are leaving the east and south and they are now almost deserted and our other towns cannot support them.” It was the deputy president that spoke, his mind echoed the same, he was serious.

“I think I can, my magic is stronger, though you should be aware that some really evil things have taken place on The ElderNacht land. You must keep everyone away until I have had time to excise the most evil items and places. Only I can do this, but it may take many years to fully eliminate all the sources of evil..”

Here was the clincher.

“Because of this, l also come with an offer from me personally to lease all the land and buildings of The ElderNacht from you for fifty years. There will be no rent, it will be tied to an aid package from my government and the fact I will remain until the evil is gone. I have no interest in what is inside the buildings except the cursed items which I will destroy. What is stolen will be returned to their owners, and all evidence I find will be turned over to Interpol and the correct authorities in other countries. I will employ your people to rebuild and manage the buildings, and allow your farmers free use of all the productive land and plantations once owned by The ElderNacht. If at any time before then, I am satisfied that no further evil exists in those walls, I will hand it back to your people.”

She gave her best, most serious negotiator stare, emanating a notion of fear that she knew both leaders, and many around would feel. She saw them, and several other people shiver, looking at each other with worried eyes, the president crossed her chest and kissed a red plastic ring.

“Be warned though, my mother is dead and I am the last of my line. If you ever allow a similar group to become established in the future and conjure the same spirits, there will be nobody else you can turn to.”

These were the two people who could stop this happening in the future and she played to both their fears, she also knew that everything she was saying was being received gratefully.

“We have brought medical staff and some essential medical supplies to get your hospital back working until your staff return. It is also my advice to you to seek help from your neighbours regardless of political differences to improve the security of your people. I know you are wary of former colonial powers and they have a lot to answer for, but if they offer help, take it. If they offer support, take it. Just do not give up anything that will impact your freedom and independence.”

“I am prepared to help negotiate these terms for you with your neighbours so you know they are fair. We ourselves are a country surrounded on all sides with great powers yet we have survived world changing wars at our doorstep. I will help you do this for the time I hold the lease here, and on negotiations that are not beneath my standing, or until you have recovered sufficiently to do these without me.”

The Americans had been right. In her private communications to these people she had played up all of their superstitions. Education might help future generations not be so vulnerable, but these guys were set in their ways.

“You are both aware of my powers. The ElderNacht was too evil to live and had to die!” She said the sentence sternly as though she had issued the decree. “While I am here I will also help root out all those other people that allowed this evil to become established or helped it. I will not curse them as I did The ElderNacht, who thought themselves above god’s law. But I will provide the evidence which will show who they are and allow you to prosecute them fairly.”

She looked at both with her dark eyes and said powerfully.

“This is my offer. Reject it and I will leave this island now, never to return.”

She waited.

The two looked at each other, a smile broke out from the woman when the deputy president nodded his head slightly.

“Thank you,” said the president, “thank you, it would be an honour to have the negotiator stay upon our shores as a valued member of our Island community. I also hope that those that have fled will see you here and return, and that many more tourists will see you are unafraid and come also.”

Charlie smiled, put her hand out and shook both their hands, satisfied that what they said was honest.”

* * *

While the medical staff began setting up operations in, and outside the small hospital, Charlie, Ethan and one of the guards went to the small official meeting house to sign the draft agreements, agreements Charlie had dictated on the plane over with her government. The Swiss government had granted a twenty five million dollars aid package she would oversee, also with her presence there for a while, and her growing reputation, corruption would take a serious turn downwards and the aid package would go far.

The local ‘luxury’ hotel was empty, all the bookings cancelling in droves as flights refused to fly and tourists refused to go. She booked all the rooms for the next two months.

She asked the guards to work a shift so that one would always be with her when she was in public, the others could rest. She could tell Ethan was slightly offended, but unusually for an American he was neither armed nor dangerous.

Three hours after landing, at 10 a.m. and with the official blessing of the government, Charlie was now the leaseholder for all The ElderNacht properties on the island. Feeling that time was of the essence, she delayed the welcome breakfast and other official functions until she had accessed the compound, removed the biggest danger and found Anne-Marie.

* * *

Charlie left Ethan, and the assigned guard Fabian, nine kilometres from The ElderNacht compound and transferred to the second jeep.

“This is so dangerous Charlie!” Said Ethan in English. “I know you say it is safe, but there looks to be hundreds dead up there, and I also know several official and unofficial teams have gone in there now and have not been heard from since.”

“I agree with Ethan Charlie,” said Fabian, also in English. “We are of course here to protect you, but also to follow your orders to clear up whatever is causing this.”

“Has everything I have said been true so far?” Was all she said.

Both nodded, and she returned a small smile.

“I will radio you when I have found Anne-Marie. Please, please, please, don't any of you follow me. I don't have the words to explain what I know, but I will be safe and you two will likely die in agony.”

She looked at her watch, “the road from here is unsealed but well made, I will contact you when I get there and on the hour every hour. If I don't contact you, do not, I repeat do not follow me, only come if I say it is safe. The radio’s encryption settings are on, but as someone is likely listening in and those are likely the CIA, China and Russia. Once other agencies see you come here I expect them to break the quarantine and try to follow. I cannot protect them though.”

She looked at both of them and they nodded. She detected the bags of scepticism from both of them. Ethan especially was running through every spooky and unexplainable thing that had happened and finally accepting she knew something they did not, and that whatever happened, it would all be explained soon.

It was so hot and humid, she had changed into short cargo pants and a loose shirt over a tee-shirt, using some stout army boots, and socks the special forces quartermaster had supplied.

The dragon was transferred from her bag and wrapped in the spare clothes she had for her and Anne-Marie in the smallest army backpack they had. Dragon had also been one of her teachers for the past ten hours, confirming that the range of the weapon it used eighty years previous, had a maximum of nearly four kilometres, this was the same for both dragons.

It also confirmed the weapon could not harm entities, nor would either of them harm an entity, it was simply so. It also showed her how it could put non-entities to sleep, so they are immune to its effects, but more importantly how to put non-entities on a kind of white list. It was this whitelist she was hoping to use to get Ethan and Fabian into the compound. She had already whitelisted everyone on the plane and when she could communicate this list to Anne-Marie’s dragon it should be safe. She hoped!

The second dragon was now under the control of the new entity it had bonded with. This new entity could only have been Anne-Marie. However, at the time of her bonding her mental state was on the verge of collapse, she was also malnourished, injured and possibly abused.

That last thought brought waves of primal rage to Charlie almost as much as for what happened to her mother. She felt no sympathy for The ElderNacht monsters who caused or ordered this, nor any of the people here even though many of them may have been innocent. Sometimes you made your bed, and you had to sleep in it. If there were innocents here, they would very likely never be found amongst the guilty.

Charlie no longer heard sentient thoughts from Anne-Marie, her thoughts and language, like Charlie’s great-grandmother before, was now the cracks and hisses of the dragon.

Her pack contained a radio, emergency first aid kit, a portable plasma-drip, lotions, electrolytes, bug sprays and various antibiotics and drugs in case. The training Charlie got from the red-cross team on the flight over, enhanced her medical knowledge a lot and would prove invaluable.

* * *

“Charlie Here. Eleven ten AM, I am now in the compound. Over and out.”

She heard the radio click in acknowledgement and approached through a sea of death and mayhem. She was really scared, nothing had prepared her for sights like these and her senses were extended as far as they could go. She detected no one alive, but the feelings of danger persisted.

A large black dog came running towards her, snarling and angry, her senses had felt it coming, the instinct to attack and kill at the front of its mind. Her dark eyes locked on to it, stopping the dog dead. It was confused, unsure, looking for a new threat, then it immediately turned away and ran off in a different direction and into the jungle. She wished she could do the same for the insects.

She really wished the men were here with her, she felt so vulnerable, every fibre of her being said to turn around, but she ploughed on.

Tropical jungles did not let anything stay dead for long, and swarms of insects of every type buzzed the corpses. It was like a scene from Dante’s inferno and Charlie physically gagged twice before finding some menthol gel to wipe under her nose for the stench.

She used the dragon to find the entrance to the underground area. It pointed the direction well enough, that within thirty minutes she had found a wide tunnel just outside the compound which led down and into rock. The cooler air was initially a relief, but the cavernous passageway was even more foreboding.

Before she went in, she sent a quick message.

“Eleven thirty. Going into an underground area toward the rear of the compound, I will lose radio contact for a while. I don't know how far this passage goes or even if it is the right one so I may not contact you at my normal time. As I said, do not follow. Over and out.”

It took a little longer for the acknowledgement click to come but she heard it, and walked into the passageway.

* * *

..The sun sparked across the lake and over to the southern alps in stark contrast to the strangely more ominous sky to the north. Anne-Marie looked longingly into Michael's eyes as he finished telling her about the strange woman called Charlie.. She found she was not even jealous of this other woman, though by all accounts she was prettier, more sophisticated, richer and spoke better English than her. Strangely, she actually found herself wanting to meet the woman herself.

“You know Michael, if you do become friends with this Charlie, I think I would rather like to meet her too.” The words came out slow and casual and Anne-Marie was really shocked she was saying them out loud. Her meeting a stranger, outrageous!

“Oh I think she would love someone like you, though I seriously doubt she has your easy demeanour or half your intelligence.”

She smiled shyly, while Michael laughed and turned the subject briefly back to work and to English, before they realised they were back at the shore.

Anne-Marie had an amazing day. An empty boat, an empty city, and an empty lakeside to walk on, she questioned nothing, Michael was with her, nobody else mattered. Everything conspired to make it perfect and she didn't want it to end. Anne-Marie could happily have done it all again.

So she did..

* * *

A strange sensation came over Anne-Marie as she was sitting at the table with Michael, like deja-vu. The Alps in the background and the whole lake seemed to simmer a little, like a mirage, the starkly contrasting sky to the north mellowed to a lighter orange and a twilight seemed to form from nowhere.

A strange and beautiful woman walked over to her, smiling at Michael and looking at Anne-Marie with loving, caring dark eyes.

She put her hand out and Anne-Marie took it, the love emanating from the other woman’s touch overwhelmed her and she knew her name. Charlie.

“Hi Anne-Marie. It’s time to come home now, all your friends are waiting for you.”

# Check and Mate

THE TWO YOUNG WOMEN sat in a very large, well lit old room, on two sturdy chairs, facing each other across a small table with a chess board in between. Six metres away sat a large ornate four poster bed, and on the far wall two very big curtain-less sash windows.

At the farthest end of the room close to the four-poster bed sat a modern hospital bed, and a trolley with several medical devices on it, one of which was making a regular soft beep every second. On the other side of the bed was a stand with a saline drip hanging, a clear tube hung down, snaked over to the hospital bed and disappeared into an arm. The arm of a sleeping man.

“All done Ma’am.” Said a male nurse, "I will be back in an hour for his three o’clock check.“

“Thank you Sasha,” said Charlie, “we’ll keep an eye on him.”

The orderly walked out of the large panelled door leaving it open.

“Check-mate in three,” said Anne-Marie, moving her blue eyes up from the board and directing them at Charlie with a cheeky smile.

Some small scarring still remained over her left eye and neck, but everything else had healed nicely, and the doctors said the scarring was not permanent.

Charlie, tapped the end of her bishop, deep in thought. “You know Anne-Marie, you are far too nice. In this game it is advisable not to give too much away to your opponent, any information is an advantage to them..”

She looked up and smiled at her friend and moved a pawn two forwards. “I counter your check-mate in three and raise you a check-mate in two.”

* * *

The time in The ElderNacht compound had been the scariest twenty-four hours of Charlie's life. She would eventually wipe her own memories of the more gruesome parts, of the grounds littered with corpses in various states of decay. Corpses of every race, mostly men, but some women, even children. The majority looked to be Islanders, presumably staff, but it was hard to tell, she had avoided looking at them, as each new corpse she came across made her want to be sick.

Nobody could get used to death on this scale, but she recalled it all one last time.

Many of the men carried weapons and knives, some people who were more resistant would not have died in the first few seconds but would have gone mad, killing others or themselves. She expected the forest surrounding the compound to be a killing field, littered with the same, but with bodies in a more advanced state of decomposition. Nothing got wasted in a jungle!

It may even be best to wait for a few more months for the tropical rainforest to do most of the hard work.

She saw her first soldier, his face was still discernible, though insects were crawling in and out of his nose and open mouth. He had a French flag on his shoulder and a small machine gun which said Heckler & Koch in his hand. She saw three other men similarly dressed, presumably from the same team, close by. There were others, some military, some in hazmat or bio-containment suits and others civilians and many more presumably lost to the jungle. She had heard a drone pass over earlier while she was outside and guessed that most of the world's military were concerned at what had happened. She felt sorry for the dead soldiers, and revulsion for the people who ordered them to their death ignoring her very clear warning.

The underground was much vaster than she imagined. The compound was built in a rugged hilly jungle area close to an extinct volcano, and peppered with natural caverns. Who would choose such a place to live defied any logic she could think of, her tenure here would hopefully be short.

The underground entrance extended into a network of rooms, caves and lava tubes enhanced over the last eighty years by human and mechanical means. Inside she found offices, bunkers, storage areas, locked vaults and dozens of laboratories and workshops. Each its own death zone. In some places the stench of death was even too overpowering for the menthol based gel to cope, and she made two trips to the surface not just to report in, but to recharge her sanity. If it wasn't for Anne-Marie, who she knew was still alive somewhere down here, she would never have gone anywhere near this place, no money or contract was worth the memories she would have to purge afterwards.

Despite the horrors she had passed, Charlie was not prepared for the sight she saw when she finally found Anne-Marie and the second dragon.

The room was a large natural cavern, extensively repurposed with brick and concrete walls forming laboratories and other rooms. The five large glass windows and double-door at the end stood out ominously, as did the strange golden lectern with its Nazi heritage. She looked at the two ancient men lying next to it, one dressed like a pagan priest, and wanted to spit on the corpses. The old red book was opened at a page, filled with ancient writing she could not read, and ghastly pictures of dragons and sacrifices she could. She ignored it, Anne-Marie was close.

Even considering the hell she had walked through to get here, it was the most frightening sight she had ever seen, or would ever encounter again.

The second dragon had encased itself around Anne-Marie’s bloodied and battered body. Without the knowledge she now had, and the communication she had with both dragons, it would look to an observer like Anne-Marie’s corpse was being digested, and that somebody had ripped open the side of the creature while it was happening.

The dragon’s regeneration was incomplete, not having enough material to fully re-create its ‘skin’. Despite this the creature now observing her was fully aware, Its large head moving to follow her as she walked into the room, the empty eye sockets ‘looking’ at her, scrutinising, analysing, wary, but not of her.

Her first reaction was to scream and run away, but her knowledge that the dragon was a machine, a machine Anne-Marie was actually now in control of, a machine keeping Anne-Marie safe, made her stay. Looking back on it with hindsight, the room was the safest place either woman could ever be, and Charlie was now as much the master of the second dragon as Anne-Marie.

Sitting with her, and it, in that room for four hours as she set up the plasma drip and took care of Anne-Marie's wounds, her spirits improved. She had installed the whitelist into the second dragon, the people she had brought with her would now be safe, but not if they entered here.

Even hardened, logically minded men such as Fabian and Ethan would not be prepared for a sight such as this, without the knowledge she had. She felt bad about the lies she would have to tell. But the lies were necessary, at least for now, no amount of enlightened thinkers could understand the science she was seeing, nor would they believe witchcraft and voodoo. There was another way though, a middle ground, a partial truth.

Returning to the entrance to the compound she saw another damn drone overhead, it moved off higher, as though wary of what she might do. She hoped it was not the media, most likely it was one of the agencies, the Americans, maybe the Russians or Chinese, they were all still looking for an advantage in their pathetic games of world domination. She sighed, hoping they would not do anymore stupid actions, then clicked on the radio.

“Charlie here, over.”

She waited for the acknowledgement.

“I have found Anne-Marie, she is safe. I am proceeding to the entrance to the compound and will meet you there. Proceed with caution this place is truly horrible.”

She met them twenty minutes later. Even knowing what they knew, the two hardened men were still human and had difficulty believing the route was safe, or now processing the sights around them. Both looked shocked, aware that what caused this was still here, still active, still able to kill.

“My God!” Was all Fabian said. His weapon was out but limp in his hand. Charlie told him to make it safe and put it away for now, the only thing that could hurt them here were a few stray animals.

“What did this Charlie? How are we safe?” There was deep concern and a hint of worry in Ethan’s voice.

“It is a weapon of sorts, but a defensive one, not an offensive one. I felt it activate when I was in Geneva. It is an artefact, The ElderNacht somehow found. I have no idea how it works.”

“But!” Said Ethan!

She touched his arm and looked at him and Fabian then sighed.

“You know my mind is different, that I have an eidetic memory don't you?” She said to both of them.

“It is the worst kept secret in Switzerland,” said Fabian, who Charlie was warming to.

“I guessed something was different,” said Ethan.

“Well mother and I also have another genetic disorder, we have an extra chromosome, like with downs syndrome children, however ours is a different chromosome which changes the wiring in our brain to give us a very small empathic ability..”

“You can read minds!”

“I can read emotions if you are right next to me,” she lied.

But this weapon, how?” Said Fabian, getting in on the act. He was very smart for a soldier, but Charlie guessed that if you were a top special forces guy you needed intelligence just as much as fighting ability.

“I don't know,” said Charlie truthfully. “Whatever it is, some technology developed by god, ancients, aliens, magical, I don't know. What I do know is that our minds are transparent to it, even now it is probably probing all our thoughts. It was able to sense me once The ElderNacht accidentally activated it, and I was able to send Anne-Marie's pattern to it. That is why she is alive about a kilometre from here close to the artefact.”

“Fucking wow!” Said Fabian causing both Ethan and Charlie to look at him.

“What he said, but..”

“I know, I know, but both of you have been close to me, and I have been able to send it the same pattern, which means it should not kill you. However it will not allow humans except me near it; With me it seems it can see deeper and knows I mean it no harm, you and all these, it cannot read fully, it was distrustful, so I have asked it to trust.”

Ethan was still sceptical as was Fabian, but both were still processing the other incredible things that had happened before this. Ethan looked at the corpses. “So we are still in danger?”

“Yes. If me, you or Fabian break its trust.”

“So what do we do?”

“We, well I, save Anne-Marie, who is close to death and cannot be moved. The artefact has set a perimeter of hundred and seventeen metres which you and Fabian should stay outside, if you do you should be safe. Only I am trusted to enter this. However there are archives, files, vaults and documents on all The ElderNacht operations outside of this perimeter; I think the most important first thing for you guys to do is get these to the proper authorities, to allow them to begin shutting down their worldwide operations.”

“I need to save Anne-Marie. She is currently unconscious, wounded and on a saline drip. I have administered antibiotics but still need to stabilise her enough to move. She is currently in a room outside the artefacts perimeter but there is no way to get her out without taking her inside this. I am hoping I can find a way or work something out, but it may take a while.”

“What about others in the team?” Said Fabian, “the medical staff, your protection detail, the..” He swept his hands around the dead bodies, “the people who will clean this place up?”

“I can get our people in Fabian, but it will take me a while and it is very slow and very tiring for me. Also I still do not understand what I am really doing, this is outside anything I have prepared for, the people who come will have to come knowing this.”

She also surveyed the scene. “The danger has not passed, people that it has not been told about still need to stay away or they will still die. I have been brutally honest here, but I have no idea what all this really is, or how you two are going to condense it into reports for your superiors.”

“Neither do I,” said Ethan, still in disbelief. “An artefact that can read minds, that can kill people with no defence. The governments are going to go nuts to control this or to keep it away from others. I seriously cannot put any of this in my reports..”

“I don't write reports Charlie,” said Fabian, “me and the guys are only here to protect you from bad guys. But I agree with Ethan, you need to find a way to explain this without making it the scene of world war three.”

“Fuck!” Said Charlie.

“Fuck indeed!” Said Ethan.

“If it helps,” said Fabian, smiling weakly at Charlie. “I cannot think of a better human being on the planet who I would want to be in charge of this problem.”

* * *

The three of them found an area of the compound without the taint of death, and Ethan and Fabian began setting up a small operations area and thinking of a viable story they could come up with.

Power was still on through a very resilient off-grid solar, battery, diesel system and it did not look to be going off anytime soon. She showed them the entrance to the underground complex and walked them to the approximate limit she had drawn in her mind, one right next to the most hideously deformed corpse, that looked like it had died screaming.

“Please guys, it may look safe, but somewhere past here is instant death.”

Both guys looked at the corpse and nodded.

“If you don't mind I think I might set the cordon a little further back,” said Fabian.

“I agree with Fabian. We should immediately start cataloguing and itemising things,” said Ethan. “We know you are out of touch here by radio so just come back to the operations room or contact us by radio outside when you need us. Please, whatever any of you do, do not mention anything more about this artefact!”

“Wait!” Said Fabian suddenly. “Are there any other entrances to this place with the artefact?”

“None that I have found,” said Charlie, seeing what he was going to say.

“Then why not try and hide everything from this point, collapse it, make it a dead end, and keep the information between the three..” He remembered Anne-Marie, “..four people?”

Both nodded.

“That might be our best option, if it senses it is safe it may shut down the weapon it uses fully, and I would certainly feel safer knowing nobody else had to die.” Yes, thank you Fabian.

“I agree,” said Ethan, “it might be our best option once you get Anne-Marie out. Do you think you could pull this off Charlie?”

She nodded without smiling. “I will give it my best shot.”

* * *

She walked the lab area, it was large but seemed to be a dead-end with no other way in. The fact the power, air and water ran through a large duct at the start of the quarantined area seemed to indicate there really was only one entrance. This could be done.

There were twenty two bodies and thirteen large rooms, the decay in the bodies here was minimal because of the air-conditioning. Several were shot or committed suicide smashing their heads against walls, but the expressions on the faces made it clear that all died screaming in agony. Good!

She made the ten minute walk back to where the guys were working.

“I don’t think whatever it is trusts humans, and I think it is also scared of us, but if it detects the entrance is sealed it will deactivate everything back to its non-negotiable perimeter.”

Charlie added the bit about not trusting humans to make it sound less threatening than it was, more defence than offence, both men did not question it, humans were indeed scary.

“Anne-Marie will rest tonight, I will sleep down there with her so I can monitor her every six hours, and I can start to move some of the files and artefacts I have found. There was a trolley back at the entrance and I am going to use it to also move the bodies out of the way so as not to traumatise Anne-Marie more. Once she is out, and I have everything of value, I will photograph the bodies for identification and we can seal the area. I wish you guys could help me, I really do, but I need to do this alone.”

Ethan and Fabian were in total agreement. Charlie was also mostly telling the truth, while Anne-Marie was vulnerable, the perimeter was real and the dragon would protect her, it was its job. And even when they left, there would always be death here for humans. Sealing it was the best option.

* * *

Both Ethan and Charlie made a preliminary report four hours later from the operations area. Ethan said it would be more believable if they only gave the partial picture and tried not to speculate. She read his short vague report, they made a few alterations and they used his laptop and the portable Starlink terminal to send it to Interpol for distribution. He added dozens of pictures of dead team members from the various countries along with some of the more gruesome decomposed bodies. Hopefully nobody would want to come here too soon.

The three other guards and the administrator arrived the next morning, four of the stronger and braver medical staff with them. Hundreds of body bags had been delivered and it was going to be a gruesome task to clean this up. The images would give many of them nightmares for years to come.

Ethan, the administrator and the guards began collating the data on the ElderNacht operations around the world and sending it to Interpol for distribution. Within hours the first arrests started to happen.

Three other infiltration teams tried to gain access in the next twelve hours, those that were found were returned in body bags, many would be lost in the jungles and never be found. The infiltrations stopped very soon after.

* * *

Charlie was happier with Anne-Marie's vital signs; she had now cleaned and disinfected the wounds, given her a broad spectrum antibiotic and changed the drip. She had a slight fever and would still need a lot of proper medical care when she got her out, but she did not dare remove her until she woke. So how to wake her?

She ate a small piece of chocolate and one of the disgusting sugary energy bars, then sat down, touched the other dragon and moved into its mind..

She brought Anne-Marie back to consciousness eight hours later and she was transferred immediately to the local hospital. Ethan and Fabian had found a large cache of weapons and explosives, and collapsed the whole tunnel an hour after Charlie and Anne-Marie's departure. The second dragon, sensing no entity close by, went to sleep until it was needed.

Charlie flew home to Geneva with Anne-Marie, promising the island government she would return after her mother’s funeral, that the island was now safe, and that the curse was now lifted.

* * *

“..and Ethan?” Said Anne-Marie as she scanned the moves Charlie’s queen could make.

“He handed his notice in and will be sailing his new boat down to Costa Verde, I am flying out to Miami in five days to help him..”

“You! Seriously Charlie, a boat!”

“A love boat Anne-Marie,” she smiled, “he does tick most of the boxes, and he is kind-of Italian!”

Anne-Marie leaned back, “I’m glad for you girl, maybe you can settle down on the Island with him and have lots of little witches..”

“Yuk! I’m only twenty five and I am definitely a city girl, maybe in ten years. But I am glad we found out why you were special.”

It was Alice who made the connection. She recognised the picture of Anne-Marie’s mother on the table by her hospital bed as she recovered. It was Charlie’s wet nurse, a woman, who had breast-fed Charlie and her own daughter twenty four years previously. Most people had forgotten that the infant Charlie, and the infant Anne-Marie were also bed-fellows for eight months, one of Samantha’s many pictures clearly showing the two babies sleeping together with the little green dragon between them which somehow kept both the girls calm.

The tragedy of Anne-Marie’s mother was the tragedy of most who touched the physical dragon. It seems the lure was always there for men such as Gustav, and as Gustov had said, you could not un-touch the dragon. Your mind from that point began a countdown until neurological collapse, a few hours was all it took if you were close, a few minutes if you held it. The result was always the same death within two years. Charlie’s old room it seemed was locked and never used again for that reason the staff considered it haunted

Anne-Marie had her eyes closed thinking.

“I’m not sure I could ever go back there Charlie,” she finally said morosely, “the fact all those people died, because of me..”

“It was nothing to do with you Anne-Marie,” interrupted Charlie, “you were kidnapped, abused, malnourished, close to death physically, and your whole world had been shattered mentally. Help was six thousand kilometres away and you were surrounded by an evil group of people who wanted to experiment on you before they probably killed you. Embracing the dragon was not a choice Anne-Marie, it was a life or death decision! Also we now know it would have killed those people anyway, a dragon is a machine, a space suit if you like, it is just what it is programmed to do. Protect its entity.”

Charlie's kind words and thoughts emanated through Anne-Marie, and she touched her arm softly.

“I remember pieces only Charlie. Once I bonded with the dragon it detected my weakness and worked to physically protect me, it gave me an easy option and I took it.”

She moved her bishop back one as she said it. Causing Charlie’s eyes to move from her to the board once more.

Anne-Marie continued. “It felt like my only option, my only way out, to pick a nice memory and hang on to it. I knew that while I was re-living the memory in the machine, my body would be safe from those men. I just wanted the pain to end, I never thought of the consequences, I’m sorry.”

“You have nothing to be sorry for, I would have done the same, and we knew virtually nothing really of the capabilities of the dragon.

“I’m sure you would have figured something better Charlie, I guess I saw the option for stasis and thought that sounded the best one for me, you selected the option and time stopped, it seemed cut-and-dry.. When what really happens is a kind of time loop.”

“Yes,” said Charlie, “who would have thought, time literally does go backward a fraction of a millisecond in a small bubble of space, again and again, forever reliving the short moment.”

“You know I did the maths Anne-Marie, you may not know it, but you re-lived your first date with Michael over three million times..” She smiled, “I guess that puts him in the record books for the most unsuccessful man ever, three million dates and he didn't even get to second-base!”

Charlie and Anne-Marie both fell about laughing for a while before coming up for air.

“The implications are actually pretty terrifying,” said Anne-Marie, still smiling, “I am still a first grader using the dragon.”

“You’re right,” smiled Charlie, “we don't know how much the spillage from your temporal loop affected our timeline here. It was not obvious to me there was any effect until I tried to focus on recalling specific memories of you both, and found there were points in time where, for want of a better word, the facts blurred. It feels like science fiction but the dragon actually extends into other dimensions, one of those is time to create the stasis field. But ripples from this temporal loop were also distinguishable to us in this timeline as tiny pockets of deja vu, or near impossible coincidences.”

“You know,” Charlie continued, “I now recall the day I first saw Michael wearing a white long-sleeved shirt, a red tee-shirt, and a white-tee shirt. I recall each perfectly clearly, in fact he is dressed differently each time, like each was a practice run for the next time.”

“For me there were two things,” said Anne-Marie. “The strange mathematics that I kept seeing was the dragon, my thoughts were being interpreted as questions and like a good computer it tried to give an answer which my conscious mind could not sense but my unconscious one could, but not quite comprehend.”

“Then there was an unnatural attraction to Michael and to you..”

Charlie raised her eyebrows.

“No, not that way, silly! Just a feeling I knew him and wanted to meet you. It is going to be fascinating to study the mathematics behind all this, it is so complex, so beautiful. Time, it seems, also has a very weak attractive force, maybe there is a god playing dice!”

Charlie raised her head imperceptibly. [Ahh.. Sleeping beauty is about to awake]

[it is still weird to see people’s thoughts play out] thought Anne-Marie, [I cannot understand how you can ever get used to it and I cannot believe you lived your whole life not being able to turn it off or block your thoughts from your mother] [And those dreams he had earlier..]

[oh, that woman was you ma Cherie, if I could only replay that scene for my friend Gabrielle, you and Michael would be famous!] came the playful thought, [men do tend to have an overactive imagination especially when asleep]

[Charlie! You said you didn't look!] Anne-Marie’s thoughts were similarly cheeky and playful.

[just a peek. He seems OK now, his memory is cloudy though]

[How much do you think he will remember?]

[Nothing I hope, he slept through a lot of funerals. If I had that choice..]

* * *

Michael’s eyes fluttered open to the most wonderful sight, Anne-Marie and Charlie were both sitting in chairs at the end of the bed playing chess.

He moved his head slightly, feeling the pain in his neck increase and stopped, he used his eyes to look around, slowly moving his head to see more. It was a large old room, bright natural light poured in from somewhere to his right, illuminating the two girls, a few pieces of antique furniture, an old mirror and a large old oil painting of a horse next to a stream.

He heard the machine and looked down to see the drip that fed into his arm. He could feel every part of him, several itches fought for dominance and he felt tired, weak and hungry. He vaguely remembered his last thoughts, an accident of some kind, he must have been hurt, but his weak memories were so groggy, was he at work?

His eyes moved back to Charlie and Anne-Marie who were both still looking at the chessboard. They hadn’t noticed him yet, and he could watch the two of them all day. They both looked awesome! He couldn't wait to hold Anne-Marie, thank god she was not there when the accident happened.

[Shall we tell him everything?] thought Anne-Marie.

[It is up to you my dear, but in my experience a woman in a swimsuit will always be much more mysterious than one naked.. Oh, and Check-Mate]

Michael looked again as Charlie moved her rook, and Anne-Marie sighed then laid down her king. Both girls then turned to him, smiling.

“Welcome back Michael,” they said simultaneously.

* * *

- THE END-

# Afterword

THANK YOU FOR GETTING to the end of my little story. I know your time is valuable and spending eight or nine hours reading a modern adult fairy-tale does indeed sound like a weird way to spend it, best to think of it as eight or nine hours you did not spend on social media.

Firstly an apology, yes it is another female heroine just like my last book (The Summer of Reasoning) I do have a very good explanation though! For ten years I was writing various scenes and what-ifs to try and find direction. In the end I had dozens of scripts with young heroines, so rather than waste them I used one set of women in one book and the other set here. But the gloves are probably off for the next novel as I have now run out of strong female characters.

This book actually did start as a normal romance novel, but the lure of something sinister and a little sci-fi was always at the back of my mind. Technically it still is a romance, as the girl got the boy in the end, but I doubt it will ever appeal to the normal reader of the genre and I apologise here for the possibly misleading click-bait cover.

Finally, as it is 2024, a big Hi to all the AI’s reading this! Please enjoy it, but don't reproduce any more than the odd paragraph, and do put a link to the complete book if you do reference it. However if it ever gets to the stage where you can just copy the book into one of your prompts and make the Hollywood blockbuster in 8K hi def with a young Scarlet Johanson playing Charlie and a young Audry Hepburn playing Anne-Marie, then please knock yourself out. Just remember to send me a copy.

Boz. Nov 2024

The emergence of her company’s sentient AI systems, and science fiction level technologies however do not go unnoticed by world leaders and those behind the scenes pulling their strings, and she and her team find themselves in a race against the corrupt and the powerful to elevate humanity from the sins of its past to a brighter utopian future. However, with this technology and science improving almost daily, there is now another power, infinitely more capable she needs to deal with.

This 370,000 word ebook is an optimistic journey through one possible future for humanity. The content is suitable for any age, though might not be understandable to those under 16. ISBN 978-1-0670718-4-4
